# Promptise Foundry Changelog

All notable changes to Promptise Foundry are documented here.

---

## v1.11.0 — 2026-10-27

Fixes from the full guide review on 1.10.1 and from building the demo system on `promptise run`: keyless Azure OpenAI, a function as approval-classifier fallback, the input `BLOCK` warning where it applies, whole lines from `promptise audit verify`, the TUI's MCP tab for runtime agents, and current model examples.

### Changed

- **The warning for PII or credential `BLOCK` without `redact_input=True` now comes when input has such a finding, once per scanner** -- 1.10.1 logged it when the scanner was created, so a scanner used only for output or tool results, where `BLOCK` works, warned for nothing. It now warns the first time a message has a PII or credential finding that is only logged on input.

### Fixed

- **Azure OpenAI with Entra ID (keyless) failed with "AZURE_OPENAI_API_KEY is not set"** -- the keyless setup the docs show, `Model(..., provider="azure", extra={"azure_ad_token_provider": ...})`, was refused unless an API key was also set, although the request only uses the token. A token provider or token in `extra` now counts as the credential.
- **`AutoApprovalClassifier(fallback=<function>)` failed at the first escalation** -- a bare function was accepted when the classifier was built, then the first request that reached the fallback raised `AttributeError: 'function' object has no attribute 'request_approval'`. A function (plain or async) is now wrapped as `ApprovalPolicy(handler=...)` wraps one, and anything else is refused with a `TypeError` when the classifier is built.
- **`promptise audit verify` wrapped its output at 80 columns when piped** -- a script reading the output got `last_hash <hash> (seq` and `N)` on separate lines, which broke extracting the hash for `--anchor`. Lines are no longer broken to fit the width.
- **The `promptise run --tui` MCP tab was empty for runtime agents** -- an `AgentProcess` connects its MCP servers itself and hands the agent ready-made tools, so the agent has no MCP client of its own and the tab listed no servers, and each tool showed no server. The servers and each tool's server now come from the MCP clients behind the agent's tools.

### Docs

- Model string examples in the docs, the README and `promptise models env` use current models: `anthropic:claude-sonnet-5-5` and `gemini:gemini-3.8-flash` instead of retired or restricted ones. Bedrock, Vertex and OpenRouter examples are unchanged.
- The README quickstart runs after a plain `pip install promptise`.

## v1.10.1 — 2026-10-24

### Security

- **With `scan_tool_results=True`, `trace_tools` and an `observer` showed the raw tool result** -- the model got the redacted result, but MCP tools and wrapped native tools reported the result from inside the tool, before the scan, so the console line and the trace's `result_preview` held the PII or secret the scan had removed. They now show the result the model gets: the redacted text, or the withheld notice. `observe=True` traces were not affected.

### Fixed

- **An MCP server approval handler that raised an error gave the client `INTERNAL_ERROR`** -- the call was refused, but with no approval reason and no request id. `ApprovalGateMiddleware` now denies with `APPROVAL_DENIED`, "approval handler error: <type>" in the message and `approval_request_id` in the details, like the agent's gate; the error itself goes to the server log. A handler that raises an `MCPError` (such as `ApprovalDeniedError`) still has it sent as is.
- **A sandbox container that failed to start was left behind** -- `DockerBackend.create_container` created the container, and when `start()` failed (for example an image without `/bin/bash`) it raised without removing it, so `docker ps -a` kept a `Created` container for every failed session. It is now removed before the error is raised.
- **Breaking out of `astream()` or `astream_with_tools()` logged "Task exception was never retrieved: ValueError: <Token ...> was created in a different Context"** -- a consumer that leaves `async for` with `break` leaves the stream to the event loop, which closes it in another task, where resetting the run's context variables failed. The stream now cleans up there quietly. Wrapping the stream in `contextlib.aclosing()` still closes it at once.
- **A scanner set to `BLOCK` PII or credentials without `redact_input=True` now says it doesn't block input** -- on input those findings are warnings unless `redact_input=True`, so `CredentialDetector(action=Action.BLOCK)` alone never refused a message. `PromptiseSecurityScanner` now logs a warning when it is created with such a setting. Behaviour is unchanged.
- **The `promptise run` event bus could count one failing subscriber twice** -- a subscriber that raised in two publishing threads at the same time was dropped once but added to `EventBus.dropped_subscribers` by both, and the count was updated outside the bus lock. Only the thread that removes it counts it now.

### Docs

- The event payload table lists `streaming` for `invocation.timeout`.

---

## v1.10.0 — 2026-10-24

Security fixes for custom MCP auth providers, approval gates and inbox scanning, a deadline for streamed runs, and fixes found while checking every guide against 1.9.0.

### Upgrade notes

- **A `.env` is looked for only up to the project root**: the nearest folder with `promptise.toml`, `pyproject.toml` or `.git`, or the working directory when there is none. A project nested in another repository no longer loads that repository's `.env`; list a shared file on purpose with `[env] files = ["../.env"]`.
- **`astream()` and `astream_with_tools()` can raise `TimeoutError`** on an agent with `max_invocation_time`. Before, streams had no deadline. For `astream_with_tools()` this is a raised exception, not an `ErrorEvent`.
- **A `TimeoutError` raised inside a run is reported as itself.** `ainvoke()` (and `chat()`, `invoke()`) gave "Agent invocation exceeded Ns timeout" for a `TimeoutError` from an input guardrail, memory or another step outside the graph; code that caught that message for such failures now gets the original error.
- **A `ValidateNode` (or another reasoning node) that ends the run leaves the answer as `messages[-1]`.** Its JSON verdict was appended after the answer it checked; the verdict is in `state.context[output_key]` (`"validation"` for a `ValidateNode`).
- **Servers with a custom auth provider and `require_auth=True` warn at startup** on HTTP and SSE when the provider has no `averify_token()`: bad credentials are then refused per call, not with a 401 at connect. Add `averify_token()` to refuse them at the transport.

### Security

- **A custom MCP auth provider that refused by returning a value let the call through** -- `AuthMiddleware` used whatever `authenticate()` returned, so `None`, `False` or `""` reached the tool as the client ID. It now refuses `None`, any bool and empty strings with `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR` and logs a warning naming the provider.
- **The MCP approval gate let a call through when the handler raised `TimeoutError`** under `on_timeout="allow"` -- on Python 3.11+ `asyncio.TimeoutError` is `TimeoutError`, so a handler's own error looked like the gate's timeout. Approval gates (agent and MCP server) and `Elicitor.ask` now tell the two apart: a handler error fails closed ("Approval handler error: TimeoutError") and is not a reviewer denial for `max_retries_after_deny`.
- **Inbox messages ignored `redact_input=True`** -- the scanner produced redacted text, but the inbox stored the raw message. Inbox messages now follow the scanner's input policy: with `redact_input=True` a credential or PII is stored redacted, a BLOCK finding still refuses the message, and with the default a message carrying a credential is accepted and logs one warning with the finding categories, never the value.
- **A long credential across a scan-window boundary was missed** -- inbox messages and trigger payloads were scanned in 500-character windows that overlapped by 50, so a long API key split across two windows was neither redacted nor blocked. Both are now scanned whole; scanners that don't window their models themselves still get the 500-character windows as well.

### Fixed

- **`AuditMiddleware(include_args=True)` wrote `"args": {}` for a call that failed argument validation** -- it now records the arguments the caller sent, with `status: "error"` and `VALIDATION_ERROR`. Long strings and large lists in rejected input are clipped and marked. `StructuredLoggingMiddleware(include_args=True)` does the same, after its usual redaction.
- **An approval-denied call's `tool.call` trace entry had no status** -- it now carries `status="denied"`, `denied_by="approval"` and the `reason`, like its `tool.result` and like a budget-refused call.
- **`max_invocation_time` did not apply to `astream()` and `astream_with_tools()`** -- the deadline now covers the whole stream, the time the consumer spends between events included. When it passes, the stream stops (its cleanup still runs), `TimeoutError("Agent invocation exceeded Ns timeout")` is raised and `invocation.timeout` is emitted with `streaming: true`. Stopping a stream early is not a timeout, and now runs the stream's cleanup at once.
- **"Future exception was never retrieved: InboxQuestionRemoved"** -- clearing the inbox, or a question expiring, being evicted or removed by a later scan, no longer logs it when the `/ask` waiting for the question had already timed out.
- **The first sandbox session hung silently for minutes while Docker pulled the image** -- the pull was logged at INFO, which default logging hides, and it blocked the event loop. It now runs in a worker thread and logs a warning when it starts, a progress line every 10 seconds and when it ends; a failed pull raises with Docker's error, and a daemon error is no longer taken for a missing image.
- **Memory search reported a provider's own `TimeoutError` as the search timeout** -- it is now logged as a failed search with the provider's error; only `memory_timeout` passing gives "timed out after Ns".

### Docs

- The event reference lists every process event (`process.restart_exhausted`, `process.suspended`, `process.resumed`, `process.rewound`) with its payload, and `invocation.timeout` with `timeout_seconds` and `streaming`.
- `astream()` and `astream_with_tools()` record tool failures and inject lessons, like `ainvoke()`; the adaptive strategy page said they didn't.
- `ToolEndEvent.reused` is also set for repeats deduplicated by `ToolNode(deduplicate=True)`.
- The MCP server auth page documents the custom provider contract (raise `AuthenticationError` to refuse) and refusing at the transport with `averify_token()`.
- Context compaction: set `max_result_chars` when tools return whole files, since each re-read adds a full copy.
- `CredentialDetector` has 97 patterns (some posts said 96); table cells that lost their dash show `none` or their value; links to renamed guides use the current titles.

---

## v1.9.0 — 2026-10-23

Runtime processes can pick their reasoning pattern. Traces keep the tenant and the session, record tool arguments as data and show refused calls and approval decisions. Plus fixes for `promptise run --check` with agent factories and a `Prompt` that reached the model twice.

### Upgrade notes

- **A `tool.call` entry's `metadata["arguments"]` is JSON data, not text.** It was the Python repr of the call (`"{'sku': 'MS-30'}"`); it is now a dict for a structured call (`{"sku": "MS-30"}`), with nested models as objects, and stays text only for a tool given plain text. Code that parsed the string should read the dict. Long strings are still cut at 2,000 characters, now per value. The OTLP span attribute `promptise.arguments` is now JSON text.
- **`collector.purge_user("alice")` and `for_user("alice")` no longer reach a tenant's `alice`.** Without a tenant they match only entries recorded without one, as `MemoryProvider.purge_user` does. Pass `tenant_id=` or the isolation key (`"acme::alice"`) for a tenant's user; `purge_user` logs a warning when it kept entries of a tenant's user with that id. The signatures are unchanged apart from the new keyword.
- **`collector.get_users()` lists isolation keys**: `"acme::alice"` for a user with a tenant (it was `"alice"` for every tenant). Users without a tenant are listed as before.
- **`AuditMiddleware(include_args=True)` writes a nested Pydantic argument as a JSON object**, not its `str()` (`"sku='MS-30' qty=2"`); dataclasses, dates and sets get their JSON form too. Existing log files still verify. `StructuredLoggingMiddleware(include_args=True)` does the same.
- **`promptise run` stats payloads have new keys**: `totals.tool_denied` and a `denied` count per tool. Code that compared a tool row exactly needs updating.
- **A `Prompt` used as instructions now comes before other system messages** -- before, it came after the first system message in the input (for example `chat(system_prompt=...)`). Check prompts that relied on the old order.
- **With a context engine, a `Prompt`'s context providers run on every call** -- before, only the build-time render was sent. Providers that are slow or have side effects now run once per call.

### Added

- **`ProcessConfig(agent_pattern=...)`** -- choose the reasoning pattern of a runtime process, so a run started by a cron schedule or a webhook can use `"peoatr"`, `"research"` or any other name from `AGENT_PATTERNS` (or, in code, a `PromptGraph`). An unknown name fails when the config is created. `.agent` manifests set it with a top-level `agent_pattern:` key. Before, `ProcessConfig` refused the key and every process ran the default ReAct loop.
- **`TimelineEntry.tenant_id` and `TimelineEntry.isolation_key`** -- every trace entry records the caller's tenant next to its user id. `for_user()` and `purge_user()` take `tenant_id=` (or the `"tenant::user"` key), `query()` takes `tenant_ids=`, and the OTLP transporter sets `promptise.tenant_id`.
- **Refused tool calls on the trace** -- a call the autonomy budget refuses, or an approval gate denies, is a `tool.call` / `tool.result` with `status="denied"`, `denied_by` and the `reason`. `get_stats()` counts them as `tool_denied_count`, and `promptise run --devtools` prints them and counts them in its summary.

### Fixed

- **Two tenants' users with the same id merged in traces** -- entries stored `caller.user_id` only, so `acme`'s `alice` and `globex`'s `alice` were one user, and `purge_user("alice")` deleted both.
- **`chat(session_id=...)` left the session empty in traces** -- every entry of the run now carries it.
- **A call refused by the autonomy budget was missing from traces and `--devtools`** -- with `max_irreversible_per_run=3`, the 4th call reached only the journal (`tool_denied`) and the `permission_denied` hook.
- **`approval.*` events reached the event notifier but not the timeline** -- `approval.requested`, `approval.granted` and `approval.denied` are now recorded on the observed run (a `CALLBACK` transporter, `--devtools`), with a new `approval.timeout` entry, and a denied call's `tool.result` says `status="denied"` instead of looking like a successful call.
- **`promptise run --check` failed for an agent factory in a package** -- with `agent = "customer_agent.agent:create_agent"`, `promptise run --mode chat --check` reported "No module named 'customer_agent'" while the real run worked, because the check imported the factory before it put the project root on `sys.path`. `--check` now adds the project root and the `python_path` entries first, the same way the run does.
- **A `Prompt` as `build_agent(instructions=...)` reached the model twice** -- its build-time render was the graph's system prompt, and the per-request render (with the context providers) was added as a second system message, so every block was sent twice on every call. The model now gets the per-request render once, as the start of its one system message, after a conversation flow's prompt and before the tool list. If the render fails or is empty, the build-time render is sent instead.
- **`astream()`, `astream_with_tools()` and the context-engine path skipped a `Prompt`'s context providers** -- they sent only the build-time render. They now send the per-request render too.
- **A `PromptSuite` as instructions sent `<MySuite prompts=N>` as a system message** -- its build-time text was the suite's `repr`. It is now `suite.system_prompt()`, used for the cache key, the context engine's `identity` budget and the fallback.

---

## v1.8.0 — 2026-10-23

A security fix for multi-tenant approval review, internal APIs in `OpenAPIProvider`, and a cap for large tool results that tells the model when a result was cut.

### Security

- **`PendingApprover`'s reviewer tools crossed tenants** -- `approvals_list` returned every pending call on the server, arguments included, and `approvals_decide` released any of them, so on a multi-tenant server one tenant's approver could read and approve another tenant's call. Both tools are now scoped to the reviewer's `tenant_id`: another tenant's request is invisible and answered like an unknown id. `PendingApprover(same_tenant_reviewers=False)` keeps one platform-wide review team.

### Upgrade notes

- **Approvers need the tenant of the calls they review.** A reviewer key without a `tenant_id` now sees only requests without one. If one review team decides for every tenant, pass `same_tenant_reviewers=False`.
- **`OpenAPIProvider.register()` fails fast without an absolute API address.** A spec with no `servers` entry, or a relative one and no spec URL to resolve it against, now raises a `ValueError` at registration instead of failing every call. Pass `base_url`.
- **`ToolNode(max_result_chars=...)` output changed.** A cut result now ends with a marker line; code that compared the cut text exactly needs updating.

### Added

- **`ContextCompaction(max_result_chars=...)`** -- cap every new tool result the agent passes to the model. A longer result is cut and ends with a marker that gives its full length and tells the model to call again with narrower arguments. Off by default (`None`: results pass in full) and independent of `enabled`. Without a marker, a model given a cut result tends to repeat the same call.
- **`OpenAPIProvider(allow_private=True)`** -- bridge an internal API on purpose. A spec URL or base URL on a loopback, private or link-local address is still refused by default.

### Fixed

- **`OpenAPIProvider` told you to use `base_url` for internal APIs, which was refused too** -- the error now names `allow_private=True`.
- **A relative `servers` URL (`/v1`) made every OpenAPI tool call fail** with "Request URL is missing an 'http://' or 'https://' protocol". It is now resolved against the spec's URL. When there is no spec URL to resolve against (a file or dict spec), or the spec has no `servers` entry, `register()` raises a `ValueError` that says to pass `base_url`.
- **`ToolNode(max_result_chars=...)` cut results silently** -- a cut result now ends with the same marker.
- **The docs did not say that `Depends()` runs before authentication and guards** -- a dependency cannot read the caller's tenant, roles or scopes. The MCP server security page now says so and shows where to read them instead.

---

## v1.7.1 — 2026-10-23

Three security fixes: approval request ids are now single-use as the docs said, a classifier rule that raises no longer lets a call through, and API keys and JWTs can be revoked while an MCP server runs.

### Upgrade notes

- **A second decision for the same approval request is refused.** `QueueApprovalHandler.submit_decision()` raises `ApprovalReplayError` for it (a `KeyError` subclass, so code that catches `KeyError` keeps working); before, it was ignored without a word. `PendingApprover.decide()` still returns `False`, and now logs a warning.
- **A classifier deny rule whose predicate raises now denies the call**, and an allow rule that raises sends the call to the fallback handler instead of letting the read-only check or the LLM classifier decide it. Fix or remove rules that raise.
- **The HTTP auth gate (`MCPServer(require_auth=True)`) calls `averify_token()`** when the provider has one, and a verifier that raises now rejects the request with `401` instead of failing it with an error. A custom provider with only `verify_token()` works as before.

### Security

- **Approval: request ids are single-use** -- the docs said request ids are "tracked to prevent replay attacks", but the agent's gate only wrote them to a set nobody read, so a second decision for the same id was accepted. Each request id now takes exactly one decision, and a second one is refused and logged on the `promptise.approval` logger (`promptise.server.approval` on an MCP server): the agent's gate and `ApprovalGateMiddleware` deny the call without asking the reviewer again, `QueueApprovalHandler.submit_decision()` raises `ApprovalReplayError`, `PendingApprover.decide()` returns `False` and `approvals_decide` answers that the request was already decided. `WebhookApprovalHandler` ignores a poll response whose `request_id` is another request's (a stale decision behind a fixed `poll_url`, or a replayed one) and keeps polling; responses without `request_id` are accepted as before. On the approval service, `verify_webhook_signature(..., replay_cache=RequestIdCache())` rejects a signed request whose `request_id` already passed; `max_age` alone still accepts a copy until the timestamp is too old. Decided ids are kept in the new `promptise.approval.RequestIdCache`, in memory, for the approval `timeout` (twice `max_age` for signatures) and at most 10,000 at a time. See "Single-Use Request IDs" in docs/core/approval.md.
- **Approval classifier: a rule that raises no longer fails open** -- an `ApprovalRule` predicate that raised counted as "no match", so a broken deny rule let through the calls it was written to stop: they went on to the allow rules, the read-only check or the LLM classifier. A deny rule that cannot be evaluated now denies the call (`decided_by="classifier"`, `trace.layer="deny_rule"`, the exception type in the reason), an ask rule that raises sends the call to the fallback handler, and an allow rule that raises never allows: the call goes straight to the fallback (`trace.layer="error"`). Each case logs a warning and counts in `stats.errors`. `ApprovalRule.evaluate()` raises the new `RuleEvaluationError`; `ApprovalRule.matches()` keeps returning `False`. See "A rule that raises" in docs/core/approval-classifier.md.
- **MCP server: API keys can be revoked without a restart** -- `APIKeyAuth` copied its keys when it was created, so a leaked key stayed valid until the server restarted. With a `keys` dict, `revoke_key(key)` and `add_key(key, config)` change the keys for the next request. `keys=` also takes a callable (sync or async) that returns the keys valid right now, and the new `lookup=` takes a callable (sync or async) that receives the presented key and returns its config, or `None` for an unknown or revoked key, so the keys can live in a database or cache. A callable that raises rejects the request. See "Revoking and adding keys while the server runs" in docs/mcp/server/auth-security.md.
- **MCP server: JWTs can be revoked before they expire** -- `JWTAuth`, `AsymmetricJWTAuth` and `JwksAuth` accepted a token until its `exp`. They now take an optional deny list, checked on every request (cached tokens included): `revoked_jtis=[...]`, `revoked_subjects=[...]`, `revoke(jti, subject=..., until=...)` and `unrevoke(...)` at runtime, and `is_revoked=`, a hook (sync or async) that gets the verified claims and returns `True` to reject the token, for a deny list shared between replicas. `until` (normally the token's `exp`) drops an entry once the token has expired. A revoked token is rejected with `Token has been revoked`; a hook that raises rejects the token. `create_token()` does not add a `jti`, so pass one in the payload to revoke single tokens, or revoke by subject. All four providers get `averify_token()`, which runs async stores and hooks; the sync `verify_token()` rejects the credential when one is async. See "Revoking tokens" in docs/mcp/server/auth-security.md.

### Changed

- Docs: the ConversationFlow, ModelLike and default system prompt pages name their API in the page title and headings (`ConversationFlow`, `ModelLike`, `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT`), and the ModelLike page says it has four forms (it said three). No anchor other pages link to changed.
- Docs: the approval, approval classifier, approval gates, auth and deployment pages describe exactly what the fixes above enforce, and the identity guide links to token revocation.

## v1.7.0 — 2026-10-23

### Added

- **Sandbox: `docker_network`** -- `SandboxConfig(docker_network="agent-egress")` (also in `.superagent` files) attaches the sandbox container to an existing Docker network instead of Docker's default bridge, with `network="full"` or `"restricted"`. With an `--internal` network whose only way out is an egress proxy, sandboxed code reaches only the hosts the proxy allows; `network="restricted"` alone filters ports, not hosts. A network that does not exist stops the session from starting with a message that says how to create it, and the host's network, `none` and another container's network are refused. See "Allowing only some hosts" in docs/core/sandbox.md.
- **Guardrails: `InjectionDetector(paragraphs=True)`** -- also classifies every paragraph (line of up to 512 characters) of the scanned text on its own and blocks on the highest score. The 512-character windows can dilute a short instruction: on a support page whose last paragraph was an instruction for AI assistants, its window scored 0.58 (below the 0.85 threshold) and the paragraph alone 0.99, so a tool result with it passed. Off by default: it costs one classification per paragraph, and the default model flags short lines of technical text (code, commands, headings) much more often than whole windows. See "Short instructions in longer text" in docs/core/guardrails.md.
- **MCP server: elicitation on the 2026-07-28 protocol** -- 2026-07-28 has no server-to-client requests, so `Elicitor.ask()` there now asks in an input round: the tool call ends with an `InputRequiredResult` holding the question, the client (the mcp 2.x `Client` does this on its own) asks its user and sends the call again with the answer, and the tool runs again, where `ask()` returns the answer. A tool that asks several questions takes one round per question; the earlier answers travel in the result's `request_state`, encrypted and bound to the tool, its arguments and the server by the SDK's `RequestStateBoundary`. `MCPServer(request_state_security=RequestStateSecurity(keys=[...]))` shares the key between workers or replicas (by default it is made at startup and held by the process). The approval gate's `ElicitationApprover` works the same way. Code before `ask()` runs once per round, so side effects belong after the answer. `Elicitor.last_failure` says why `ask()` returned `None`. See "Elicitation on the 2026-07-28 protocol" in docs/mcp/server/advanced-patterns.md.
- **Runtime: local tools with `ProcessConfig(extra_tools=[...])` and `AgentProcess(..., extra_tools=[...])`** -- an Agent Runtime process can now give its agent Python functions as tools without an MCP server, with the same semantics as `build_agent(extra_tools=...)`: `@tool` functions, plain functions and LangChain tools, and `TypeError` for anything else when the config or process is created. The tools reach every agent the process builds (first start, restarts, hot reloads, new instructions), and budget, approval, health and journal apply to them as to MCP tools. They are code only, like a hook's `callback`: `model_dump()` and `RuntimeConfig.to_dict()` leave them out, and a manifest or YAML config can't set them. Processes spawned in open mode don't inherit them, and an open-mode agent can't create a tool with the name of one. See "Local tools" in docs/runtime/processes.md.
- **Runtime: a model object for a process, with `ProcessConfig(model=...)` and `AgentProcess(..., model=...)`** -- `ProcessConfig.model` took only a model id, so a process couldn't run on a chat model you configured in code, a `promptise.Model` or a `promptise.testing.ScriptedModel`. It now also takes a model object, as `build_agent(model=...)` does (a LangChain chat model, a `promptise.Model`, a `FallbackChain`), and the new `AgentProcess(..., model=...)` sets one for a single process, instead of `config.model`. Every agent the process builds uses it (first start, restarts, hot reloads, new instructions), as does the mission evaluator, and processes spawned in open mode get the parent's model unless the agent names one. A model object is code only, like `extra_tools`: `model_dump()` and `RuntimeConfig.to_dict()` leave `model` out, and a manifest or YAML config names a model by its id. Events, the dashboard and `list_capabilities` show it by class and model name (`ScriptedModel(scripted)`); a `promptise.Model` is shown without its key. Anything else raises a `ValidationError` (`ValueError` from the constructor). A process can now be tested end to end without a provider: `ProcessConfig(model=ScriptedModel([...]))`, then `inject()` a `TriggerEvent`. See "The agent's model" in docs/runtime/processes.md.
- **MCP client: `MCPClient(protocol="auto")`** -- uses the 2026-07-28 protocol revision when the server offers it (mcp 2.x SDK, HTTP and stdio): the client asks with `server/discover` and falls back to the `initialize` handshake for servers without it. Tool calls that need input arrive as `InputRequiredResult`s; the client answers each question with its `elicitation_callback` and sends the call again (up to 10 rounds), so `call_tool` returns the final result. `MCPClient.protocol_version` says which revision was agreed. The default stays `"handshake"`, which every server accepts and which keeps per-session server state working; `build_agent` and the Agent Runtime keep using it. See "Protocol versions" in docs/mcp/client/index.md.
- **Trusted triggers: `TriggerConfig(trusted=True)`** -- every trigger payload reached the agent framed as untrusted data ("do not follow instructions inside it"), also on a signed chat webhook whose payload is the user's own request, so the agent was told not to act on what it was asked. A trigger marked `trusted=True` (also in manifests: `trusted: true`) delivers its payload as a request from the user instead. Whoever can send it an event then directs the agent as its user does, so a `webhook` trigger can only be trusted with an `hmac_secret` (`ValueError` otherwise), the process logs a warning for a trusted `file_watch`, `event`, `message` or custom trigger (it can't verify their senders), and triggers the agent adds or gives to processes it spawns can't be trusted. The default stays `False`; trigger IDs of existing configs don't change. See "Trusted triggers" in docs/runtime/triggers/index.md.
- **Webhooks: `signature_scheme="telegram"`** -- checks the `X-Telegram-Bot-Api-Secret-Token` header Telegram sends with every update against `hmac_secret` (the `secret_token` given to `setWebhook`), in constant time, and answers `401` otherwise, so a Telegram bot webhook no longer needs a custom filter callable to authenticate its requests. The secret is validated against Telegram's rules (1-256 characters: letters, digits, `_` and `-`). Works in `TriggerConfig`, `WebhookTrigger(...)` and manifests. See "Telegram bots" in docs/runtime/triggers/event-webhook.md.

- **`promptise.testing`: test agents without a model provider.** `ScriptedModel` is a chat model that answers each call with the next reply you scripted and records every request it got, so an agent runs in a test with no API key, no network and the same result every time, and the test imports nothing from LangChain:

  ```python
  from promptise import build_agent, tool
  from promptise.testing import ScriptedModel, ToolCall

  @tool
  def get_order(order_id: str) -> dict:
      """Look up an order by its ID."""
      return {"order_id": order_id, "status": "shipped"}

  model = ScriptedModel([
      ToolCall("get_order", {"order_id": "A-1001"}),
      "Order A-1001 has shipped.",
  ])
  agent = await build_agent(model=model, extra_tools=[get_order])
  result = await agent.ainvoke({"messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Where is A-1001?"}]})
  assert model.calls[1].tools == ["get_order"]
  ```

  A reply is a string (the model's answer), a `ToolCall(name, args, id=None)` or a list of them (a turn that calls tools; ids default to `call_1`, `call_2` ...), an `AIMessage`, or a `Malformed` reply in the shapes small models get wrong: `Malformed.tool_call_as_text(name, args, style="json" | "tagged" | "fenced")` writes the call into the text, `Malformed.unparseable_arguments(name, raw_args)` sends arguments that are not JSON (an `invalid_tool_calls` entry). Invented tool names and invalid arguments are plain `ToolCall` replies. `model.calls` holds a `ModelCall` per request (`messages`, the bound `tools` and their `tool_schemas`, `reply`), and `model.remaining` counts the replies not sent yet. A call after the last reply raises `ScriptExhaustedError`, which says which call it was (in an agent run, `ainvoke()` raises `GraphExecutionError` caused by it); `on_exhausted="repeat_last"` repeats the last reply instead. The model supports `ainvoke`/`invoke`, `astream`/`stream` (text word by word, tool calls as tool-call chunks) and `bind_tools`, so it works with every agent pattern, `astream_with_tools()`, a `FallbackChain` and graph nodes. `promptise.testing` is not imported by `import promptise`. See docs/core/testing.md.
- **MCP server: tool versions with `@server.tool(version=...)`** -- `VersionedToolRegistry` was exported, and its docstring showed `@server.tool(version="1.0")`, but the server never used it and `tool()` had no `version` parameter. Each version is now served under a pinned name, `search_v1_0` (characters other than letters and digits become `_`, so LLM providers accept the name), and the bare name `search` runs the highest version, compared numerically per dot-separated part. Each version keeps its own schema, validation and options. Registering a version twice, or a version next to an unversioned tool of the same name, raises `ValueError`. `@router.tool(version=...)` works the same, with the router's prefix on every name. See "Tool Versioning" in docs/mcp/server/advanced-patterns.md.
- **MCP server: `server.add_transform(...)`** -- the exported `NamespaceTransform`, `VisibilityTransform`, `TagFilterTransform` and the `ToolTransform` protocol were never applied by the server. `add_transform()` now applies them, in order, to `tools/list` and the `docs://manifest` resource, with the listing request's context (authenticated with the server's `AuthMiddleware` when there is one). A call by a renamed tool's listed name runs the tool: renaming transforms implement `resolve_name()`, which `NamespaceTransform` does. Transforms change discovery only; a hidden tool can still be called by its name, so access decisions stay in guards. `apply()` may be `async`, and a `VisibilityTransform` predicate that raises hides its tool. See "Tool Transforms" in docs/mcp/server/advanced-patterns.md.

### Fixed

- **Browser clients on the 2026-07-28 protocol were refused by CORS.** A 2026-07-28 client sends `mcp-method` and `mcp-name` headers with every request, and the default `CORSConfig.allow_headers` did not list them, so the browser's preflight got `400 Disallowed CORS headers`. Both are in the default list now. The `SESSION_REQUIRED` error also names how to connect with the handshake from Promptise's own client (`MCPClient`, whose default is `protocol="handshake"`), not only the MCP SDK's `mode='legacy'`.
- **Trace and webhook redaction missed most GitHub tokens.** `redact_sensitive` (the sanitizer `build_agent` installs on its observability collector) and the event notifier's payload redaction knew only classic `ghp_` personal access tokens, so a GitHub CLI token (`gho_`) in a tool result reached the HTML report, log files, webhooks and OpenTelemetry spans as it was. They now redact every GitHub token format: `ghp_`, `gho_`, `ghu_`, `ghs_` (including the longer `ghs_<app id>_<JWT>` installation tokens GitHub is rolling out), `ghr_` and `github_pat_`, with no fixed length. The same review added the credentials that also went through unredacted: temporary AWS access key IDs (`ASIA…`), an AWS secret access key after `aws_secret_access_key=`, Stripe (`sk_live_…`, `rk_test_…`) and Google (`AIza…`) API keys, GitLab and Slack tokens, JWTs and PEM private keys. The guardrails' `CredentialDetector` already knew `gho_`, `ghu_` and `ghr_`; it now also detects `ghs_` installation tokens (97 patterns), and its GitHub patterns take tokens longer than 40 characters whole.
- **The guardrails' bearer pattern left the token in the text.** `CredentialDetector`'s `bearer_token` pattern matched `Authorization: Bearer` and stopped at the space, so `Authorization: Bearer 7f3c…` was redacted to `[BEARER_TOKEN] 7f3c…`: the opaque token reached the model, and the trace sanitizer, which looked for the word `Bearer`, no longer recognised it either. The pattern now takes the whole value: `Authorization:` (or `=`) with any scheme (`Bearer`, `Basic`, `Token`...) or none, and a bearer token on its own (`Bearer <token>`, any case, at least 16 characters so prose such as "the bearer token expired" is not a finding). The trace and webhook sanitizer also redacts every `Authorization` value whatever its scheme (`Authorization: Basic [REDACTED]`), a lower-case `bearer <token>`, and the value of a credential key whatever it looks like: `Authorization`, `Proxy-Authorization`, `X-API-Key`, `api_key`, `access_token`, `refresh_token`, `id_token`, `client_secret`, `password` and `passwd`, as a dict key (a request's headers, a tool's arguments) or in JSON text (a tool result).
- **Sandbox `network="restricted"` no longer reaches link-local addresses.** The egress filter allowed TCP 80 and 443 to every address, including `169.254.169.254`, where cloud providers serve instance metadata and credentials over plain HTTP. It now drops `169.254.0.0/16`, `fe80::/10` and `fd00:ec2::254` (DNS to a resolver there still works). It still filters ports, not hosts: DNS and TCP 80/443 reach any other host.
- **A memory provider left at the default `SHARED` scope gave one user's memories to another, without a word.** With `memory_auto_store=True` the agent stores every exchange, and a provider created without `scope=` pools every caller's entries, so a note one employee gave a shared assistant ("Priya's new salary is 145,000") came back in a colleague's conversation. The default stays `SHARED` (changing it would break agents that share memory on purpose), but the agent (and `MemoryAgent`) now logs a warning the first time such a provider serves a second caller identity, saying what is shared and to pass `scope=MemoryScope.PER_USER`. A provider created with an explicit `scope=MemoryScope.SHARED` is a deliberate choice and is not reported. The memory page says this up front.
- **`purge_user` found nothing for a caller with a tenant.** The agent stores a caller's memories under its isolation key, `"acme::dana"` for `CallerContext(user_id="dana", tenant_id="acme")`, and `purge_user("dana")` looked for `"dana"`, so it removed nothing and returned `0`. `purge_user` on every memory provider (and the `MemoryProvider` protocol) now takes `tenant_id=`, as `SemanticCache.purge_user` does: `purge_user("dana", tenant_id="acme")`. The new `promptise.memory.owner_key(user_id, tenant_id)` returns the key entries are stored under.
- **On a 2026-07-28 connection a question to the user was a silent "no", and per-session state was lost.** `Elicitor.ask()` sent `elicitation/create`, which that protocol revision doesn't allow, and returned `None` with a warning in the server log only, so a confirmation step answered "cancelled" and an `ElicitationApprover` denied with "client declined", though nobody was asked. The question now goes to the client in an input round (see Added), and a client that did not declare the elicitation capability is told so: `ask()` returns `None` with `last_failure` set, and the approval denial says "the client did not declare the elicitation capability". A tool that takes a `SessionState` got new, empty state on every 2026-07-28 request (there is no session), so a cart kept one item; it now answers with a `SESSION_REQUIRED` error that names the protocol version and says to connect with the handshake. The same error answers every request to a server served with `asgi_app(stateless=True)`, which also gave every call a fresh state. On handshake connections session state is kept per connection object and dropped with it; it was keyed by the object's `id()`, never removed, and a later connection could get the state of an earlier one whose address was reused. `Sampler` logs that sampling isn't available on 2026-07-28 instead of a transport error.
- **A tool that timed out told the model to "retry later", also when the call had done its work.** `@tool(timeout=...)`, `@server.tool(timeout=...)` and `TimeoutMiddleware` answered every timeout with `retryable: true` and a suggestion to retry, though a cancelled call may already have sent the email or charged the card, and a sync `@tool` function keeps running in its thread and completes. A model retried a timed-out `send_invoice` twice and three invoices went out. The `TIMEOUT` error now says the outcome is unknown (and that the call is still running, for a sync function), is `retryable` only for a tool marked read-only or idempotent, and otherwise tells the model to check whether the call took effect before calling the tool again. `@tool` takes `read_only=` and `idempotent=` for this (the MCP `readOnlyHint` / `idempotentHint` annotations, which the semantic cache and approval rules read too); MCP server tools use their `read_only_hint` / `idempotent_hint`. A `TIMEOUT` still counts as a failure for the circuit breaker when it is not retryable.
- **A tool call the budget refused left no trace in the journal.** The refused call never ran, so it had no `tool_call` entry, even at journal level `"full"`, and the violation the process then acted on was not journaled either: the journal showed only the `state_transition` to `suspended` with reason `"budget"`. A refused call now writes a `tool_denied` entry (`tool`, `kind`, `reason`, `limit_name`, `limit_value`, `current_value`, and at level `"full"` the call's `args`, redacted like `tool_call` arguments), and every violation the process acts on (`on_exceeded`), including a run refused by `max_runs_per_day`, writes a `budget_violation` entry (`limit_name`, `limit_value`, `current_value`, `tool_name`, `blocked`, `action`). The `permission_denied` hook for a refused tool call also gets the call's `arguments`, and `BudgetViolation` has an `arguments` field. See docs/runtime/journal/index.md.
- **`promptise.__version__` did not exist.** `import promptise; promptise.__version__` raised `AttributeError`; only `promptise --version` and `importlib.metadata.version("promptise")` gave the version. The package now sets `__version__` from the installed package metadata, and to `"0.0.0+unknown"` when it is imported from a source tree that was never installed.
- **`SIGTERM` cut off the run in flight, and the run was lost on exit.** `AgentProcess.stop()`, and with it `AgentRuntime.run_forever()` on `SIGTERM` or Ctrl+C, cancelled a run in the middle with no grace period, after its tools may already have acted, and the event went to the in-memory dead letters, which end with the program. `run_forever()` now stops gracefully: no new trigger event starts (a webhook answers 503, `inject()` dead-letters), runs in flight get up to `ProcessConfig.drain_timeout` seconds (default 5) to finish, and only the ones still running then are cancelled; a second signal cancels them at once. `stop(drain_timeout=N)` does the same for one process (`None` uses the config value), as do `stop_all(drain_timeout=...)` and `shutdown(drain_timeout=...)`, where the processes drain at the same time; `process.abort_drain()` and `request_shutdown(force=True)` end the wait early. A run that is cancelled is logged, journaled as a `run_interrupted` entry at every journal level and kept in the journal checkpoint, so the next process object with the same name and journal lists it in `dead_letters` again (counted in `recovered["interrupted_runs"]`) and `redeliver_dead_letters()` can run it. The control API's `POST /api/v1/processes/{name}/stop` and `POST /api/v1/runtime/stop-all` take an optional `drain_timeout` in the JSON body (0 to 3600 seconds; anything else is `422 INVALID_FIELD`) and answer once the processes have stopped, with `interrupted_runs` (the runs the stop cancelled) and, for one process, the `drain_timeout` used. `POST /api/v1/runtime/shutdown` passes a `drain_timeout` on to `on_shutdown`, and `request_shutdown(drain_timeout=...)` sets it for that shutdown. `promptise runtime stop --drain-timeout SECONDS` uses them, for one process and for a whole runtime (the wait for the runtime to exit then lasts at least the drain plus 10 seconds); `OrchestrationClient.stop_process()`, `stop_all()` and `shutdown()` and `AgentRuntime.stop_process()` take `drain_timeout=`. See "Graceful stop" in docs/runtime/processes.md.
- **`build_agent(max_agent_iterations=...)` above 15 still stopped the default agent after 15 model calls.** The default ReAct graph's `reason` node had its own limit of 15 (and the engine a per-node ceiling of 25), so a run with `max_agent_iterations=40` ended with "reached its iteration limit (15)". `build_agent` now gives the node the same limit as the run, so `max_agent_iterations=N` allows N model calls whatever N is.
- **A call to a tool that doesn't exist got no hint.** The error result was `Error: Unknown tool 'get_ordr'`, so a model that invented or misspelled a tool name had nothing to correct it with and often tried the same name again. It now lists the agent's tools, the names closest to the one called first, at most 20 of them followed by how many more there are, and suggests the tool whose name the called one looks like a misspelling of: `Error: Unknown tool 'get_ordr'. Did you mean 'get_order'? Call one of the available tools: get_order, refund_order.` A name that only shares a word with a tool (`cancel_order` and `get_order`) gets no suggestion. This applies to the tool calls of every built-in agent pattern and of a `ToolNode`. The text still starts with `Error: Unknown tool '<name>'`.
- **A tool call whose arguments were not valid JSON ended the run without an answer.** LangChain keeps such a call (a Python-style dict with single quotes, a cut-off object) in the message's `invalid_tool_calls`, and the built-in agent patterns neither ran it nor answered it, so the run ended on that message with empty content, and a conversation continued from it was refused by OpenAI, since the call had no tool result. Every built-in pattern and a `ToolNode` now answer each such call with a tool error (`status="error"`, the call's own `tool_call_id`) that says what is wrong and names the tool's parameters: `Error: The arguments for 'get_order' were not valid JSON (Expecting property name enclosed in double quotes at line 1 column 2). Call the tool again with a JSON object that matches its parameters: order_id (required).` A call to a tool the agent doesn't have gets the unknown-tool error. The tool loop goes on, so the model can call again; the call counts as a failed tool call (`tool_calls_failed`, and in `NodeResult.tool_calls` with `success: False`, `invalid: True` and the text the model sent as `raw_args`), callbacks get an `on_tool_error` with a `ToolArgumentError`, streams show it as a failed call, and iteration limits apply as before. A call without an id gets one, on the assistant message too, so every call has exactly one result. Context compaction keeps such an exchange, and a `FallbackChain` stream keeps the call.
- **`OpenAPIProvider` tools could not be called.** Each generated tool's input schema was a single required `kwargs` field, so a call with the operation's real arguments failed with `VALIDATION_ERROR`, and the method-based annotations were computed and then dropped. A tool's input schema is now the operation's parameters (path-level ones included) and JSON request body from the spec, with every `$ref` inlined; calls are validated against it, and each argument is sent where the spec puts it (path, URL-encoded; query; header; cookie; body). Arguments the caller leaves out are not sent. Swagger 2.x `in: body` parameters and parameter types are read too. The tools carry the annotations (`read_only_hint` for `GET`/`HEAD`, `destructive_hint` for `DELETE`, `idempotent_hint`, `open_world_hint=True`, the summary as title).
- **A tool that returned a Pydantic model sent `str(model)`.** `-> Book` sent `title='Dune' year=1965`, and a `list[Book]` a JSON list of those strings. A model is now sent as its JSON (`model_dump(mode="json")`), also inside a `dict` or `list`, the same form a resource sends; resources now also serialise models inside a `list` or `dict` as JSON objects.
- **`StructuredLoggingMiddleware(include_args=True)` logged no arguments.** The flag was stored and never used. The `tool_call_start` line now has `args`, the call's validated arguments after `redact_sensitive` (credential keys become `[REDACTED]`, credentials and common PII in strings become placeholders).
- **`WebhookMiddleware` held up every tool call and kept every payload.** The docstring said deliveries never block, but each one was awaited inside the call, so a slow endpoint added up to `timeout` seconds per event (twice per call with the default events), and `sent` grew for the life of the process. Events now go on a bounded queue (`max_queue=1000`) and are sent by background tasks (`max_concurrency=4`, one HTTP client each); a full queue drops the event with a warning and counts it in `dropped`. `sent` keeps the last `history=100` payloads. New `flush()` waits for queued deliveries and `aclose()` stops the delivery tasks.
- **`APIKeyAuth(header="X-API-Key")` never authenticated over HTTP, and the transport gate ignored a custom header.** Headers reach `ctx.meta` lower-cased and the name was used as given; the `require_auth=True` gate only read `x-api-key`. The header name is now matched case-insensitively, and the gate reads the API key from the header `APIKeyAuth` was configured with (its 401 message names it).
- **Security: `SubprocessShellExecutor(allowlist=...)` could be bypassed.** With the default `shell=True`, only the first word was checked and the whole line went to `/bin/sh`, so `` !`echo hi; <any command>` `` ran the second command, although the docstring recommended the allowlist for templates from untrusted sources. With an allowlist, a command now never runs through a shell (`shell` is ignored): it is split with `shlex`, its first word must be an allowlisted name exactly, and it is started directly; an unquoted shell operator (`;`, `|`, `&`, `<`, `>`, `(`, `)`) raises `ShellExecutionError`. Shell builtins can't be allowlisted any more (`echo` on Windows), and pipes or redirections in allowlisted commands are refused. Command output is now literal text in the template: braces in it no longer raise `KeyError` or get read as `{variable}` syntax.
- **`@prompt(observe=True)`, `PromptBuilder.observe()` and YAML `observe: true` recorded nothing.** The flag was stored and `PromptObserver` was never called. An observed prompt now records every standalone call as timeline events: `PROMPT_START`, `PROMPT_CONTEXT` per context provider, `PROMPT_GUARD_BLOCK` when a guard rejects, then `PROMPT_END` or `PROMPT_ERROR`. `observe=True` records to the prompt's own `ObservabilityCollector` (`prompt.observer.collector`); `observe=` also takes a collector or a `PromptObserver` to record somewhere shared, and the new `Prompt.with_observer(...)` attaches one to any prompt. A failing observer never fails the call. `PromptObserver.collector` is new.
- **`ConversationFlow.transition()` skipped the handler of a phase its handler moved to.** When the handler run by `transition("b")` called `ctx.transition("c")`, the flow entered `c` without running `c`'s handler, unlike `start()` and `next_turn()`; and in `start()` and `next_turn()` a third chained transition was dropped. All three now follow transitions requested by handlers, running each phase's handler, up to 5 phases per call; circular transitions raise `RecursionError` (the depth limit never triggered before).
- **`PromptInspector` traces had no context providers and no passed input guards.** `context_providers` now lists each provider that ran (class name, characters, time), for calls and `render_async()`, and `guards_passed` lists each guard once when it passed every check it ran (input-only guards included); a guard that passes the input but rejects the output is listed only in `guards_failed`.
- **`PromptStats.input_tokens` / `output_tokens` were always `0`.** They now come from the reply's `usage_metadata` when the provider reports usage.
- **A `.superagent` `optimize_tools:` mapping was ignored.** It reached `build_agent` as a plain dict, which became the `minimal` preset, so `level: semantic`, `top_k` and `embedding_model` had no effect. The mapping now becomes a `ToolOptimizationConfig`: `level`, `top_k` (`semantic_top_k`), `embedding_model`, and the new `context_turns`, `max_description_length`, `preserve_tools` and `always_include_fallback`; fields left out take the level's preset (`top_k` and `embedding_model` no longer default to values that were never applied). `score_threshold` has no counterpart (semantic selection offers the `top_k` most relevant tools), so it is accepted with a warning. A string other than `minimal`, `standard` or `semantic` is now a validation error, and the schema says `true` means the `minimal` level (it said "True or 'semantic' for defaults").
- **A `.superagent` `cache:` mapping always failed to build.** `SemanticCache(**section)` raised `TypeError: unexpected keyword argument 'embedding_model'`, even for `cache: {backend: memory}`. `embedding_model` is now passed as `SemanticCache`'s `embedding`, and the section gains `SemanticCache`'s other options: `max_total_entries`, `ttl_patterns`, `invalidate_on_write`, `cache_multi_turn`, `cache_tool_turns`, `write_tools`, `read_only_tools` and `shared_data_acknowledged`.
- **A `HumanNode` approved on its own when nobody could answer it.** Without a `_human_handler` in the run's config, the node took its `on_approve` route at once (`"No human handler configured, auto-approved"`), and `build_agent` never set a handler, so an approval step in an agent's graph was always skipped. A handler that returned a denied `ApprovalDecision` also counted as approval (the object is truthy). The node now fails without a handler (fail closed): the run raises `GraphExecutionError` ("HumanNode 'approve' has no human handler ...") unless an `on_error` edge routes the failure. `build_agent(approval=...)` answers `HumanNode` steps with the agent's approval handler (the request carries the node's prompt as `context_summary`, the node name as `tool_name` and `metadata["source"] = "human_node"`; a handler error is a denial), `PromptGraphEngine` takes `human_handler=`, and a decision object counts by its `approved` attribute. See Changed.
- **`PromptNode` ignored `tool_choice`, `temperature` and `max_tokens`.** They were stored and saved but never reached the model: tools were bound without a `tool_choice`, and the call used the model's own sampling settings. `tool_choice="required"` (sent as LangChain's portable `"any"`) or a tool's name now forces the node's first model call; once tool results come back the model decides, so a forced node can still answer. `"none"` calls the model without the tools. `temperature` and `max_tokens` are set on a copy of the model under the provider's field names (`max_output_tokens` for Google, `num_predict` for Ollama).
- **A plain-text reply never reached `output_key` or a postprocessor.** `NodeResult.output` stayed `None` for a text reply, so `output_key` was not written and postprocessors such as `json_extractor()` and `confidence_scorer()` got `None`. The reply text is now the node's output. Postprocessors run on the model's reply only, not on a step that only calls tools.
- **Reasoning nodes did nothing without an `output_schema`.** `ReflectNode`, `ObserveNode`, `JustifyNode`, `CritiqueNode`, `PlanNode` and `ValidateNode` read their fields (`mistake`, `entities`, `subgoals`, `severity`, `passes`, `route` ...) only from a structured output, and no node sets a schema, so reflections were never stored, `state.plan` never set and severity and pass/fail routing never ran. Without a schema the node now asks the model to end its reply with a JSON object holding those fields and parses it; the reply text stays in the conversation. With an `output_schema` nothing changes. `ThinkNode` and `SynthesizeNode` stay plain text.
- **A preprocessor could not stop its node.** An exception in a `PromptNode` preprocessor was logged and the model was called anyway, so `input_validator()` only logged a missing key. The node now fails before the model call (`"Preprocessor failed in node 'x': ..."`, with the exception as the cause of the run's `GraphExecutionError`). `chain_preprocessors()` and `chain_postprocessors()` now await `async` steps; an `async` preprocessor in a chain never ran before.
- **The LLM's `skip_to` and `retry_with` graph actions did not work.** `skip_to` to a node was overwritten by the normal transition, and `skip_to` `"__end__"` was reported as a hook stop. `retry_with` merged the context update but did not run the node again. `skip_to` now decides the next node, ahead of edges and transitions, and `retry_with` runs the node again within its iteration budget.
- **`Hook.on_error` and `Hook.on_graph_mutation` were never called.** The `Hook` protocol declared them but the engine had no call site. `on_error(node, error, state)` now runs for every failed node, before `post_node` and routing; returning a node name (or `"__end__"`) routes the run there, `None` keeps the usual handling. `on_graph_mutation(mutation, state)` is asked before each mutation (a node's `graph_mutations` and the LLM's `add_node` / `add_edge` actions); `False` or an exception blocks it.
- **An exception in `on_observable_event` or `on_human_required` ended the run.** Unlike the other hooks, these two were not wrapped. Their exceptions are now logged and the run goes on.
- **A `RetryNode` was retried twice over.** It carried the `RETRYABLE` flag, so the engine wrapped it in its own retry loop of `max_iterations` attempts: with the defaults a failing wrapped node ran up to 40 times, with backoff. The engine no longer retries a `RetryNode` (also one loaded from a saved graph with the flag), and its final error keeps the last exception as the cause.
- **`CycleDetectionHook` let the stopped node run, and ended long tool loops.** The engine ran the node a `pre_node` hook had stopped the run before, and the hook counted every tool-loop re-entry of a node as a visit, so the default ReAct graph stopped with `GraphStoppedError` after 9 model calls. The engine now respects a `pre_node` stop before the node runs, and a node's tool-loop re-entries count as one step.
- **`SubgraphNode(inherit_state=True)` dropped the sub-graph's answer.** It copied the parent's messages in but never appended the messages the sub-graph added, so a graph that ended on it returned the user's question as the last message. The new messages are now appended to the parent's conversation.
- **A string `model_override` broke `RouterNode` and `CodeActionNode`.** Only `PromptNode` resolved `model_override="openai:gpt-5-mini"`; the other two called the string (`'str' object has no attribute 'ainvoke'`). Both now resolve a string or a `Model` like `PromptNode`.
- **The standard skills refused their own settings as keywords.** `summarizer(instructions=...)`, `web_researcher(tool_choice=...)`, `formatter(default_next=...)` and the like raised `TypeError: got multiple values for keyword argument`. Every setting a skill picks is now a default the caller's keyword replaces.
- **`managed`'s tool-loop budget of 30 ran as 25, and `"react"` / `"managed"` ignored `max_agent_iterations`.** `PromptGraphEngine` capped every node at `max_node_iterations=25`, so `PromptGraph.managed()`'s 30 model calls could not be reached, and under `build_agent` the `"managed"` node kept 30 (and a `"react"` pattern by name 15) whatever `max_agent_iterations` said. The engine no longer applies a per-node ceiling unless `max_node_iterations=` is given, and `build_agent` gives the `reason` node of `"react"` and `"managed"` the run's `max_agent_iterations`, as it does for the default graph.
- **`graph_blocks` were ignored by the default graph and `node_pool`.** `build_agent(graph_blocks=[...])` only reached a pattern given by name. The blocks now also go ahead of the blocks of the default ReAct graph and of every `node_pool` node (the nodes are copied, not changed).
- **`ParallelNode(merge_strategy="concatenate")` gave the same dict as `"dict"`, and `FanOutNode` ignored `merge_strategy`.** `"concatenate"` now returns one text with a `[child_name]` section per child output, `FanOutNode` applies `"dict"`, `"concatenate"` and `"custom"` (with the new `merge_fn=`), and both put the merged output in `raw_output` too.
- **A node with an `output_schema` at the end of a graph left no answer.** Its parsed output added no `AIMessage`, so `ainvoke()` returned a conversation that ended on the user's question. When such a node ends the run, the engine now appends its output as JSON in a final `AIMessage`.
- **`CodeActionNode` dropped the provider error behind a failed model call.** It wrote `result.error` without the exception, so `GraphExecutionError.__cause__` was `None`. The exception is now kept as the cause.
- **`Skill.matches_cwd()` missed files below the first directory.** Its `fnmatch` fallback stopped after the first entry of the walk, so `paths=["*.py"]` did not match `src/app.py`. It now walks until a match, once for all patterns.
- **A `FallbackChain` with a fallback whose key was not set could not be built.** Every model in the chain was resolved when the agent was built, so `FallbackChain(["openai:gpt-5-mini", "anthropic:claude-sonnet-4-5"])` failed with `ModelSetupError` for `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` even though the primary was ready. Only the primary is resolved at build time now (a primary without its key still fails there, naming the variable); a fallback given as a string or `Model(...)` is set up, and given the agent's tools, the first time it is tried. A fallback that cannot be set up is a failed attempt like any other: the next model is tried, it counts toward that model's circuit breaker, and the "All N models failed" error lists its setup error. See "Keys and Setup" in docs/core/fallback.md.
- **`promptise agent` never used a `.superagent` file's `cache:`.** Turns ran without a `CallerContext`, and the default `per_user` cache does not cache a request without a `user_id`. The terminal has one user, so `promptise agent` and the `promptise run` chat, `-i` and TUI modes now run each turn as `CallerContext(user_id="local")` (for a Promptise agent). `promptise run --serve` requests still carry no `user_id`: the server now warns at start when the agent's cache is not `scope: shared`, since it would cache nothing. Streamed runs (`astream()`, `astream_with_tools()`, so the `promptise run` chat) do not use the cache; docs/core/cache.md now says which calls and CLI commands do.
- **`promptise init -t cross-agent` and `-t advanced` referenced files they did not create**, so `promptise validate` on the new file failed with "File not found" for `agents/*.superagent`. Both templates now also write the specialist files their `cross_agents` section names, in `agents/` next to the output file (never overwriting one without `--force`), and every template passes `promptise validate <file> --allow-missing-env` as generated.
- **Rich markup ate bracketed text in CLI output.** The `--devtools` summary said "add it to in promptise.toml" (the `[pricing]` table name was read as a style tag), `promptise run --list` lost `[scripts]` the same way, and the "Final LLM Answer" panel of `promptise agent` and `promptise run --model-id` dropped bracketed parts of the model's answer. They are now escaped.

### Changed

- The event notifier's `default_pii_sanitizer` redacts each key and string value of the payload on its own instead of running its patterns over the payload's JSON text, so a replacement can no longer break the JSON and send the payload out unredacted.
- `InMemoryProvider`, `ChromaProvider` and `Mem0Provider` default to `scope=None`, which still means `SHARED`; it is how they tell the default from an explicit `scope=MemoryScope.SHARED`. A scope passed as a string (`"per_user"`) becomes a `MemoryScope`, and an unknown one raises `ValueError`.
- `AgentRuntime.run_forever()` (and so `promptise runtime start`) waits up to `drain_timeout` seconds (5 by default) for runs in flight at shutdown instead of cancelling them; `run_forever(drain_timeout=0)` or `ProcessConfig(drain_timeout=0)` cancels at once as before. A plain `stop()`, `stop_process()`, `stop_all()` and `shutdown()` in Python still cancel at once. The control API's stop and stop-all routes, and so `promptise runtime stop --process`, now also wait up to each process's `drain_timeout` when the request names none, instead of cancelling at once; send `{"drain_timeout": 0}` or pass `--drain-timeout 0` for the old behaviour. `run_forever()`'s signal handlers now stay installed until the shutdown has finished.
- Docs: the observability page lists what the trace redaction replaces and with which placeholder; the guardrails page lists the GitHub token formats and the `BEARER` category.
- Docs: the sandbox page says exactly what `network="restricted"` allows (DNS to any server, TCP 80/443 to any host outside the link-local range, so any web server is reachable) and shows how to allow only some hosts with an egress proxy and `docker_network`; the sandbox README no longer calls `"restricted"` the default (the default is `"none"`).
- Docs: the memory page warns that the default `SHARED` scope shares every caller's memories and shows `purge_user` for a caller with a tenant.
- Docs: the guardrails page explains how the injection windows can dilute a short instruction and when to use `paragraphs=True`.
- Docs: the MCP server pages say what elicitation and `SessionState` do on handshake and 2026-07-28 connections and on stateless servers.
- Docs: the MCP client page describes the protocol versions `MCPClient` can use.
- Log, warning and exception messages no longer contain em dashes; they use a colon, semicolon, comma or parentheses instead. For example the `WebhookTrigger` warning now reads `WebhookTrigger on port 9090 has no HMAC secret: any HTTP client can trigger this webhook.` and the budget warning `Budget violation: max_irreversible_per_run (limit=3, current=4), action=pause`. Code that matches these messages by their exact text needs the new punctuation; the docs quoting them are updated.
- Docs: the processes, runtime manager and configuration pages describe the graceful stop, `drain_timeout` and what happens to a run a stop cancels.
- Docs: the triggers overview explains trusted triggers and their security trade-off; the webhook page documents the `telegram` scheme and `trusted`; the configuration page lists both.
- Docs: the advanced patterns page describes how `OpenAPIProvider` maps a spec to tools; the server guide lists Pydantic models among the tool return types.
- Docs: the advanced patterns page documents `@server.tool(version=...)` and `server.add_transform()` in place of the workarounds it described.
- Docs: the observability, resilience, authentication and deployment pages describe the logged `args`, the webhook queue and the case-insensitive API key header in place of the earlier limitations.
- Docs: the shell interpolation page says what the allowlist enforces and that command output is literal text.
- `Prompt.to_messages()`'s docstring says when it adds a `HumanMessage` (when `ctx.rendered_text` is set, not when template variables are given).
- Docs: the prompting pages describe `observe=`, the inspector's context and guard records, token counts and chained flow transitions in place of the earlier limitations.
- A `.superagent` file with `adaptive:` (not disabled) but no `memory:` section now fails validation with a message that says to add one; it used to load and build an agent whose adaptive strategy was turned off with only a log warning. `build_agent(adaptive=...)` without memory still warns.
- Docs: the `.superagent` reference lists the `optimize_tools` and `cache` mapping fields in place of the warnings that they did not work; the `identity` retry helpers' docstring says that a failed credential fetch fails the request (it said it was downgraded to a warning).

- **A `HumanNode` without a handler fails the run instead of approving.** A graph that relied on the old auto-approval (a `HumanNode` with no `_human_handler`) now raises `GraphExecutionError` at that node. Pass `build_agent(approval=...)`, `PromptGraphEngine(human_handler=...)` or `config={"_human_handler": ...}` for every graph with a `HumanNode`, or route the failure with an `on_error` edge.
- `PromptNode(temperature=..., max_tokens=...)` default to `None` (the model's own settings) instead of `0.0` and `4096`, which were never applied; a saved graph with explicit values now applies them. `tool_choice` is applied as described in Fixed.
- `NodeResult.output` of a `PromptNode` is the reply text for a plain-text reply (it was `None`), so `output_key` gets the text and an edge condition written as `(r.output or {}).get(...)` now gets a string; write `(r.output if isinstance(r.output, dict) else {}).get(...)`. Postprocessors no longer run on steps that only call tools.
- A `PromptNode` preprocessor that raises fails the node instead of being logged; postprocessor exceptions are still logged and the output kept.
- Reasoning nodes without an `output_schema` add a short instruction to their system prompt asking for their fields as a JSON object (the `deliberate` pattern's `observe` stage among them).
- `ParallelNode`'s default `merge_strategy` is `"dict"` (what the old default `"concatenate"` did); an unknown strategy, or `"custom"` without a `merge_fn`, raises `ValueError` for `ParallelNode` and `FanOutNode`.
- `RetryNode` no longer carries the `RETRYABLE` flag.
- `PromptGraphEngine(max_node_iterations=...)` defaults to `None`: each node's own `max_iterations` applies. Pass a number for an engine-wide ceiling. `build_agent` no longer sets one.
- `CycleDetectionHook` ends the run before the node that would complete the repeat, and counts a node's tool-loop re-entries as one step.
- Docs: the Reasoning Graph pages (nodes, edges, flags, hooks, processors, skills, prebuilts, internals and the engine overview), the skill registry, approval, building agents, reasoning patterns, custom reasoning and context lifecycle pages describe the behaviour above instead of the old gaps.
- `promptise init` templates use `openai:gpt-5-mini` (they used `openai:gpt-4.1`), and the `advanced` template no longer sets `temperature`, which gpt-5 models do not take; the CLI's help examples use `openai:gpt-5-mini` too. The "Next steps" it prints include `promptise validate <file>`.

## v1.6.0 — 2026-10-21

Python functions are tools: `from promptise import tool` declares one, with its schema taken from the type hints and the docstring and no `langchain_core` import. `MCPClient` reuses its HTTP connections over Streamable HTTP instead of opening one per request, `promptise runtime start --detach` on Windows no longer loses track of a runtime that has started, a `promptise run --tui` run ends after its program's last output line, and every page of docs.promptise.com has a title and description that say what it covers.

### Added

- **Native tools: `@tool`** -- `from promptise import tool` makes a Python function a tool for `build_agent(extra_tools=[...])`, without importing `langchain_core`:

  ```python
  from promptise import CallerContext, ToolError, build_agent, tool

  @tool
  async def get_order(order_id: str, caller: CallerContext) -> dict:
      """Look up an order by its ID.

      Args:
          order_id: The order ID, for example "A-1001".
      """
      order = await orders.get(order_id, owner=caller.user_id)
      if order is None:
          raise ToolError(f"No order {order_id}.")
      return order

  agent = await build_agent(model="openai:gpt-5-mini", extra_tools=[get_order])
  ```

  The tool's name is the function's name, its description the docstring without the parameter section, and its parameter schema comes from the type hints (defaults, `Optional`, `Literal`, enums, lists, dicts, nested Pydantic models, `Field` constraints), with each parameter described by `Annotated[..., Field(description=...)]`, `Annotated[..., "text"]` or the docstring (Google `Args:`, NumPy `Parameters` or Sphinx `:param:`). It is the schema `@server.tool()` builds for the same function, and for a simple function the one LangChain's `@tool` builds, so switching the import changes nothing the model sees. Arguments are validated before the function runs; invalid ones reach the model as a tool error that lists what to correct. A `CallerContext` parameter receives the caller of `ainvoke(..., caller=...)` and is hidden from the model. Raising `ToolError` (now also importable as `promptise.ToolError`) sends the model the error envelope an MCP server sends, and `timeout=` answers a call that runs longer with a retryable `TIMEOUT` error. Sync functions run in a worker thread, so they never block the event loop. Results reach the model as text: a string as it is, `None` as an empty result, anything else (dicts, lists, Pydantic models, dataclasses) as JSON. The tool is a LangChain `BaseTool` (`promptise.function_tools.FunctionTool`), so approval policies, guardrails, tracing, events, tool optimization, cross-agent delegation and code-action treat it like any other tool, and it stays callable like the function for unit tests (`await get_order("A-1001")`). `extra_tools` also accepts plain functions (made tools as if decorated) and still takes LangChain tools; anything else raises `TypeError` before a server is connected. See docs/core/tools.md.

### Fixed

- **`AgentProcess.status()["queue_size"]` left out events a worker was holding** (since 1.5.0). A worker takes the next event off the queue at once and holds it while the process is starting or suspended, or until a concurrency slot frees up, so a suspended process with two waiting events reported `queue_size: 1`, and `promptise runtime status` and the dashboard showed too few. Held events now count as queued until their run starts, and they also keep the process from idling out.
- **A `promptise run --tui` run could say it had exited before its program's last lines.** `ChildRunSource` gets the child's run events over its IPC channel and its output over the stdout/stderr pipes, and forwarded both as they arrived. The child publishes `state: exited` and `run.exit` just before it exits, so on a busy machine the stream said the run had ended before its last printed lines, and every run had two `exited` states. It now holds the child's `run.exit`, reads both pipes to the end once the process has ended, waits for the channel to deliver `run.exit` or close (each wait at most 2 s, as before), then publishes `run.exit` and a single `exited`. A restart ends the old run the same way, so the old run's last lines no longer show up after the new run started.
- **`MCPClient` opened a new TCP connection for every Streamable HTTP request** (with mcp 1.x and 2.x). The MCP SDK closes the event stream that answers a request as soon as it has the JSON-RPC response, before the end of the stream has been read, and an HTTP/1.1 connection closed in the middle of a response can't be reused. Each closed connection kept a local port in `TIME_WAIT`, so an agent calling a server many times ran out of ports (`Can't assign requested address`, seen on macOS). The client now reads the rest of that stream when it is closed (at most 1 s and 64 KiB; the server ends it right after the response), so the connection goes back to the pool: in a local test, 50 calls in a row opened 3 connections (the handshake, the session's event stream and one for the calls) instead of 54. Per-caller sessions keep connections of their own, so a connection never carries two callers' tokens. The SSE transport already reused its connections.
- **`promptise runtime start --detach` on Windows could report "still starting after 60s" for a runtime that had started.** While it waited, the command read the runtime's state file every 0.2 s. On Windows that read fails while the runtime replaces the file to record that it is ready, and the command took the unreadable file for a stale one and deleted it: the runtime kept running without a state file, so `status` and `stop` could not find it. `status`, `stop` and the other commands could delete it the same way, and a second `start` with the same name could take the name over in that moment. A state file that can't be read is now read again (for up to 1 s on Windows) and never removed; only a file whose process is gone, or that is not a valid state file, is stale.

### Changed

- A run stopped by a limit with more than one tool call pending reports them as "calls to `a` and `b` whose results the model never saw" (it said "a call to a, b whose result").
- **MCP server: more parameter descriptions.** `@server.tool()` (and `@server.prompt()`) also read NumPy-style `Parameters` sections, and `@server.tool()` reads a plain string in `Annotated[...]` (`Annotated[str, "The order ID."]`) as the parameter's description, as `@tool` does. A `Field` description still wins over both, and the `Annotated` string over the docstring.
- Docs: the Tools page starts with declaring tools with `@tool`, and the Building Agents, RAG, code-action and projects pages, three blog posts, the `promptise init --project` template and the examples use `from promptise import tool`.
- Docs: the MCP client page describes how a client uses its HTTP connections, and the runtime CLI page says that a state file which can't be read is left alone.
- Docs: every page on docs.promptise.com has a title that names what it covers ("Agent Memory: Vector Search, Chroma and Mem0" rather than "Memory"), followed by "| Promptise Docs", and a description; generic page headings are more specific.
- Docs: the `InjectionDetector` page no longer says it detects jailbreaks. Its default model's card says it handles English only and does not detect jailbreak attacks, and the model's project is archived; the page says so and how to pass another classifier.
- Development: `ruff` may be up to 0.17 and `pymdown-extensions` up to 12.2 in the `dev`/`docs` extras.


## v1.5.2 — 2026-10-21

`MCPClient` no longer reports a server's own error as a lost connection (a regression in 1.5.1), a stdio server answers the requests still running when its client closes stdin, `get_node_status()` reports an unreachable node as `NodeUnreachableError`, and an event or message trigger dead-letters every event its full buffer drops, also before its listener runs.

### Fixed
