- **`MCPClient` reported a server's JSON-RPC error with code -32000 as a lost connection** (new in 1.5.1, with mcp 1.x and 2.x). The client took every -32000 error for the SDK's "Connection closed", but -32000 is also the generic server-error code, and a Promptise server answers a `ToolError` raised in a resource or prompt handler with it. Reading a missing resource raised `Connection to ... was lost (MCPError: There is no handbook page 'holidays'.)` although the connection was fine and later calls worked. Only the SDK's own error (code -32000, message `Connection closed`, no data) and a closed stream count as a lost connection now; a server's error is reported as such again, as in 1.5.0: `Failed to read resource 'docs://pages/holidays': There is no handbook page 'holidays'.`
- **A stdio server exited without answering a request when the client closed stdin right after sending it** (a shell pipe, a one-shot script; with mcp 1.x the `tools/call` answer was almost always missing, with 2.x sometimes). Both SDKs cancel the requests still running when the read side ends. The server now keeps the session open after stdin's EOF until those requests are answered and written to stdout, waiting at most `shutdown_timeout` seconds (30 by default), and stops waiting at once for a request that waits on the client (an elicitation).
- **`RuntimeCoordinator.get_node_status()` raised a plain `RuntimeError` for an unreachable node**, so failover code catching `NodeUnreachableError` missed it. It raises `NodeUnreachableError` (a `RuntimeError` subclass, with the `node_id`), as documented; `except RuntimeError` still catches it, and the message still starts with `Status request failed:`. `start_process_on_node()`, `stop_process_on_node()` and `inject_event_on_node()` already raised it.
- **Events an event or message trigger dropped before its listener ran were counted but not dead-lettered.** The overflow handler was only set when the trigger's listener started, so a burst (or a bus that replays a backlog on subscribe) that filled the 100-event buffer during `start()`, `add_trigger()` or an agent's `add_trigger` was only counted in `dropped_events`. The handler is now set when the trigger is created, so every dropped event is dead-lettered with reason `"trigger buffer full"`.

### Changed

- Docs: the event, message and webhook trigger page no longer says a full event or message buffer drops events "with a warning"; it describes the 1.5.1 behavior (counted in `dropped_events` and dead-lettered). The triggers overview lists the `trigger buffer full` dead-letter reason and the `set_overflow_handler()` member of custom triggers, and the coordinator page says which errors `get_node_status()` raises. The MCP deployment and building-servers pages describe what a stdio server does when stdin closes, and the client page says a server's error is not a lost connection.

## v1.5.1 — 2026-10-21

Promptise now works with the MCP Python SDK 2.x as well as 1.x, the SPIFFE extra installs, sandboxed code no longer runs as root, PII patterns stop redacting parts of numbers, and a set of runtime fixes, among them a stop() race that could run a finished run again.

### Upgrade notes

- **The MCP SDK 2.x is supported, and a new install gets it.** The requirement is `mcp>=1.9,<3` (was `<2`), so Promptise installs next to packages that need the 2.x SDK, and 2.x clients reach a Promptise server with the 2026-07-28 protocol revision. Promptise's APIs are unchanged. Code of your own that reads MCP SDK objects (the `Tool`, `CallToolResult`... that `MCPClient` and `TestClient` return) follows the SDK: 2.x has snake_case attributes (`tool.input_schema`, `result.is_error`). Pin `"mcp>=1.9,<2"` to keep the 1.x SDK. `pydantic-settings` is now a direct dependency (the 1.x SDK installed it, 2.x doesn't).
- **On a 2026-07-28 connection the server can't send requests to the client**, so elicitation (including `ElicitationApprover`) and sampling are declined there, and gated calls fail closed. Promptise's `MCPClient` always uses the handshake, where they work; with the 2.x SDK's own client use `Client(..., mode="legacy")`.
- **Sandboxed code runs as `nobody`.** `SandboxConfig.user` (also in a `.superagent` `sandbox:` section) defaults to `"65534:65534"`; before, the container ran as the image's default user, root in `python:3.11-slim`. The workspace (`HOME`) and `/tmp` stay writable and `pip install` still installs into the workspace. Set `user="root"` for the old behavior, or the image's user name for a custom image.
- **`promptise[identity-spiffe]` depends on `spiffe`** (py-spiffe), the package that exists on PyPI. 1.5.0's `pyspiffe>=1.0` could not be installed, so `[identity-spiffe]` and `[identity-all]` were unusable.
- **PII redaction changed for some inputs.** A number pattern now matches a whole number or nothing (an order number `2024-118-5521` is no longer redacted as a phone number, a 14-digit value after `NPI` no longer as an NPI); a spaced IBAN is redacted whole as `[IBAN]` (it was partly redacted as an Aadhaar number); contextual bank accounts take up to 34 digits.
- **Some trigger IDs change once.** A trigger whose ID hashed a function `filter_expression` (its ID changed on every start anyway), or a `custom_config` value with a credential-like key or a URL with user info, gets a new stable ID: those values are now masked before hashing, as documented.
- **`promptise runtime start` has an in-process event bus.** `event` triggers and escalations with an `event_type` now work between the processes of one CLI runtime (an `event` trigger could not start before).
- **A run refused by `max_runs_per_day` is dead-lettered** (reason `"budget: max_runs_per_day"`) and its `task_completed` outcome is `"refused"`; it reported `"success"` and was lost.
- **An unset `${VAR}` in a manifest raises `ManifestEnvVarError`**, which is both a `ManifestError` and the `EnvVarNotFoundError` raised before.
- **An `async def` `filter_expression` is rejected** when the `TriggerConfig` is built; it was never awaited, so every event passed.

### Fixed

- **mcp 2.x: `import promptise` failed** (`McpError` is `MCPError`) and the server and client could not run on it. A compatibility layer (`promptise.mcp._compat`, `promptise.mcp.server._sdk`) registers the server's handlers on either SDK's lowlevel server, reads protocol fields in either spelling, hands the SDK `httpx2` objects with 2.x, keeps HTTP 401/403/404 statuses that 2.x no longer raises (so `MCPConnectionRejectedError` and session re-initialisation behave as with 1.x), reports a failed `bearer_token_provider` as `MCPCredentialError`, and keys `list_changed` notifications and `SessionState` on the connection (2.x builds a new session object per request). The 1.x code paths are unchanged.
- **SPIFFE SDK mode never worked** -- it imported `pyspiffe.workloadapi...` and called `WorkloadApiClient(spiffe_socket_path=...)` / `fetch_jwt_svid(audiences=...)`. It now uses `spiffe.WorkloadApiClient(socket_path=...)`, `fetch_jwt_svid(audience=...)` and `JwtSvid.token`, also for `AgentIdentity.auto()` with `SPIFFE_ENDPOINT_SOCKET` set; a missing or invalid socket raises `CredentialAcquisitionError`.
- **PII: `phone_us` matched the last ten digits of longer numbers** (`2024-118-5521` became `2[PHONE]`); spaced IBANs were read as Aadhaar / My Number and partly redacted (an IBAN is now detected in groups of four, checked with the mod-97 checksum, and findings inside it are dropped); and `exp 12/2025` became `[CARD_EXPIRY]25` -- EIN, NPI, BSN, DEA, Medicare, NHS, NY licence, passport, PAN, VIN, SWIFT, DOB, IPv4, MRN, SSN, ITIN, SIN, RRN and NDC patterns had the same boundary problem.
- **`stop()` during a finished run's `task_completed` hook dead-lettered the event** ("process stopped", "run cancelled"), and `redeliver_dead_letters()` then ran the finished run again, repeating its tool calls. The event is released before the hooks run; only events whose run did not finish are dead-lettered.
- **`redeliver_dead_letters()` didn't wake an idle-suspended process**, so redelivered events never ran.
- **Trigger IDs with a function filter changed on every start** (they hashed `repr()`, which has a memory address); they hash the function's module and name.
- **Event and message triggers dropped events silently** when their 100-event buffer was full. They count them (`dropped_events` in `status()["triggers"]`) and the process dead-letters them (`"trigger buffer full"`).
- **`promptise runtime start` could not use custom trigger types** (validate passed, start failed with "Unknown trigger type"). `start` and `validate` take `--import MODULE` to import the module that registers them, and both report an unknown type before anything starts.
- **`promptise runtime validate` printed a traceback for an unset `${VAR}`** and, for a directory, stopped at that file; it reports the file and goes on. `promptise runtime start` printed a traceback too (for a directory, after "All processes stopped"); it now checks every manifest before starting anything.
- **An approval-denied call used up `max_irreversible_per_run`** (counted when the call starts, before the gate asks). A denied call gives its irreversible action back; it still counts as a tool call and against the cost budget.
- **An unreachable cluster node raised `aiohttp` or timeout errors** from remote operations; they raise `NodeUnreachableError`, a `RuntimeError`, as documented.
- **The `low_confidence` mission escalation had no `process_name`**, unlike the budget and health payloads.
- **A lost connection could be reported as `Failed to call tool 'x': `** (an empty reason), depending on whether the request or the session noticed first; it is reported as `Connection to ... was lost (...)` on both SDKs. This also fixes a flaky test (`test_auto_reconnect_off_reports_the_lost_session`).
- **Dashboard:** the Processes tab hid Name and Model below about 160 columns (it now drops the least important columns first); the Logs tab's log panel pushed the journal panel off the screen; the header's Events counter showed runs; the Events tab's Payload column was always `-` (a new `payload_summary` in the `task_created` hook data fills it).
- **`GET /triggers` showed the password of a URL in `custom_config`** (`scheme://user:pass@host`); URL user info is masked.
- **Messages:** `COMMANDS_NOT_ALLOWED` suggested callback hooks, which a JSON deploy can't carry; `rewind --plan` said "N of M entries after it" with N counting the target; the escalation warning without an event bus now says how to fix it; a refused cluster node was logged with doubled quotes; and every budget refusal logged LangChain's "Error in BudgetGuard.on_tool_start callback" warning (the guard now logs the refusal at INFO).
- **Manifests:** unknown top-level, `config:` and `memory:` keys get "did you mean" hints like the other sections.

### Changed

- New: `SandboxConfig.user`; `promptise.runtime.InProcessEventBus`; `promptise.runtime.ManifestEnvVarError`; `promptise.runtime.distributed.NodeUnreachableError`; `promptise.approval.on_approval_denied`; `--import` for `promptise runtime start` and `validate`.
- CI: a `test-mcp-v1` job runs the MCP tests and mypy with `mcp<2`; the main matrix resolves to the 2.x SDK.
- Docs: installation (MCP SDK versions, identity extras), SPIFFE provider, sandbox user, MCP server SDK compatibility, triggers, processes, hooks, budget, coordinator and CLI pages describe the changes above.
- Docs corrected where the code was right: a run refused by `max_runs_per_day` does emit `budget.exceeded` (budget.md); every journal entry has an `entry_id`, so `secret_access` and `mission_evaluation` entries can be rewind targets (journal index, rewind.md); the rewind worked example's undone count matches its plan and the resume comes before the checkpoint; manifests' triggers are fully validated at load and `get_secret` exists only in open mode (manifests.md); hook `env` values stay `${VAR}` until the hook runs (hooks.md); the dashboard collector fills the Events, Recent Activity and journal panels, and its trigger IDs are the stable ones (dashboard.md).
- The `purge_user` docstrings (cache, memory, observability) describe deleting one user's data on request instead of naming a law (docs only).

---

## v1.5.0 — 2026-10-20

The Agent Runtime now does what its documentation says: unwired features (lifecycle hooks, rewind, distributed mode, the `promptise runtime stop | status | restart` commands) work, and the runtime docs were rewritten against the code.

### Upgrade notes

These changes can alter the behavior of existing code or deployments:

- **Control API errors no longer quote exceptions.** A failure caused by an exception inside the runtime (deploy, start, stop, restart, suspend, resume, secret rotation, inbox refusals, rewind, unexpected errors) answers with a fixed `message` and an `error_id`; the exception and its traceback are logged on `promptise.runtime.api` under that id. Pydantic validation errors still list each field. Code that parsed the exception text out of `message` should use the `code` (and `OrchestrationAPIError.error_id` to find the log entry).
- **Unknown keys are reported.** Manifests reject unknown keys in every section (with the dotted path and the valid keys), and a `servers:` entry that is neither an HTTP (`url`) nor a stdio (`command`) server is an error instead of being skipped. In Python, `OpenModeConfig`, `JournalConfig`, `ContextConfig`, `BudgetConfig`, `HealthConfig`, `MissionConfig`, `SecretScopeConfig`, `EscalationTarget`, `ToolCostAnnotation` and `DistributedConfig` still ignore unknown keys but emit a `FutureWarning` naming them; a future release will reject them. A `TriggerConfig` option that belongs to another trigger type (`watch_path` on a `cron` trigger) is now an error.
- **Journal recovery is on by default.** With a journal configured, a process's first `start()` restores the context state, mission, budget counters, conversation and invocation count from the last checkpoint (`JournalConfig.recover_on_start=True`). A clean `stop()` writes a checkpoint too. Set `recover_on_start=False` to always start fresh.
- **Conversation and memory survive `stop()` / `start()`.** `stop()` no longer clears the conversation history or closes the memory provider; `close()` does. Set `ContextConfig.conversation_clear_on_stop=True` for the old behavior.
- **`SecretScopeConfig.revoke_on_stop` is honored.** With `revoke_on_stop=False`, secrets now survive a stop (every stop revoked them before). `secrets` values are `SecretStr`; `${VAR}` references are resolved when the process starts, not when the config is built, and literal values are masked in `repr()` and JSON dumps.
- **Health `on_anomaly="escalate"` always suspends the process**, and sends the notification only when `escalation` is set (without a target it only logged before).
- **Inbox messages are scanned and consumed.** `InboxConfig.scan_with_guardrails=True` (the default) is now applied: flagged messages are refused (`422 MESSAGE_REJECTED` over the API). Directives and context messages are removed after the run that saw them instead of repeating in every run until their TTL; answered questions are removed.
- **Control API error codes.** Deploying a name that exists is `409 PROCESS_EXISTS` (was `409 DEPLOY_FAILED`); `DEPLOY_FAILED` is now `422` for a valid config that could not be created. Rotating an unknown secret is `404 SECRET_NOT_FOUND` (was `404 PROCESS_NOT_FOUND`). An oversized request body is `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, an unexpected error `500 INTERNAL_ERROR`. See the error-code table in docs/runtime/api.md.
- **API deploys can't run commands by default.** A deploy whose config contains stdio (command) MCP servers, command hooks, or open mode with `open_mode.allow_tool_creation` (the open-mode default) is refused with `403 COMMANDS_NOT_ALLOWED` unless the API is created with `OrchestrationAPI(allow_command_deploys=True)` (`promptise runtime start --allow-command-deploys`). Deploy open-mode agents with `"open_mode": {"allow_tool_creation": false}`.
- **API deploys can't use `${VAR}` references by default.** A deploy, `POST .../triggers` or `PATCH` of `budget` / `health` / `mission` whose body contains a `${VAR}` reference (outside `instructions`) answers `403 ENV_REFERENCES_NOT_ALLOWED`. Allow specific variables with `OrchestrationAPI(allowed_env_vars={...})` (`--allow-env-var NAME`, repeatable) or all of them with `allow_env_references=True` (`--allow-env-references`). An API-deployed process's secrets resolve only those variables.
- **API-chosen paths stay in a data directory.** `journal.path` (file journal), `context.memory_persist_directory` and file-watch `watch_path` values in deploys and `POST .../triggers` must lie inside `OrchestrationAPI(data_dir=...)` (default `./.promptise`); others, and paths with `..` or `~`, answer `403 PATH_NOT_ALLOWED`. The deployed agent's own `add_trigger` watch paths are held to it too.
- **Agent-connected MCP servers are checked and get no credentials.** With an empty `open_mode.allowed_mcp_urls`, `connect_mcp_server` refuses hosts that resolve to loopback, private, link-local or other internal addresses (list such URLs in `allowed_mcp_urls` to allow them), and a process deployed through the API may connect no server at all. Agent-connected servers no longer receive the process identity's credential or a caller's token; list URLs that should in the new `open_mode.identity_mcp_urls`. An open-mode process that may connect any server logs a warning at start.
- **Agent-added and spawned webhooks listen on loopback.** A webhook trigger an open-mode agent adds, or passes to `spawn_process`, with a non-loopback `webhook_host` is refused unless the new `open_mode.allow_public_webhooks` is on (and then it needs an `hmac_secret`).
- **Unsigned loopback webhooks refuse browser requests.** A webhook trigger without `hmac_secret` on a loopback address answers `421` for a non-loopback `Host` header and `403` for a cross-site `Origin`, like the control API. Local clients that send neither are unaffected.
- **Spawned processes inherit the parent's hooks.** The parent's `hooks` manager is dispatched for every process it spawns (after the child's own hooks), so its `permission_request` and other hooks apply to children.
- **`ContextConfig(env_prefix="")` is refused** (an empty prefix exposed the whole environment).
- **`promptise runtime start` removes `PROMPTISE_RUNTIME_TOKEN` from its environment** after reading it, so processes, hooks and stdio MCP servers it starts no longer inherit the control API token.
- **`POST .../start` and `.../restart` of a process whose start raised a `KeyError`** (such as a missing secret) answer `500 START_FAILED` / `RESTART_FAILED` instead of `404 PROCESS_NOT_FOUND`.
- **Trigger IDs are stable.** Config triggers get IDs of the form `<type>-<digest>` instead of position-based `static_<n>`. The old IDs are still accepted by the API trigger routes, with a deprecation warning in the response.
- **`DEFAULT_PRODUCTION_CONFIG` no longer sets `distributed.enabled=True`.** The flag did nothing before; it now starts a network transport, so the preset leaves it off and is identical to `DEFAULT_DEVELOPMENT_CONFIG`. `DistributedConfig(discovery_method="multicast")` is rejected (it was never implemented).
- **`ShellHook` gets a minimal environment** (no inherited credentials) unless `inherit_env=True` or `env_passthrough` is set, and runs without a shell.
- **The budget daily reset honors `daily_reset_hour_utc`**; the counters no longer also reset at midnight UTC.
- **`StateError` subclasses `ProcessStateError`** (it is still a `ValueError`), so all three `except` clauses catch an invalid transition.
- **`Mem0Provider.close()` keeps stored memories.** It called Mem0's `Memory.reset()`, which deleted every memory.

### Fixed

- **`AgentProcess.stop()` could hang on Python 3.10 and 3.11 when a hook was running** -- hook timeouts used `asyncio.wait_for`, which before Python 3.12 can swallow the caller's cancellation, so a worker cancelled by `stop()` kept running. Hook timeouts no longer use it, and `stop()` cancels workers, listeners and the heartbeat until they have really ended.
- **`AgentProcess.stop()` could hang forever after an open-mode reload during a run** -- the MCP client a hot reload replaces is closed once the run that asked for the reload ends, by the worker that ran it. When `stop()` (or `AgentRuntime.stop_all()`) cancelled that worker during the close, `MCPMultiClient` swallowed the `CancelledError`, the worker went back to waiting for events, and `stop()` waited for it forever. `MCPMultiClient.__aexit__` now still closes every client when it is cancelled, then re-raises the cancellation.
- **`ClusterNode.stop()` swallowed its own cancellation** -- it awaited its background tasks under `suppress(CancelledError)`, so cancelling a stop that was waiting for them was ignored. It now waits with `gather(..., return_exceptions=True)`; the foreground `promptise runtime start` shutdown waits for its signal waiter the same way.
- **`PostgresConversationStore` failed on first use** -- the `sessions` table DDL had an unescaped `DEFAULT '{}'`, so `str.format()` raised `IndexError` while creating the tables and every Postgres-backed conversation store failed on its first call. The default is now escaped like the `messages` table's.
- **`retry(prompt, max_retries=-1)` raised `TypeError: exceptions must derive from BaseException`** -- it ran no attempt and then raised `None`. A negative `max_retries` now raises `ValueError` when the retry is built, and `_Fallback` refuses an empty prompt list the same way. Retries and fallbacks re-raise the last error as before.
- **Restarting, starting or removing a `FAILED` process left its old tasks running** -- the old trigger, listener and heartbeat kept going, so events fired twice and dead-lettering continued after removal (`AgentRuntime.restart_process()`, `start_all()`, `remove_process()` and `POST /restart`). They are now torn down first.
- **`stop()` from `CREATED` raised `StateError` and from `FAILED` never reached `STOPPED`** -- both now stop cleanly and emit `process.stopped`.
- **Queued trigger events vanished on `stop()`** -- they are dead-lettered (`"process stopped"`) instead.
- **An idle-suspended process never woke up** -- with `idle_timeout`, a cron or webhook agent stayed suspended forever while ticks piled up. The next trigger event now resumes it (`process.resumed` with `reason="trigger"`).
- **`resume()` didn't reset the idle clock**, so the process was suspended again at the next heartbeat; and events queued while suspended were reordered. Both fixed.
- **`process.failed` had an empty `error` after repeated failures** -- it carries the real error now.
- **Webhook bodies over 1 MiB got `500`** -- they get `413` (checked against `Content-Length` and while reading chunked bodies). A request that fails with `500` or `503` releases its replay key, so the sender's retry is accepted.
- **File-watch events were dropped when the process was busy** -- events now wait (changes to a waiting file are merged); only when `watch_max_pending_events` different files are waiting is a new one dropped, with an error log and a count in `FileWatchTrigger.dropped_events`. Polling compares the size as well as the mtime.
- **Health: raising `error_window` (or other window sizes) on a running monitor silently disabled the check** -- windows are resized live, keeping the recorded data. `clear_anomalies()` (and `DELETE /health/anomalies`) now resets the cooldowns, and the anomaly history is bounded (`HealthMonitor.MAX_ANOMALIES`, 1000). `on_anomaly="stop"` is accepted by `HealthConfig` (it was unreachable).
- **Mission: `success_check` could not be used from a process** -- `MissionConfig.success_check` passes it to the tracker. A completed or failed mission no longer becomes active again after a restart (it is restored from the journal). `check_mission` returns the evaluation history, not only the last one. `POST /mission/fail` stores the reason and emits `mission.failed`.
- **Secrets: `get_secret` results were written to a `level="full"` journal in plain text** (see Security). Rotating an unknown secret returned `PROCESS_NOT_FOUND`; the client's `rotate_secret(ttl=)` was ignored by the server.
- **Inbox: eviction dropped the newest lowest-priority message** and critical messages could push the inbox past `max_messages`. It now evicts the oldest lowest-priority message (answered questions first) and refuses a message (`InboxFullError`, `409 INBOX_FULL`) when nothing can be evicted for it. `POST /messages` checks the process state like `send_message()` (`409 PROCESS_NOT_RUNNING`). An `/ask` call whose question is removed by `DELETE /inbox` or evicted from a full inbox answers `410 QUESTION_REMOVED` (it ended with a closed connection or `500`); `process.ask()` raises `InboxQuestionRemoved` or `InboxFullError`.
- **Context: `PATCH /context` wrote the allowed keys before answering `403`** -- it is atomic now: nothing is written when any key is refused. Context updates are journaled (`context_update` entries) and replayed on recovery.
- **Manifests: `context: initial_state:` was silently dropped** -- every `context:` key now sets the `ContextConfig` field of the same name (`world:` still sets `initial_state`).
- **API: `PATCH` of `budget`, `health` or `mission` with `enabled: true` answered `200` and did nothing** -- the subsystem is now built (or torn down) on the running process (`AgentProcess.apply_governance_config()`). Trigger routes were config-only: an added trigger started only after a restart and a deleted one kept firing. They now start and stop the trigger on a running process.
- **`OrchestrationClient.remove_process()` and `clear_inbox()` raised `JSONDecodeError` after succeeding** -- `204` responses are handled.
- **Memory providers broke after a restart** (`ChromaProvider is closed`) -- the provider is created once and closed only by `close()`.
- **Budget counters reset on every restart** -- they are checkpointed in the journal and restored, so a restart can't reset the daily limits.
- **`promptise runtime stop`, `status` and `restart` were placeholders, and `--detach` just exited** -- see Added. `promptise runtime start` also shuts down cleanly on `SIGTERM` (`docker stop` used to skip `stop_all()`).
- **Escalation failures were silent** -- a webhook answering with an HTTP error status, a failed delivery, an `event_type` without an event bus and a target with nothing to deliver to are now logged as warnings (they were debug logs or nothing).

### Added

- **Lifecycle hooks are dispatched** -- `AgentProcess` dispatches all 13 `HookEvent`s. Declare them with `ProcessConfig.hooks` (`HookConfig`: an async `callback`, or a `command` run by `ShellHook`), in a manifest's `hooks:` section (commands only), or with `process.hooks.on(...)`. Hooks have `priority`, `once`, `timeout` (5 s for a command, 30 s for a callback), `fail_closed` and `tag`; blockable events can be refused with `HookBlocked`, and a hook can return data (for example a rewritten prompt). `ShellHook` passes the event as JSON on stdin, runs without a shell, gets a minimal environment plus `env` / `env_passthrough`, and is killed beyond `max_output_bytes`. See docs/runtime/hooks.md.
- **Rewind** -- `AgentProcess.rewind_plan(entry_id, mode)` and `rewind(...)` restore the conversation, the context state or both to a journal entry (modes `both`, `conversation_only`, `code_only`, `summarize`), emitting `process.rewound`. API: `GET /api/v1/processes/{name}/rewind/plan` and `POST /api/v1/processes/{name}/rewind`; client: `rewind_plan()` / `rewind()`; CLI: `promptise runtime rewind <process> [<entry_id>] [--mode ...] [--plan]`. See docs/runtime/journal/rewind.md.
- **Distributed mode** -- `DistributedConfig` is read: with `enabled=True`, `AgentRuntime.start_all()` / `run_forever()` start a `ClusterNode` that runs the management transport and either hosts the coordinator (`coordinator=True`, with `discovery_method="registry"` or `"static"` and `static_nodes`) or registers with one (`coordinator_url`), re-registering every `heartbeat_interval`. New fields: `coordinator`, `node_id`, `transport_host` (default loopback), `advertise_url`, `auth_token` (one cluster token, required off loopback unless `allow_unauthenticated=True`), `static_nodes`, `node_timeout`. See docs/runtime/distributed/.
- **`promptise runtime` control** -- `start` serves the control API on loopback with a random token and records the runtime in a private state directory (`~/.promptise/runtime`, or `$PROMPTISE_RUNTIME_STATE_DIR`; `0700`/`0600`, ownership checked). `status`, `stop` (graceful, then `SIGTERM`, `--force`, `--timeout`) and `restart` find it there or use `--url` / `--token` (`$PROMPTISE_RUNTIME_TOKEN`) for a remote API, and `-p/--process` acts on one process. `start --detach` runs the runtime in the background with a log file; `start` has `--runtime-name`, `--api-host` and `--api-port`. `logs` shows the newest entries with their entry IDs, and `--follow` works.
- **Process API** -- `add_trigger()` / `remove_trigger()` on a running process, `apply_governance_config(section)`, `fail_mission(reason)`, `record_operator_action()`, `suspend_reason`, `recovered`, and `status()` now includes the health summary and file-watch `pending_events` / `dropped_events`.
- **New events** -- `process.suspended` (`reason`: `manual`, `idle`, `budget` or `health`), `process.resumed` (`reason`, `suspended_for`), `process.restart_exhausted` and `process.rewound`.
- **`AgentRuntime.run_forever()`** -- starts every process and serves until `SIGTERM` / `SIGINT` (or `request_shutdown()`), then shuts down cleanly. `AgentRuntime(config)` now registers the processes in `config.processes` (only `from_config()` did).
- **Webhook trigger** -- `TriggerConfig.webhook_max_body_bytes` (default 1 MiB).
- **Security settings** -- `OrchestrationAPI(allow_env_references=, allowed_env_vars=, data_dir=)` and `promptise runtime start --allow-env-references` / `--allow-env-var NAME` (passed on to `--detach` runtimes); `OpenModeConfig.identity_mcp_urls` and `allow_public_webhooks`; `DistributedConfig.allowed_node_hosts` and `RuntimeTransport(allowed_node_hosts=)`; `AgentRuntime.add_process(deploy_policy=)` and `SecretScope(source=, env_references=)`; `SecretResolutionError` (a `KeyError`). New API error codes `ENV_REFERENCES_NOT_ALLOWED` and `PATH_NOT_ALLOWED` (both `403`).
- **Budget** -- `BudgetConfig.warning_threshold` (default `0.8`): the fraction of a limit at which `budget.warning` is emitted (it was fixed at 80%).
- **File-watch trigger** -- `watch_backend` (`auto`, `watchdog` or `polling`), `watch_recursive` (default `True`), `watch_poll_interval` (default 1 s) and `watch_max_pending_events` (default 10,000).
- **Health** -- `HealthMonitor.apply_config()`, `clear_anomalies()` returning the number cleared, and `health_status()` (public, JSON-safe).
- **Inbox** -- refusal exceptions `InvalidInboxMessage`, `InboxRateLimitError`, `InboxFullError`, `InboxMessageRejected` and `InboxQuestionRemoved` (all `ValueError` subclasses), `MessageInbox(scanner=...)`, `remove_consumed()`, `prompt_questions()`, `parse_answers()` and `scan_inbox_message()`.
- **Mission** -- `MissionTracker.fail(reason, source=)`, `restore()`, final `completed` / `failed` states and an evaluation history.
- **Secrets** -- `SecretScope.rotate(name, value, ttl=)`, `list_metadata()` (name, active, source, TTL and expiry; never the value), `sanitize()`, and `SecretNotFoundError` (a `KeyError`).
- **Context** -- `ContextConfig.state_tool` gives the agent an `update_state(key, value)` tool (off by default; limited to `writable_keys` when set) and `ContextConfig.conversation_clear_on_stop`.
- **`JournalConfig.recover_on_start`** (default `True`) and `RedactingJournal`.
- **Control API** -- `OrchestrationAPI(on_shutdown=)` adds `POST /api/v1/runtime/shutdown`; `OrchestrationClient.shutdown()`; `OrchestrationAPIError` (a subclass of `httpx.HTTPStatusError` with the API's error `code`), exported from `promptise.runtime`; `ERROR_CODES` lists every code; config updates answer with `live` and `effective_from` (for example `"next_invocation"`, `"next_budget_check"`, `"restart"` or `"redeploy"`) so a change that only applies later says so.

### Changed

- `ProcessStateError` takes an optional keyword `message`; `StateError` passes its message through it instead of setting the inherited attributes itself and skipping `ProcessStateError.__init__`. The attributes and messages are unchanged.
- **The core approval types live in a private module** -- `ApprovalRequest`, `ApprovalDecision`, `ApprovalHandler`, `ApprovalRule` and `ClassifierDecisionTrace` are defined in `promptise._approval_types`, so `promptise.approval` and `promptise.approval_classifier` no longer import each other. Import them from the same places as before (`promptise`, `promptise.approval`, `promptise.approval_classifier`); they are the same classes. `typing.get_type_hints(ApprovalDecision)` now resolves `trace` (it raised `NameError`).
- **Silent `except` blocks** -- the ones that hide a real failure now log it: a failing cache-purge notifier, `strategy.parse` or context-block render in a node, an `on_fallback` hook, a mission journal write (all `DEBUG`), and a budget escalation that could not suspend the process (`WARNING`). The deliberately silent ones say why in a comment. Small code-quality fixes from the same CodeQL pass: a constant conditional in `PromptiseSecurityScanner`'s composable (`detectors=`) setup, `@node` passing its docstring description to `BaseNode` instead of overwriting it, an unused logger.
- Open-mode meta-tools go through public process methods. A spawned process inherits the parent's budget, health, approval policy, guardrails, trigger delivery, journal level, open-mode limits, hooks and deploy policy, gets none of its secrets, MCP servers or identity, and cannot spawn processes itself.
- The trigger payload scan reads exactly the payload text the prompt shows (the rendered JSON, cut at `max_payload_chars`), in overlapping windows, instead of the payload's individual strings.
- `TriggerConfig` validation names the field and the fix: `watch_backend="watchdog"` without the package installed is an error at construction, and `InboxConfig` rejects a `default_ttl` longer than `max_ttl`. `EscalationTarget.webhook_url` must be an `http://` or `https://` URL.
- Every mutating API request is logged to the `promptise.runtime.api` logger (method, route, status, client; never the body) and, when it succeeds and the process has a journal, recorded as an `api_request` journal entry.
- HTTP MCP server credential headers (`Authorization`, `x-api-key`, names containing `token` or `secret`, ...) are masked in `repr()` and JSON dumps of server specs.
- The file journal replaces its checkpoint file atomically.

### Security

- **MCP server: session-binding fingerprints are keyed** -- the principal that binds an HTTP/SSE session to the credential that opened it was a bare SHA-256 of the bearer token or API key, so anyone who saw a fingerprint (a log line, a debug dump) could test guesses of a short API key offline. It is now an HMAC-SHA256 under a random per-process key: still stable for the life of the sessions it guards, useless outside the process. Session behaviour is unchanged. (CodeQL `py/weak-sensitive-data-hashing`)
- **Audit log files are created owner-only** -- `AuditMiddleware(log_path=...)` created the file `0o644`, so every local user could read which client called which tool (and the arguments with `include_args=True`). A new file is now created `0o600`. An existing file keeps its permissions; appending and resuming the chain are unchanged, and on Windows the call behaves as before. To tighten a log created by an earlier version, `chmod 600` it. (CodeQL `py/overly-permissive-file`)
- **Orchestration API: exception text no longer reaches HTTP clients** -- see Upgrade notes. (CodeQL `py/stack-trace-exposure`)
- **Webhook trigger: request values are escaped before they are logged** -- the "rejected request from ..." warnings logged `request.remote` as is; behind a forwarded-header middleware that is whatever the client sent, so a CR/LF in it could forge log entries. Logged values now have line breaks and other control characters escaped and are capped at 200 characters (private helper `promptise._logsafe.log_safe`). (CodeQL `py/log-injection`)
- **Runtime log lines escape client-supplied values** -- the control API (refused requests, the audit line, trigger IDs, PATCH sections), `AgentRuntime` (process names) and the distributed transport, coordinator and registry (node IDs and URLs) logged request values as is; they now go through `log_safe` like the webhook trigger's. A failed secret rotation no longer logs the secret's name: the warning carries the process, the exception type and the `error_id` the client gets (the audit line still records the route). (CodeQL `py/log-injection`, `py/clear-text-logging-sensitive-data`)
- **`${VAR}` references are found in linear time** -- `ENV_VAR_PATTERN` backtracks quadratically on a string full of unterminated references (`${A:-${a:-${a:-...`), and the control API ran it over every request body to refuse environment references, so one request could stall the API's event loop. `promptise.env_resolver.iter_env_references()` (and `has_env_reference()`) find the same references in one pass; the resolver, `validate_all_env_vars_available`, manifests, secret scopes and the API use them. `ENV_VAR_PATTERN` is unchanged and still exported. (CodeQL `py/polynomial-redos`)
- Secret values never reach the journal in plain text: when secret scoping is on, the journal is wrapped in `RedactingJournal`, which replaces current and rotated values (the last 10 per secret) with `[REDACTED]`; `get_secret` results are no longer journaled at `level="full"`. The conversation history kept between runs and the data passed to lifecycle hooks are redacted the same way.
- Inbox messages are scanned for prompt injection before they reach the prompt (`scan_with_guardrails`, see Upgrade notes).
- API deploys can't start local programs (stdio MCP servers, command hooks) unless `allow_command_deploys=True`. Open mode with `allow_tool_creation` now counts as such a deploy: `create_tool` runs model-written Python in the runtime's process, and its restricted builtins are not an isolation boundary. The docs and docstrings now say so plainly and point to the Docker sandbox for untrusted code.
- Hardened control-API deploys against reading the host's environment: `${VAR}` references in API requests are refused unless the operator allows them, an API-deployed process's secret scope resolves only allowed variables, and resolution errors in API answers no longer reveal whether a variable exists.
- Answers to inbox questions are redacted: secret values in an `/ask` answer, `process.ask()` result or `GET /inbox` listing are `[REDACTED]`, as in the journal.
- Agent-connected MCP servers are held to the same address checks as escalation webhooks when no allowlist is set, an API-deployed process needs an explicit allowlist, and the process identity's credential and caller tokens are only sent to servers the operator configured or listed.
- The trigger payload scan now covers the whole payload text placed in the prompt; text near the end of the `max_payload_chars` budget could reach the prompt unscanned.
- API-chosen journal, memory and watch paths are confined to `data_dir`; `env_prefix` can't be empty; `GET /triggers` masks credential-like `custom_config` values; unsigned loopback webhooks refuse cross-site browser requests; agent-added and spawned webhooks stay on loopback unless allowed; spawned processes inherit their parent's hooks.
- Distributed transport: a bearer header with non-ASCII bytes is a `401` (it was a `500`), and a registry coordinator can limit the hosts nodes register with (`allowed_node_hosts`); the docs describe why registered URLs need that limit.
- `ShellHook` no longer passes the host environment to hook programs and never runs the event payload through a shell.
- The CLI's state directory holds bearer tokens and is created private (`0700` / `0600`); directories or files owned by another user or readable by others are refused.
- `DistributedConfig` requires a cluster token for a non-loopback transport or a coordinator on another host.

### Deprecated

- Position-based trigger IDs (`static_<n>`) in the API trigger routes; use the stable IDs from `GET /triggers`.
- Unknown keys in the section config models (`FutureWarning`; they will be rejected).
- The manifest `entrypoint` key, which the runtime never read (`FutureWarning`). Declare hooks in `hooks:` instead.
- `MissionConfig.on_complete` remains deprecated and ignored (`FutureWarning`); use `auto_complete`.

## v1.4.3 — 2026-10-18

### Fixed

- `promptise run --tui` no longer shows a `NoMatches` traceback when it is closed at the moment its once-a-second status refresh runs.

## v1.4.2 — 2026-10-18

### Fixed

- `promptise run` no longer refuses a `promptise.toml` owned by root when it runs as another user, the usual case in a container image where `COPY` leaves files owned by root. Manifests owned by another non-root user, or writable by anyone, are still refused.

## v1.4.1 — 2026-10-18

### Fixed

- OpenAI and Azure OpenAI models now report token usage when they stream. With recent `langchain-openai` releases, models built from a provider id (`"openai:gpt-5-mini"`) left `stream_usage` unset, so every streamed call (`astream_with_tools`, and the chat, `-i` and terminal UI modes of `promptise run`) was recorded with 0 tokens and no cost. Pass `stream_usage=False` to opt out.

## v1.4.0 — 2026-10-17

### Added
- **`promptise run` runs your project, like `npm start`** -- a project is described by `promptise.toml` (or `[tool.promptise]` in `pyproject.toml`), found from the current directory upwards, the way `package.json` describes a Node project. `promptise run` runs the manifest's `main` file as a normal, complete Python program (`__name__ == "__main__"`, `sys.argv`, exit codes as with `python main.py`), and with `--devtools`, `--tui`, `--prod` observability or in the agent modes, every agent the program builds is instrumented automatically, with no changes to your code (a plain `promptise run` adds nothing to the agents). A `.py` file, `file.py:factory` or `package.module:factory` can be named instead; without a manifest, `promptise run some_file.py` uses the defaults. See docs/core/projects.md.
- **The project manifest** -- `name`, `version`, `description`, `main`, `agent` (an agent factory, `async def create_agent(ctx: RunContext)`), `python_path`, and the tables `[scripts]`, `[env]`, `[dev]`, `[prod]`, `[serve]` and `[pricing]`. Unknown keys are errors that name the dotted path, so typos are caught. `RunContext` (`promptise.run`) tells a factory the project, root, mode, flags and the `--model` override.
- **Modes** -- `script` (the program), `chat` (an interactive chat with the factory's agent: streaming, tool calls inline, `/help /stats /tools /reset /exit`), `once` (`-i TEXT` or `-i -` for stdin, `--json` for the answer and stats as JSON) and `serve`. `--mode auto` picks one from the target and the main file.
- **Scripts** -- `[scripts]` holds named commands, like npm scripts: `promptise run dev`. A script runs through the shell from the project root; extra arguments are appended, `promptise run` options are passed on to scripts that are `promptise` commands, the running Python's `bin` directory comes first on `PATH`, and `promptise run --list` lists them.
- **Environment** -- `-e K=V` sets a variable for one run; `--env-file` files and then `[env] files` (default `.env`) are loaded in order and never override a variable that is already set; `[env] vars` sets defaults; every missing `[env] required` variable is reported at once (exit 2). `PROMPTISE_NO_DOTENV=1` turns file loading off.
- **`--devtools` / `-d`** -- a live trace of every agent on stderr (each LLM turn with model, latency, tokens and estimated cost, each tool call with arguments and result, errors and agent events) and a summary per agent, model and tool when the run ends. Costs come from the manifest's `[pricing]` table (USD per 1M tokens, globs allowed); there is no built-in price list. `[dev] report` / `--report PATH` writes a JSON run report.
- **`--tui` / `-t` / `-tui`** -- a full terminal UI with Console/Chat, Trace, Tools, Metrics, Events, Approvals, Agents, MCP, Logs and Run tabs. Digits switch tabs, `esc` leaves the input, `r` restarts the program, `s` stops it, `a` / `d` approve or deny a pending tool call (for agents with a `QueueApprovalHandler`), `q` quits, `?` shows help. The program runs in a child process, so nothing it prints draws over the UI; in script mode the console input goes to its stdin.
- **`--watch` / `-w`** -- restarts the program when a project file changes (`[dev] watch`, `[dev] ignore`), like `nodemon`.
- **`--prod` and `--check`** -- JSON log lines on stderr (stdout stays the program's output), run events and, with `[prod] observe`, every timeline event as JSON log lines, and a graceful SIGTERM (a script shuts down as on Ctrl+C and exits with 143; the HTTP API drains, giving in-flight requests `[prod] shutdown_timeout` seconds, and exits with 0). Every run checks the manifest, the environment and the entry first and lists every problem (exit 2); `--check` also compiles the script or imports the factory, then exits 0 or 2.
- **`--serve`** -- an HTTP API for the agent factory: `GET /healthz`, `GET /readyz`, `GET /v1/info`, `POST /v1/invoke` (`{input, session_id?}` → `{output, duration_ms, usage, request_id}`), `POST /v1/stream` (Server-Sent Events). Bearer auth on `/v1/*` from `[serve] auth_token_env` (default `PROMPTISE_SERVE_TOKEN`), required on a non-loopback host; without a token, loopback-only `Host` and no cross-site `Origin`; `429` beyond `[serve] max_concurrency`; `503` while draining after SIGTERM; errors never include the exception, only an `error_id` that is logged.
- **`promptise init --project [DIR] [--name NAME] [--model MODEL] [--force]`** -- creates a project: `promptise.toml` (with `dev`, `ui`, `start`, `serve` and `check` scripts and the model provider's API key in `[env] required`), `agent.py` (a `create_agent` factory with one local tool), `main.py` (a normal script that also runs with `python main.py`), `.env.example`, `README.md`, and `.env` / `.promptise/` / `__pycache__/` added to `.gitignore`. It refuses to overwrite files without `--force`, listing every conflict. Also available as `promptise.run.scaffold.scaffold_project()`.
- **Example project** -- `examples/run/support_bot/`: a support agent with three local tools that works with `promptise run`, `--devtools`, `--tui` and `--serve`.

### Changed
- `promptise run` without `--model-id` now runs a project. The inline REPL (`promptise run --model-id ... [--stdio/--http/--instructions/--trace/--raw]`) is unchanged.

## v1.3.5 — 2026-10-15

### Fixed
- **Code-action: a `max_tool_calls` too small for the job gave a wrong answer, not an error** -- the prompt said "the program may make at most N function calls ... so plan the calls you need", so models counted their own calls and stopped early (`if calls >= 50: break`, `orders = orders[:49]`): the program never reached the cap, exited cleanly, and its partial result was the answer (3 of 46 benchmark programs did this at the default budget). And after a program did reach the cap, the repair asked for "a program that needs at most N calls", so the model wrote one that read only the first N records, which succeeded (5 of 5 runs of the guide's `max_tool_calls=20` demo answered wrong, none raised). The prompt now says each program has a call cap but not its value, and tells the model never to skip, sample or truncate records, never to count its calls, and to let `ToolBudgetExceeded` raise; the error a refused call raises in the program no longer gives the value either. The repair after a budget hit asks for a program that does the whole job with fewer calls (batch tools, no repeated fetches) or the reply `CANNOT_FINISH_WITHIN_CALL_CAP`. That reply, or a budget hit with no repair left, raises the new `CodeActionBudgetError` (see Changed). An answer from a program written after a budget hit is logged at WARNING. Promptise still cannot tell whether a program that stays within the cap read all the data; the guide says so.
- **Audit log: a role or guard denial was recorded as an authentication failure** -- a guard denial raises `AuthenticationError` with `code="ACCESS_DENIED"`, and the client got `ACCESS_DENIED`, but `AuditMiddleware` recorded only `error: "AuthenticationError: ..."`, so a verified caller without the role read exactly like a failed login, and anyone counting failed logins with `error.startswith("AuthenticationError")` counted every denial. An entry for a call that raised an `MCPError` now also has `error_code`, the code the client received (`ACCESS_DENIED`, `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR`, `VALIDATION_ERROR`, `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`, `TOOL_ERROR`, ...). `error` is unchanged. `error_code` is inside the HMAC chain; logs written by earlier versions have no `error_code`, still verify, and a 1.3.5 server continues their chain. The audit docs now also say that with `require_auth=True`, a request with a missing or invalid token is rejected with `401` by the HTTP gate before the MCP layer, so it is only in the HTTP access log, never in the audit log (by design, unchanged).

### Changed
- **New `CodeActionBudgetError`** (`promptise.engine`) -- a code-action run that cannot do the job within `max_tool_calls` raises it, with `max_tool_calls`, `attempts` and `report`. It is a `GraphExecutionError`, so existing handlers still catch it; budget failures that raised a plain `GraphExecutionError` in 1.3.4 raise this subclass. The code-action prompt and repair message changed (see Fixed): a model that would have answered from part of the data now ends the run with this error.

---

## v1.3.4 — 2026-10-15

### Fixed
- **`max_agent_iterations=N` ran N+1 model calls** -- the engine counted a node execution after running it and stopped only once the count passed the limit, so `build_agent(max_agent_iterations=N)` (the engine's `max_iterations`) allowed N+1 node executions: N+1 model calls and N+1 rounds of tool calls in the default ReAct graph. `N` now allows exactly `N` node executions, which in the ReAct graph is `N` model calls. The `build_agent` docstring and the engine docs say what is counted.
- **`BudgetHook` stopped a run silently and returned a raw tool result as the answer** -- when the budget ran out in the middle of a tool loop, `ainvoke()` returned normally with the last tool result as the last message and `last_report.error` unset. Such a run now raises `GraphBudgetExceededError` (a new `GraphStoppedError`, itself a `GraphExecutionError`) with `reason` (`"Token budget exceeded: 5078/5000 tokens"`), `stopped_by`, `pending_tools` and the report, like `GraphIterationLimitError` for iteration limits. Any hook that ends a run the same way (`CycleDetectionHook`, a custom hook) raises `GraphStoppedError`. A budget reached on the node that answered still returns the answer.
- **One `BudgetHook` capped an engine's lifetime, not a run** -- `tokens_used` was never reset, so after the first run hit the cap every later run on the same engine stopped after its first node. The hook now counts the run's own tokens (`GraphState.total_tokens`), so every run, concurrent ones included, gets the whole budget; `tokens_used` / `cost_used` report the latest run.
- **Context compaction could make a model re-read the same results until its budget ran out** -- a result longer than `keep_result_chars` was cut in the ledger with a note telling the model to call the tool again for the full text, and the re-read was cut again as soon as the next exchange started, so a model that re-read cut results one at a time could never have them all in full (reproduced with a scripted model: the default ReAct agent looped to its iteration limit). A call the model made more than once is now shown in full in the ledger (and shrunk last under `max_tokens`), and the excerpt says how much was cut and that a re-read is kept in full.
- **Schema-invalid tool calls skipped the server pipeline over HTTP and stdio, and echoed the whole input** -- the MCP SDK (1.10+) validated a call's arguments against the tool's `inputSchema` before Promptise saw the call, so an invalid call was never authenticated, rate limited or audited, and came back as plain text, `Input validation error: <jsonschema message>`, quoting the offending value in full (a 1 MB string against `max_length=10` produced a 1 MB error). The SDK's check is now off; the server validates every call itself, inside its pipeline, on every transport and in `TestClient`, and the error is the structured `VALIDATION_ERROR` envelope.
- **`VALIDATION_ERROR` envelopes could grow with the input** -- one entry per invalid item, so a list of 50,000 bad values produced a 9 MB error. The envelope now lists at most 20 field errors (`details.omitted_errors` counts the rest), clips field paths and messages, and caps the message at 1,000 characters; it never includes the rejected value.
- **Redacting credentials corrupted JSON** -- the connection-string patterns (`postgres://`, `mysql://`, `mongodb://`, `redis://`) and the `password=` / `secret=` patterns ran to the next whitespace, so on a JSON tool result they swallowed the closing quote, brace and comma: `{"config": {"db": "postgres://..."}, "region": "eu"}` became `{"config": {"db": "[POSTGRES_CONNECTION] "region": "eu"}`. Those patterns now stop at quotes and backslashes (URLs also at `<>{}`), and when the scanned text is a JSON document a redaction replaces text inside a string value only: the result parses to the same structure, JSON encoded inside a string stays valid, escape sequences are never split, and a number that has to be redacted becomes a quoted label.
- **mcp 2.x clients logged a 31-error WARNING on every connection to a Promptise server** -- an mcp 2.x client (current Pydantic AI, OpenAI Agents SDK) opens with `server/discover`, which the 1.x SDK failed to validate, logging `Failed to validate request: 31 validation errors for ClientRequest` (94 lines) before answering `-32602`. The server now answers a request for any method it does not implement with `-32601 Method not found` and logs it at DEBUG; the client falls back to `initialize` as before. A notification of an unknown method is ignored. Works with every supported `mcp` version (`>=1.9,<2`).
- **The Quick Start's first agent raised `TypeError`** -- "Your First Agent" calls `build_agent(model=..., instructions=...)`, but `servers` was a required keyword, so the first example a new user ran failed with `build_agent() missing 1 required keyword-only argument: 'servers'`. `servers` is now optional (see Changed).
- **The Quick Start's "Add Tools" example never started its tool server** -- the agent launched `tools.py` without `--serve`, so the child process ran the agent again (which launched another `tools.py`, and so on) instead of serving its tools, and the MCP handshake never happened. The example now passes `args=["tools.py", "--serve"]`. The same example in the blog posts "What is MCP", "Build an AI agent with Promptise" and "How to build an AI agent in Python" is fixed too (the last one also never called `server.run()`). Every `python` block on the Quick Start page now runs in the test suite with a scripted model, the stdio example against its real server.
- **Health: one anomaly silenced every other anomaly of its kind for 5 minutes** -- `HealthConfig.cooldown` (300 s by default) was kept per anomaly type, so once the agent was reported stuck on one call, getting stuck on a different call (in the same run or in the next runs) was not reported: no anomaly, no `health.anomaly` event, no `on_anomaly` action. The cooldown now applies to the same anomaly only: the same stuck call (tool and arguments), the same loop (whichever call of the cycle it is caught at), or the same empty-response / error-rate signal. The health docs now explain the cooldown and how to use `HealthMonitor` as an in-run guard in a `pre_tool` hook (`cooldown=0`, so every repeat is reported).
- **The mission judge printed "No tools discovered" on every evaluation** -- `build_agent()` printed `[promptise] No tools discovered from MCP servers; agent will run without tools.` to stdout whenever an agent had no tools, and the mission judge is built without tools, so a process with a mission wrote that line into the application's output once per evaluation. See Changed.
- **`promptise audit verify --strict` summary grammar** -- with one warning it read "1 warning fail the check"; it now reads "1 warning fails the check under --strict".
- **Open mode: the model was not told that a tool it creates is usable only from its next run** -- the run that calls `create_tool` keeps the agent and tool list it started with, but `create_tool` answered `Tool 'x' created. Rebuild successful` and its description said nothing about when the tool appears, so models kept looking for the tool and creating it again. See Changed.
- **Docs: `sandbox_custom_tools=False` does not give agent-written code full Python builtins** -- docs/runtime/meta-tools.md said it did. The code runs with the same restricted builtins (no `open`, `exec`, `eval`) and may additionally import a fixed standard-library allowlist (`json`, `math`, `re`, `datetime`, ...); the docs and the `_build_dynamic_tool` docstring now say so.

### Changed
- **The "Available tools" block is no longer added to the system prompt by default** -- every `PromptNode` with tools appended a summary of them (parameters and the first 120 characters of each description) to its system prompt, on top of the tool definitions the provider already receives. On the 40-tool test server that is 997 tokens on every model call (a third on top of the 3,043 tokens of tool definitions); a 48-tool guide run measured 1,882 of a 6,843-token first request. New `tool_summary` option on `build_agent`, `PromptGraphEngine` and `PromptNode`: `"off"` (the default), `"names"` (one line of names, 178 tokens on the same server) or `"full"` (the previous block). Set `tool_summary="full"` to keep the 1.3.3 prompt. A string default now renders quoted (`cursor: string=""`, was `cursor: string=`).
- **The compaction ledger no longer invites re-reading "something that changes over time"** -- its header now tells the model to use the listed facts and call again only for the rest of a cut result or when it is waiting for something to change, such as a job's status. The `managed` pattern's instructions say the same. Repeated calls still run the tool, so polling works as before.
- **`max_agent_iterations` / `PromptGraphEngine(max_iterations=...)` allow one node execution fewer than in 1.3.3** (see Fixed): an agent that needed exactly N+1 steps under a limit of N now raises `GraphIterationLimitError`; raise the limit by one to keep the old allowance.
- **A `BudgetHook` (or other hook) that stops a run mid tool loop raises** instead of returning (see Fixed). Catch `GraphBudgetExceededError` / `GraphStoppedError`.
- **Input validation runs inside the middleware chain** -- a call whose arguments fail validation now goes through the server's middleware (authentication, rate limiting, audit, in your order) and the tool's guards before it gets its `VALIDATION_ERROR`, on the live transports and in `TestClient`. An unauthenticated caller now gets `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR` rather than a schema error, invalid calls count against rate limits, and `AuditMiddleware` records them (`status: "error"`, no `args`). Middleware sees `ctx.state["_tool_arguments"] == {}` for such a call; the handler, dependency injection, `CacheMiddleware` and `ApprovalGateMiddleware` never see the rejected input (no cache lookup, no approval request). Resource templates and prompts follow the same order.
- **`build_agent(servers=...)` is optional** -- it defaults to `None`, the same as `servers={}`: an agent with no MCP tools (just the model, or `extra_tools`).
- **`build_agent()` no longer prints to stdout when an agent has no tools** -- with no servers configured nothing is reported (a `DEBUG` log record on `promptise.agent`); when servers are configured but none of them offers a tool, a `WARNING` naming the servers is logged on `promptise.agent` instead of the old `print`.
- **Health cooldown is per anomaly, not per anomaly type** -- see Fixed. A process whose agent gets stuck on several different calls now reports each (one `health.anomaly` event each, and the `on_anomaly` action for the run), where it reported only the first within `cooldown`. Repeats of the same anomaly are still reported once per `cooldown`, also across runs.
- **Open mode: `create_tool` and `connect_mcp_server` tell the model the change applies from its next run** -- `create_tool` now answers `Tool 'x' created. You can call it from your next run, not in this one: this run keeps the tools it started with, so finish the current task without it.` (it was `Tool 'x' created. Rebuild successful`), and `connect_mcp_server` answers `Connected to MCP server 'x' at <url>. Its tools are available from your next run, not in this one: ...` (it ended with `Rebuild successful`). Both tool descriptions say the same, and `create_tool`'s description no longer says `run(**kwargs) -> str` (declared parameters are passed to `run` as keyword arguments).

### Documentation
- `docs/core/engine-prebuilts.md`, `reasoning-patterns.md`, `engine-nodes.md`: the ledger "cuts redundant tool calls at equal accuracy" claim is now conditional on the model, with the guide's measurements (gpt-4.1-mini re-read records under 1.3.3 compaction; gpt-5-mini did not).
- `docs/core/tool-optimization.md`: new "The tool summary in the system prompt" section with token measurements.
- `docs/core/engine-hooks.md`: `BudgetHook` is per run, may overshoot by one node, and raises `GraphBudgetExceededError` mid tool loop.

---

## v1.3.3 — 2026-10-11

### Fixed
- **The mission LLM judge always failed** -- `MissionTracker` built its judge with `build_agent()` without the required `servers` argument, so every evaluation raised `TypeError` before any model call and was recorded as `achieved=False, confidence=0.5, reasoning="Evaluation failed: build_agent() missing 1 required keyword-only argument: 'servers'"`. With the default `confidence_threshold` (0.7) the mission went to `paused` after its first evaluation, evaluations stopped, and `auto_complete` could never trigger. The judge is now built with no servers and evaluates the evidence; it is shut down after each evaluation. `MissionTracker.evaluate()` also accepts a chat model instance as `model`.
- **`MissionConfig.max_invocations` was never enforced** -- a mission ran past its limit indefinitely. The mission now fails once it reaches `max_invocations` successful invocations: right after the last one (once its evaluation has run, so a mission completed by that run is not failed), with a `mission.failed` event whose `reason` is `"max_invocations"`, and later invocations are skipped, as for `timeout_hours`.
- **Health `on_anomaly` was skipped unless the anomaly was less than 5 s old when the run ended, and never applied to a run that raised** -- `AgentProcess` acted only on an anomaly detected in the last 5 seconds, and only after a successful run. A stuck agent keeps calling after the third identical call, so by the end of its run the anomaly was older: no pause, no escalation, no `health.anomaly` event. A stuck loop that ended in `GraphIterationLimitError` (or any error) never reached the check, and a `high_error_rate` anomaly was acted on only if a successful run ended within 5 s. Each anomaly is now acted on once, when the run that produced it ends, whether it succeeded or raised, however long ago it was detected; `high_error_rate` is acted on when the failure is recorded. A later healthy run no longer re-applies the action for an anomaly that was already handled (it did within the 5 s window). Every new anomaly emits its own `health.anomaly` event; the action runs once per run, for the latest.
- **`GET /api/v1/processes/{name}/journal` was always empty for processes with the default `process_id`** -- the endpoint read entries by `process_id` while the process files them under its name (so they survive a restart), and `AgentRuntime.add_process()` gives a random UUID `process_id`. It now reads by name.
- **`PATCH /api/v1/processes/{name}/instructions` changed nothing until a restart** -- it answered `"effective_from": "next_invocation"` but only set fields read when the agent is built (`start()`, a restart, an open-mode hot reload). The next invocation now rebuilds the agent with the new instructions before it runs, keeping the connected MCP servers, conversation history and process state; an invocation already running finishes with the old ones. The new `AgentProcess.update_instructions(text)` does the same from Python. The new instructions replace any an open-mode agent set for itself (`rollback()` keeps them), and a non-string `instructions` value is refused with `422 INVALID_CONFIG`.
- **`AgentProcess` never closed its MCP client, so every stop, restart and hot reload leaked the previous connection and its stdio servers** -- the process opens its own `MCPMultiClient` for the servers in its config, and nothing closed it: each `stop()`/`start()` cycle, restart-policy restart or open-mode hot reload left the previous stdio server processes running (still stuck in a hung call, if they were). `stop()` and restarts now close the client after shutting the agent down, and a hot reload closes the replaced agent and its client once the invocation that asked for the reload has finished with them (that run keeps the tools it started with). If a hot reload's rebuild fails, the process keeps its previous agent (it was shut down before the rebuild); a client whose agent fails to build is closed.
- **An agent identity no longer overrides a server's `api_key`** -- `build_agent(identity=...)` (and `AgentProcess`) presented the identity's bearer to every server without a `bearer_token`, including one configured with `HTTPServerSpec(api_key=...)`. The client sent `Authorization: Bearer <identity token>` next to `x-api-key`, the server's auth gate checked the bearer first, and the connection failed with `401`, so one agent could not use identity-authenticated servers and API-key servers together. An explicit per-server credential now always wins: `bearer_token`, `api_key`, or an `Authorization` / `x-api-key` header in `headers`.
- **The built-in token endpoint and `MCPClient.fetch_token()` speak OAuth 2.0 (RFC 6749)** -- `enable_token_endpoint()` read only a JSON body and answered a standard form-encoded `client_credentials` request with `400`, and `fetch_token()` posted JSON without `grant_type`, so it failed against a standard token endpoint. The endpoint now accepts `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` requests and HTTP Basic client authentication, answers errors with the standard `error_description` (`message` is kept), and sends `Cache-Control: no-store`. JSON bodies are still accepted. `fetch_token()` sends a form-encoded `grant_type=client_credentials` request, and retries once as JSON when a built-in endpoint from 1.3.2 or earlier asks for JSON.
- **Tool name collisions: the list matches the routing** -- when two servers expose a tool with the same name, `MCPMultiClient.list_tools()` (and so the agent's tool list) contained every copy, while calls went to the server listed last and the warning said only that version is used. It now returns only that server's copy; `list_prompts()` likewise. A server whose session is re-opened no longer takes a colliding name over from a server listed after it.
- **`promptise audit verify --strict` no longer prints "OK ... intact." when it fails** -- with only warnings (a truncated last line, say), the summary said `OK` while the exit code was `1`. It now reads `FAILED ... no tampering found, but N warning(s) fail the check under --strict`, and `--json` adds `strict` and `passed` (the exit code's verdict) next to `ok` (the chain's verdict).
- **A built-in identity guard implies `auth=True`** -- `guards=[RequireAuth()]` (or `HasRole`, `HasScope`, `RequireClientId`, the tenant guards) on a tool without `auth=True` denied every caller, valid credentials or not, because `AuthMiddleware` authenticates only `auth=True` tools. Such a guard now implies `auth=True`, as `roles=[...]` already did, on tools, resources, prompts and router-level guards. Custom guards are unchanged.
- **Custom auth providers can hand their claims to `AuthMiddleware`** -- the documented `ctx.state["jwt_payload"] = claims` was ignored (`AuthMiddleware` read only the built-in providers' private `_jwt_payload`), so roles, scopes and tenant from a custom provider never reached `ctx.client`, and `roles=[...]` denied every call. `ctx.state["jwt_payload"]` is now the public key for a custom provider's verified claims.
- **`MCPClient` no longer triggers the MCP SDK's `DeprecationWarning`** -- with mcp 1.30 every HTTP connection warned `Use streamable_http_client instead.` The client now uses `streamable_http_client` when the installed SDK has it and falls back to `streamablehttp_client` on older SDKs (down to the minimum, mcp 1.9).
- **Per-client rate limits that collapse into one shared bucket are reported** -- `RateLimitMiddleware` keys its buckets by `ctx.client_id`, which is set only when `AuthMiddleware` runs before it and the tool is authenticated; otherwise every caller silently shared one `"global"` bucket, and one runaway client throttled all the others. When an `AuthMiddleware` is installed but cannot identify the caller for the limiter (it runs after the limiter, or the tool is not `auth=True`), the server now logs a warning naming the tools, once, at startup (and on `TestClient`'s first call). A `key_func` marks the key as deliberate and is not reported. A limiter without a synchronous `consume(key)` method now raises `TypeError` when `RateLimitMiddleware` is created, instead of failing every call with `INTERNAL_ERROR`.
- **A forwarded caller token no longer overrides a server's `api_key`** -- `forward_caller_token` is on by default, so during an invocation whose `CallerContext` carries a `bearer_token`, every tool call (and `read_resource` / `get_prompt`) to a server configured with `HTTPServerSpec(api_key=...)` or an `x-api-key` header went over a session that sent `Authorization: Bearer <caller token>` next to `x-api-key`. The server's auth gate checked the bearer first and answered `401`, so an API-key server failed every call made for a user with a token: the agent-identity bug above, again. An API key now wins by default and the caller's token is not sent to that server. `HTTPServerSpec(forward_caller_token=True)` still forwards it, explicitly: both headers are then sent and the server decides which counts (a Promptise server checks the bearer first). The resolved choice is `HTTPServerSpec.forwards_caller_token`.
- **A `.agent` manifest dropped `forward_caller_token`** -- `manifest_to_process_config()` did not carry it to the `HTTPServerSpec`, so a manifest could not opt a server out of receiving caller tokens. It is carried now, as `AgentProcess`'s own dict normalisation already did.
- **`agent_pattern="debate"` replied with the judge's verdict, never looped, and had no tools** -- its critic and judge answered in plain text (no `output_schema`), so the critic's severity never sent the proposer back (it had no `revise` route either), the judge's `on_fail` never fired, and the run went proposer → critic → judge once and replied with the judge's text ("1) Does it pass ALL criteria? False ..."). `build_agent` also built it without the agent's tools. Critic and judge now return structured output (`DebateCritique`, `DebateVerdict` in `promptise.engine.prebuilts`): a severity above 0.3 or a failed verdict sends the answer back to the proposer, which sees the critique and verdict and has the agent's tools; a new `answer` stage writes the reply. Every stage has a budget (critic and judge `max_rounds`, proposer `max_proposer_steps`), so a debate always ends with a written answer. Same bug class as the 1.3.0 PEOATR fix.
- **`agent_pattern="deliberate"` replied with its reflection and never looped** -- plan and reflect answered in plain text, so the plan's quality gate and reflect's `continue` / `replan` / `answer` route never fired: every run went think → plan → act → observe → reflect once and replied with the reflection ("1) Overall progress ... 2) Mistakes ..."). Plan and reflect now return structured output (`PeoatrPlan`, `PeoatrReflection`), so their routes fire, every stage has a budget (plan 3, act `max_act_steps`, reflect 4), and a new `answer` stage writes the reply.
- **`agent_pattern="research"`: the verify step could not fail** -- it was a `GuardNode` with no guards: it passed every time without a model call (the "cross-check the findings" instructions it was given were never used), so the documented loop back to search never happened. Verify is now a model judgement (`ValidateNode`) that checks the findings against the tool results in the conversation and returns a `ResearchVerdict` (`passes`, `issues`): every claim backed by a tool result, nothing contradicting the sources, the whole question covered. A failed check sends the run back to search, which sees the issues; verify runs at most `max_verify_rounds` times (default 2), then the run goes to `synthesize`. Search now has a budget for the whole run (`max_search_steps`, default 12; it was 5, and a search that used it ended the run without an answer) and hands over to `synthesize` when it is used.
- **`agent_pattern="autonomous"` could reply with routing JSON, named tools as steps, and ran one tool round per step** -- every routing call appended the full planner prompt and step menu to the conversation as a new system message and the model's JSON choice as an AI message, so the prompts piled up and a run that finished could reply `{"next_node": "__done__", ...}` or a JSON error object; a chosen tool step ran one model call, so it made one round of tool calls and its model did not see the results unless the agent picked it again; and the model often named a tool (`"get_policy" is not available`). Now the routing exchange is sent for its one call and never added to the conversation; a chosen step runs its whole tool loop (re-run while its model calls tools, up to its `max_iterations`; the default `reason` step allows 10 rounds, `max_tool_rounds`); a tool name runs the step that has that tool, and an unknown name is reported to the next routing call only; and when the agent finishes without its terminal node having run last, that node runs (`synthesize` in the default pool, or the new `AutonomousNode(answer_node=...)`; with no terminal node, the node asks the model for the final answer), so the reply is always a written answer. The default pool is built without tools too (it was empty, and the run failed with "No nodes in the pool"). `AutonomousNode` now streams its steps' model chunks and tool events, so `astream_with_tools()` returns the answer (its `DoneEvent` was empty).
- **`PromptGraph.peoatr()` / `.research()` passed to `build_agent` as a graph ran as a node pool** -- they were built in the default `"autonomous"` mode with no edges, so `build_agent(agent_pattern=PromptGraph.peoatr(...))` wrapped their nodes in an `AutonomousNode` instead of running the graph. They are now static graphs, like the other built-in patterns.
- **Code-action: `max_tool_calls` did not count failed tool calls** -- the cap compared it with the number of *successful* calls, so a program could call a failing tool without limit (331 calls ran under the default cap of 50; a program looping a failing tool 120 times under `max_tool_calls=10` made all 120). Every call a program makes now counts: successful, failed, an unknown tool, or one a `pre_tool` hook blocked.
- **Code-action: reaching `max_tool_calls` was silent, and a repair could not recover from it** -- the bridge answered the call with an error and logged nothing, so a program that caught exceptions finished with a wrong answer (`report.error` `None`); and the budget was shared with the repair attempts, so a program repaired after hitting the cap failed its first call (or printed a made-up result such as `None spent $0.00`). Now a call beyond the cap raises `ToolBudgetExceeded` in the program (a `RuntimeError`, without running the tool, and so does every later call), the node logs a warning, and the program's output is discarded even if it caught the error. The model is told the program needed more than `max_tool_calls` calls and asked for one that needs fewer; each repaired program gets a fresh budget, so a run makes at most `(max_repairs + 1) × max_tool_calls` calls. With no repair left the run raises `GraphExecutionError`, and `ExecutionReport.error` names `max_tool_calls`. The prompt states the limit. A crash's error now ends the final error message (`program failed after N attempt(s); last attempt: ...`).
- **Code-action: MCP tool results reached the program as JSON text** -- an MCP tool answers with the text of the server's result, so `list_orders("2026-09")` arrived as the string `'["SO-9001", ...]'` and programs iterated its characters, although the docs said data arrives as lists and dicts. An MCP tool's text result that is valid JSON now reaches the program decoded (lists, dicts, numbers; other text stays a `str`), also behind the approval, guardrail and tracing wrappers. A Promptise error envelope (`{"error": {"code": ..., "message": ...}}`) raises in the program as an `MCPToolError`, like an MCP error result. Python tools are unchanged: a `str` they return stays a `str`.
- **Code-action: `ExecutionReport.tool_calls` was always 0** -- `CodeActionNode` recorded its calls without counting them. The report now counts the programs' calls, failed ones included, and each observation has its duration.
- **Sandbox cleanup always took 5 s** -- `SandboxSession.cleanup()` stopped the container gracefully before removing it, and the container's `/bin/bash` (PID 1) ignores `SIGTERM`, so every cleanup waited out `docker stop`'s 5 s timeout, blocking the event loop. Code-action paid it once per program and once more for `build_agent`'s probe. A non-persistent session's container is now killed (new `SandboxBackend.kill_container()`, which defaults to `stop_container()`) and removed, about 0.1 s; a code-action program with no tool calls takes about 1 s in all. The Docker calls in `stop_container()` / `remove_container()` no longer block the event loop.
- **Open mode: `create_tool` ignored its `parameters`** -- every agent-written tool took one `input` string, so a tool written as `def run(a, b)` failed with `run() got an unexpected keyword argument 'input'`. The declared JSON object schema is now the tool's argument schema: the model is offered its parameters, the arguments are validated (invalid ones raise `ToolArgumentError`), and `run` gets them as keyword arguments, as plain JSON, with each property's schema `default` for one left out. A `parameters` that is not an object schema (or lists parameters without `properties`, or names one that is not a valid Python keyword argument) is refused with an error and no tool is created. Empty `parameters` keep the free-form `input` string.
