- **Open mode: a change refused by `max_rebuilds` was applied on the next restart** -- the meta-tools set the new instructions, tool or server before the hot reload checked the limit, and the refused change stayed in the process's dynamic state, which the next `start()` (deploy, crash recovery, `restart_policy`) built the agent from. The limit is now checked before anything changes, under the rebuild lock, and a refused change is not made at all: `modify_instructions`, `create_tool` and `connect_mcp_server` answer `Error: max rebuilds (N) reached; the change was not applied` (`create_tool` and `connect_mcp_server` used to say the tool was created or the server connected). A change whose rebuild fails is undone too (a failed `create_tool` left its tool behind). The limit counts over the process object's lifetime, as documented; restarts do not reset it.

### Documentation
- Runtime reference corrected where it did not match the code: mission states are `active` / `paused` / `completed` / `failed`, `eval_every` defaults to `1`, the injected `[Mission]` block and what a low-confidence or failed evaluation does (`docs/runtime/governance/mission.md`); the injected `[Budget Remaining]` block and the budget escalation payload (`"type": "budget_violation"`, `violation` as an object with `limit_name`, `limit_value`, `current_value`, `tool_name`) (`budget.md`); `on_anomaly` accepts `"log"`, `"pause"` and `"escalate"` (the docs listed `"stop"`, which `HealthConfig` rejects), when the action runs, error-rate detection counts failed invocations, and the `HealthMonitor` method list (`health.md`); the instructions and journal endpoints (`api.md`), hot reload (`meta-tools.md`) and stop/restart (`processes.md`).
- [testing.md "Testing with authentication"](../mcp/server/testing.md#testing-with-authentication): passed `algorithm=` (not a `JWTAuth` parameter), relied on a guard without `auth=True`, and made a token without `exp`; it now runs, and the test suite runs it.
- [auth-security.md "Custom auth provider"](../mcp/server/auth-security.md#custom-auth-provider): stored the claims under a key `AuthMiddleware` did not read; it now shows the `jwt_payload` contract and runs.
- [caching-performance.md "Tiered rate limiting"](../mcp/server/caching-performance.md#tiered-rate-limiting): defined `async check(key, ctx)`, which `RateLimitMiddleware` never calls (every call failed with `INTERNAL_ERROR`); it now uses `consume(key)` with a `key_func` that puts the tier in the key. A new note says when buckets are per client.
- [showcase.md](../resources/showcase.md): the database and payments MCP server snippets used `MCPServer(auth=...)`, `AuthMiddleware()` without a provider and `RateLimitMiddleware(requests_per_minute=...)`, none of which exist, and passed `"${JWT_SECRET}"` / `"${AUDIT_SECRET}"` literally as keys. They read the keys from the environment and run.
- [approval-gates.md "Audit visibility"](../mcp/server/approval-gates.md#audit-visibility): the approval request id is in the error the client receives and in the gate's log lines, not in the audit entry as the page said.
- [observability.md](../mcp/server/observability.md): a key rotation warning comes on the first audited call after startup, not at startup.
- [identity/guide.md](../identity/guide.md): an explicit per-server `api_key` (or `x-api-key` header) wins over the agent identity, like `bearer_token`.
- [multi-user-systems.md](../guides/multi-user-systems.md) and [secure-multi-tenant-platform.md](../guides/secure-multi-tenant-platform.md): `AuditMiddleware(secret=...)` raised `TypeError` (the parameter is `hmac_secret`), and `JWTAuth`, `AuditMiddleware` and `PostgresConversationStore` were given `"${JWT_SECRET}"`-style strings, which these classes take literally (only `.superagent` files and a few documented parameters expand them). The examples read the keys from the environment with `os.environ[...]` and the audit examples write `audit.jsonl`, verifiable with `promptise audit verify`. The 30-tool server in multi-user-systems.md also used `MCPServer(auth=...)`, `RateLimitMiddleware(requests_per_second=...)` and `server.mount(router)`, none of which exist, and an `on_authenticate` hook with the wrong signature; it runs now, and the test suite runs it and both audit examples. The same literal-key fix in config.md (`HTTPServerSpec`) and a blog post (`JWTAuth`); showcase.md's gateway example includes its routers with `include_router()`.
- [Server Specs](../core/agents/server-specs.md#per-user-tokens), Multi-User Identity, Multi-Tenancy, Building Agents and Tool Adapter pages: which credential a server sees for a caller with a token (`forward_caller_token` unset, `True` or `False`, with and without an API key).
- agentic-runtime.md no longer shows `MissionConfig(on_complete=...)`; [mission.md](../runtime/governance/mission.md) lists it as deprecated.
- [reasoning-patterns.md](../core/agents/reasoning-patterns.md) and [engine-prebuilts.md](../core/engine-prebuilts.md): debate, deliberate, research and autonomous described as they now run (debate's critic and judge loops and `answer` stage, deliberate's routes and `answer` stage, research's model-judged verify loop and its bound, autonomous's tool loops, routing and final answer); [engine-nodes.md](../core/engine-nodes.md#autonomousnode) and [engine-internals.md](../core/engine-internals.md): `AutonomousNode` (`answer_node`, the tool loop per step, routing kept out of the conversation).
- [Code-action](../guides/code-action.md#the-tool-call-budget), [engine prebuilts](../core/engine-prebuilts.md#code-action-one-sandboxed-program), [reasoning patterns](../core/agents/reasoning-patterns.md) and [building agents](../core/agents/building-agents.md): the tool-call budget, MCP results arriving as data, and the latency per program (about 1 s, not 1-2 s). [Meta-tools](../runtime/meta-tools.md) and [configuration](../runtime/configuration.md#openmodeconfig): how `create_tool` uses `parameters`, and what `max_rebuilds` counts and refuses. [Sandbox](../core/sandbox.md): `cleanup()` kills the container, and `kill_container()`.

### Deprecated
- **`MissionConfig.on_complete`** -- it was documented (`"stop"`, `"continue"` or `"suspend"`) but never read: a completed mission stopped the process whenever `auto_complete=True`, whatever `on_complete` said. Setting it now emits a `FutureWarning`; it will be removed. Use `auto_complete` (`False` keeps the process running after completion; its later invocations are skipped).

### Changed
- A tool, resource or prompt with a built-in identity guard is now authenticated: a caller without credentials gets `AUTHENTICATION_ERROR` where it got `ACCESS_DENIED` (it was denied either way).
- `MCPMultiClient.list_tools()` / `list_prompts()` return one entry per name when servers collide (the copy calls are routed to).
- A form-encoded request to the built-in token endpoint must carry `grant_type` (RFC 6749); a JSON request still defaults to `client_credentials`.
- `HTTPServerSpec.forward_caller_token` defaults to `None`: forward the caller's token unless the server has an API key. A server with an API key no longer receives caller tokens unless it sets `forward_caller_token=True`; `True` and `False` behave as before.
- `PromptGraph.debate()` has a fourth node, `answer`, and new parameters `tools`, `blocks` and `max_proposer_steps`; `max_rounds` now bounds the critic and the judge (default 3, was 5 for the proposer, which never looped). `PromptGraph.deliberate()` has a sixth node, `answer`, and new parameters `blocks` and `max_act_steps`. `PromptGraph.research()` takes `max_search_steps` and `max_verify_rounds`, and its `verify` node is a `ValidateNode` (a model call per check) instead of a `GuardNode`; pass `verify=False` to skip it. `PromptGraph.autonomous()` takes `blocks` and `max_tool_rounds`. The multi-stage patterns make one more model call than before: their answer stage (and research's verify).
- Code-action's `max_tool_calls` caps each program, not the whole run: a repaired program gets a fresh budget (a run makes at most `(max_repairs + 1) × max_tool_calls` calls), and failed calls count. A program that reaches it fails instead of answering.
- Code-action programs get MCP tool results that are JSON text decoded, so code that parsed the text itself (`json.loads(list_orders(...))`) now gets a list and must drop the parse.
- `AgentProcess.rollback()` is no longer refused once `max_rebuilds` is reached (it cleared the dynamic state and returned the refusal, so the rollback happened only at the next restart); it counts as a rebuild. If its rebuild fails, the previous state is restored.
- An open-mode tool created with `parameters` takes those parameters instead of `input`.

---

## v1.3.2 — 2026-10-11

### Fixed
- **Code-action: `post_tool` hooks got their arguments swapped and could not rewrite results** -- `CodeActionNode` called `post_tool(name, args, result, state)` instead of the documented `post_tool(tool_name, result, args, state)` and ignored what the hook returned, so a hook that redacts or truncates tool results had no effect on code-action agents. The hook now gets the documented arguments, and a returned string replaces the result the generated program receives and the run records.
- **A repeated tool call got a stale result once a tool loop compacted** -- once a run had 6 tool results (the default ReAct agent's `context_scope="auto"` switching to the ledger), and on every call of a `context_scope="ledger"` node (`agent_pattern="managed"`), a call with the same tool and arguments as an earlier successful call got that earlier result back and the tool never ran. An agent polling `queue_status(job_id)` or a health check saw "running" until its iteration limit although the job had completed, the server never got the requests, and `trace_tools`, `observer`, callbacks and engine hooks showed nothing for those calls. A repeated call now runs the tool. Answering a repeat from the earlier result is an explicit opt-in for tools whose result cannot change during a run (see Changed), and a call answered that way is reported, marked as reused. The ledger's note to the model now says each entry is the result as it was when the call ran and that the current value of something that changes, such as a status, can be fetched again (it said not to call a tool again for the listed results); the `managed` pattern's prompt says the same.
- **A run stopped by an iteration limit in the middle of a tool loop returned a tool result as the answer** -- when the ReAct `reason` node used its 15 iterations (or the run reached `max_agent_iterations`) right after calling tools, the engine logged "Stuck node 'reason' — forcing graph end" and the run ended on that tool result, so `result["messages"][-1].content` was the raw tool output, such as a `queue_status` JSON, instead of an answer. Such a run now raises `GraphIterationLimitError` (new in `promptise.engine`, a `GraphExecutionError`) with the node, the `limit` and the tools whose results the model never saw (`pending_tools`); `ExecutionReport.error` carries the message. `astream()` and `astream_events()` raise it after their output, and `astream_with_tools()` ends with an `ErrorEvent`. A limit reached after the model answered, or one the graph routes to an `"error"` transition or an `__error__` node, ends the run as before.
- **`ToolNode(deduplicate=True)` treated calls from earlier runs as duplicates and left them without a result** -- the node kept its record of past calls on the node object, which every run of the graph shares. A call with the same tool and arguments as any call the node had made before, in the same run, an earlier run on the same agent or a concurrent one, did not run, and no `ToolMessage` was written for its `tool_call_id` (`NodeResult.tool_calls` recorded it as `"DUPLICATE: identical call already made"`). The model got no result for the call, which providers that need one result per call reject, and a status check the model repeated in a later turn never reached the tool. Deduplication now covers only the calls of one model turn, and its record belongs to the run, not the node. When the model asks for the same tool with the same arguments twice in one turn, the tool runs once and each repeat gets its own `ToolMessage` with that result, reported as reused the way `ContextCompaction(reuse_results=...)` reports it: `trace_tools`, the `observer`, callbacks (`metadata["promptise_reused"]`), engine hooks (`on_tool_reused`) and `"reused": True` in `NodeResult.tool_calls`. A call in a later turn, a later pass of a `tool_selector`, or another run runs the tool, and a failed call is never reused. If the graph reaches the node again before the model's next turn, calls that already have a result are not run again.
- **`ToolNode` calls were missing from `astream_events()` and `astream_with_tools()`** -- a `ToolNode` emitted no `on_tool_start` / `on_tool_end` events, so in a custom graph that runs tools with one, a streamed run showed no `ToolStartEvent` / `ToolEndEvent` for its calls, reused calls included, and `DoneEvent.tool_calls` was empty. A `ToolNode` now streams each call with the same events and fields as a `PromptNode`: `tool_index`, `duration_ms`, `success`, `reused`, and for a failed call the error's summary. It runs a tool the same way too: as a tool call, so a tool with an `InjectedToolCallId` argument works (it raised before), and an MCP tool error reaches the model as the server's error envelope with its code (`{"error": {"code": "TOOL_ERROR", "message": ...}}`) in a `ToolMessage` with `status="error"`, where it was `"Error: MCPToolError: ..."` without a status. A tool that handles its own `ToolException` and an unknown tool count as failed, a non-string result reaches the model as JSON (it was a Python repr), and every `ToolMessage` either node writes carries the tool's `name`. The calls of one turn still run one after another.
- **Engine hooks' `pre_tool` / `post_tool` never ran for `ToolNode` calls** -- a hook that rewrites arguments, audits results or refuses a tool had no effect on calls a `ToolNode` made. They now run around each call exactly as for a `PromptNode`: `pre_tool` returns the arguments to use, `post_tool` the result the model sees, and a `pre_tool` hook that raises blocks the call (the tool does not run, no `post_tool`, the model gets `"Error: <message>"`, and the call counts as failed). A repeat answered from the turn's earlier result gets `on_tool_reused` instead, as before. The rules are now documented under *Tool Hooks* in the engine hooks guide.
- **`ToolNode(validate_inputs=True)` did nothing** -- the parameter (on by default, documented as "validate tool arguments before calling") was never used, so invalid arguments reached the tool and came back as a raw pydantic `ValidationError`, and a `null` for an optional parameter of a Python tool failed the call. The node now validates each call's arguments against the schema the model is offered for the tool, after `pre_tool` hooks (an MCP tool, which has checked its own arguments since 1.3.1 and reports a rejected call to `trace_tools` and the `observer`, is left to do so). Invalid arguments do not reach the tool: the model gets the `ToolArgumentError` message from 1.3.1, each problem by argument path and an instruction to correct the call, in a `ToolMessage` with `status="error"`; the call counts as failed, streams as failed, is never reused, and callbacks get `on_tool_error` with the `ToolArgumentError` (code `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`). As for MCP tools, an explicit `null` for an optional parameter that cannot be null counts as not given. `validate_inputs=False` passes the arguments to the tool as they are.
- **The iteration-limit error named the tool "?" for a `tool_selector` graph** -- when a run whose tool calls came from a `ToolNode(tool_selector=...)` hit an iteration limit, `GraphIterationLimitError` said "after a call to ? whose result the model never saw" and `pending_tools` was `["?"]`, or named a tool from an earlier model turn when the model had answered before. It now names the tools of the trailing tool results, including those a selector called.

### Changed
- **Ledger compaction no longer answers repeated calls from earlier results by default** -- since 1.1.0, `context_scope="ledger"` nodes (the `managed` pattern) served every repeated `(tool, args)` call from the earlier result, and so did the default ReAct agent (`"auto"`) once its tool loop compacted. To keep that for lookups whose result cannot change during a run, opt the tools in: `build_agent(context_compaction=ContextCompaction(reuse_results=["get_employee", "lookup_*"]))` (tool names, `*` wildcards allowed) or `reuse_results=True` for every tool; `PromptNode(compaction=...)` takes it per node. It applies once the node compacts and never to a failed call. No MCP annotation turns it on: `idempotentHint` says a repeated call has no further side effects, not that its result stays the same. A reused call is reported like any other, marked as reused: `trace_tools` prints `↺ Reusing an earlier result for tool: ...`, the `observer` timeline and `observe=True` record it with `reused: true` in the metadata, LangChain callbacks get its start and end with `metadata["promptise_reused"]` (`promptise.engine.nodes.REUSED_TOOL_METADATA_KEY`), engine hooks get the new optional `on_tool_reused(tool_name, args, result, state)`, `NodeResult.tool_calls` records it with `"reused": True` (it was `"cached": True`), and `astream_with_tools()` ends it with a `ToolEndEvent` whose new `reused` field is `True`. `reuse_results` round-trips through `save_graph()` / `load_graph()`.

---

## v1.3.1 — 2026-10-11

### Fixed
- **MCP tools sent `null` for every optional parameter the model left out** -- LangChain's tool input parsing (langchain-core 1.x) fills in every field with a default, and the agent's tool schema gives an optional parameter without a schema default the default `None`, so the MCP tool sent `{"limit": null, ...}` for each one. A server that validates its input rejected the call (`Input validation error: None is not of type 'integer'`): every MCPcast-generated server, and any tool whose schema has an optional, non-nullable parameter. Only the arguments the model gave are sent now, at every level of nesting, and the server applies its own defaults. An explicit `null` is sent only where the schema allows null; a `null` for an optional parameter that cannot be null counts as not given (logged at debug level). The same applies to MCPcast's readiness run.
- **Invalid tool arguments failed silently** -- a call whose arguments did not match the tool's schema (including an explicit `null` for an optional, non-nullable parameter) failed before it was sent, with a raw pydantic error for the model and nothing in `trace_tools` or the observability timeline. It now raises `promptise.tools.ToolArgumentError` (a `ToolException`, code `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`): the model gets each problem by argument path (`- items.0.qty: Input should be a valid integer (got "two")`) and is told to correct the call, `trace_tools` and the timeline show the call and the error, and a `tool.error` event carries the code. Behind an approval gate, invalid arguments are reported the same way and never reach a reviewer.
- **Nested tool arguments were model reprs downstream** -- nested objects reached `trace_tools`, the observability timeline, `ApprovalRequest.arguments` / `raw_arguments`, `approval.*` events and adaptive-strategy denial lessons as generated model instances (`Args_create_order_items_Item_2(sku='A', ...)`), so reviewers saw reprs and `ApprovalRule.argument_contains` / predicates matched them instead of the data. They are plain JSON now (dicts and lists, the fields the model set). Python tools behind the approval gate, guardrail scanning or tracing still receive validated values (model instances for model parameters) and their own defaults.
- **Free-form MCP tool arguments were dropped** -- a tool whose input schema has no properties but allows any keys (`additionalProperties`) was called with `{}`, whatever the model passed.
- **Redaction missed current OpenAI and Anthropic API keys, and OTel spans exported error text unredacted** -- the credential pattern behind observability and event redaction matched only `sk-` followed by letters and digits, so `sk-proj-…`, `sk-svcacct-…` and `sk-ant-…` keys (which contain `-` and `_`) passed through. It now matches them. `OTelMiddleware` put the raw exception message on spans (`mcp.error.message`, the status description and the recorded exception, including its traceback); all three now go through the same redaction, and the span's own automatic exception recording is turned off so nothing is exported unredacted.
- **`trace_tools=True` printed every cross-agent delegation twice** -- each `ask_agent_<name>` (and `broadcast_to_agents`) call and its result were printed, and recorded by an `observer`, once by the delegation hooks and again by the wrapper that traces non-MCP tools, although the peer ran once. Cross-agent tools now report only through their delegation hooks, so each delegation prints once, with the delegation's own arguments (and, for a broadcast, the per-peer results).
- **A failed `astream_with_tools()` run was not logged** -- the consumer gets a generic `ErrorEvent` and the cause was dropped at every log level (1.2.1 logged it). The failure is logged again as one `ERROR` line on `promptise.agent` (the level the engine logs a failed run at) with the error type, message, agent id, session id and invocation id. The message goes through the observability redaction (API keys, tokens, URL credentials, card numbers, emails) and is truncated; a guardrail violation is summarised, not quoted. `astream()` and `ainvoke()` raise the failure to the caller and are unchanged.
- **`request_more_tools()` without arguments enabled the whole tool catalogue** -- with semantic tool selection on a large server, the next model call could be offered hundreds of tools and fail on OpenAI's 128-tool limit. An argument-less call now returns the next `semantic_top_k` tools most relevant to the conversation that the model was not offered yet (each further call pages on). One call returns at most 32 tools (a longer `tool_names` list is cut, and the reply says how many were left out), and a model call is offered at most 100 indexed tools.
- **`optimize_tools` stripped more than descriptions from tool schemas** -- schema minification (on at every level: `"minimal"`, `"standard"`, `"semantic"`) rebuilt each tool's argument model from field types and defaults only. It dropped the constraints shown to the model (`enum` values a `Literal` cannot hold, `pattern`, `minLength`/`maxLength`, `minimum`/`maximum`, `format`, `minItems`/`maxItems`, ...) and the model config, so a free-form tool (no properties, `additionalProperties`) lost its "takes any keys" setting and was called with `{}` again. Minification now removes only descriptions and keeps types, required and optional fields and their defaults, nullability, constraints and other field settings, the model config, and nested models wherever they sit (`Optional[list[Model]]`, `dict[str, Model]`). Optional parameters the model leaves out are still not sent, `null` handling and plain-JSON nested arguments work as without optimization, and `max_schema_depth` still flattens deeper objects to `dict`, keeping the field's own constraints and nullability.
- **Top-level parameter constraints and free-form schemas did not reach the model** -- LangChain builds the schema it offers the model (`BaseTool.tool_call_schema`) by copying each field's type, default and description, which drops `json_schema_extra` and the model config. Constraints on nested objects reached the model, those on top-level MCP tool parameters did not, and a free-form MCP tool was offered as a tool without parameters (`{"properties": {}}`), with or without `optimize_tools`. MCP tools now offer their argument schema as is, and the approval gate, guardrail scanning and tool tracing wrappers offer what the tool they wrap offers.

### Documentation
- **MCP server OpenTelemetry example** -- the docs showed `OTelMiddleware(endpoint=...)`, which raises `TypeError`: the middleware takes `service_name`, `tracer_provider` and `meter_provider`, and the exporter (and its endpoint) is configured on the provider. The examples now build an OTLP `TracerProvider` and run as written; the span attribute is `mcp.status` (not `mcp.tool.status`). The docs also said `OTelMiddleware` and `PrometheusMiddleware` do nothing without their packages; they raise `ImportError`, whose message now names the extra (`pip install "promptise[all]"`) as well as the packages. The `PrometheusMiddleware` docstring no longer promises a `/metrics` route the server does not add.

---

## v1.3.0 — 2026-10-11

### Upgrading from 1.2.x

1.3.0 is a security and correctness release. Most of it needs no code changes, but several defaults now fail closed. Check this list before upgrading:

- **MCP servers listen on `127.0.0.1` by default.** `server.run(transport="http")` without `host=` is no longer reachable from other machines or containers. In Docker or behind a load balancer, pass `host="0.0.0.0"` and set `allowed_hosts`. A public bind without `AuthMiddleware` or `allowed_hosts` logs a warning at startup.
- **`CORSConfig(allow_origins=["*"], allow_credentials=True)` raises `ValueError`.** List the origins that may send credentials.
- **Resources and prompts go through auth, guards and middleware like tools.** `require_auth=True`, `require_tenant`, router auth and guards, and rate limits now also cover resource reads and prompt requests, so a client without a token gets an MCP error where it used to read them. Resources and prompts can take `auth=`, `roles=` and `guards=` like tools.
- **Guardrails fail closed.** `guardrails=True` / `PromptiseSecurityScanner.default()` needs `transformers` and `torch`. If a model-backed head cannot run, scans are blocked instead of passed.
- **Sandbox fails closed.** `build_agent(sandbox=...)` raises when the sandbox cannot start (no Docker client, Docker not running, gVisor missing) instead of building the agent without sandbox tools. The default network is `"none"`.
- **Identity credentials fail closed.** When an agent's identity cannot get a token (IdP down, expired file), the call fails with `MCPCredentialError`; nothing is sent unauthenticated.
- **Runtime transport and API need a token on public binds.** `RuntimeTransport(host="0.0.0.0")` without `auth_token` raises (pass `allow_unauthenticated=True` to opt out). `OrchestrationAPI` rejects an empty `auth_token`. On loopback, both refuse browser-originated requests (cross-site `Origin`, non-loopback `Host`).
- **`ProcessConfig`, `TriggerConfig` and `TriggerDeliveryConfig` reject unknown keys**, and `ProcessConfig.journal` is off by default.
- **MCP tool errors raise.** An MCP tool that returns an error result raises `MCPToolError` when called directly (`tool.ainvoke(...)`); inside an agent the model still sees the server's message, and the call counts as failed.
- **Event webhook signatures changed.** Receivers must verify the new `t=...,v1=...` header with `promptise.events.verify_event_signature()`; the old bare-hex signature is gone.
- **Webhooks to private addresses are refused at send time**, including after DNS changes. Set `allow_private_networks=True` on the sink or handler for internal receivers.
- **Semantic cache stores less.** Follow-up turns and turns that called tools are not cached by default (`cache_multi_turn=True`, `cache_tool_turns=True` for read-only tools), `scope="per_session"` needs a `user_id`, and `build_agent(cache=...)` raises `ImportError` when the cache's packages are missing.
- **Cross-agent delegation.** `broadcast_to_agents` is opt-in (`include_broadcast=True`), the model can no longer pick a timeout, and delegation stops at `max_delegation_depth` (default 3). `.superagent` files reject `auth:` on HTTP servers (use `bearer_token:` / `api_key:`), and `promptise validate` fails on missing environment variables.
- **Reasoning Graph.** Pydantic structured outputs are dicts in `result.output` and `state.context`; a `PromptNode` stops at its own `max_iterations` (default 10); unknown `agent_pattern` names raise `ValueError`.
- **Observability** redacts secrets and personal data in traces by default (`ObservabilityConfig.redact_sensitive=False` to turn it off), and prompt text is recorded only at `FULL` unless `record_prompts=True`.
- **Audit logs.** `AuditMiddleware(log_path=...)` with signing on needs a key (`PROMPTISE_AUDIT_SECRET` or `hmac_secret=`); without one it raises instead of signing with a random per-process key nobody could verify. A restarted server continues the file's chain instead of starting a new one, and entries carry `seq`. Verify a file with `verify_audit_log()` or `promptise audit verify`.
- **RAG.** `rag_to_tool(format="json")` returns `{"notice": ..., "results": [...]}` instead of a bare list, and markdown/text output is fenced as untrusted data. Indexing a document again replaces its chunks. A `RAGPipeline` is one shared corpus: scope retrieval per caller yourself (see *Who can retrieve what* in the RAG guide).
- **Adaptive strategy** keeps lessons per caller (`scope="per_user"`); lessons saved by 1.2.1 or earlier carry no scope and are no longer shown. Re-add the ones you need.

### Added
- **MCP client: `bearer_token_provider` for short-lived credentials** -- `MCPClient(bearer_token_provider=...)` takes a callable (sync or async) that returns the current token and is asked on every HTTP request, instead of a fixed `bearer_token`. When the server answers `401`, the provider is called with `force_refresh=True` and the request is re-sent once with the new token (concurrent rejections share one refresh). A session opened with a token the provider has since renewed is reopened before the next call; calls still running on the old session finish there and are never re-sent. HTTP and SSE only. New `MCPCredentialError` (a `MCPClientError`) when the provider cannot supply a token.
- **Identity: `get_credential(audience, force_refresh=True)`** -- on `AgentIdentity` and every provider, skips the cached credential, for when a resource rejected it before its `exp` (rotated or revoked key, clock skew). Active providers (`from_entra`, `from_aws` STS, `from_gcp`, `from_spiffe`) now know their default audience (`provider.default_audience`), so `get_credential()` and `get_credential(<default audience>)` share one cached credential. `JwksAuth.from_discovery()` accepts `leeway` like the constructor.
- **Core: `build_superagent()` builds a whole `.superagent` team** -- `await build_superagent("coordinator.superagent")` (also `from promptise import build_superagent`) builds the agent and every agent under `cross_agents:`, at any depth, each from its whole file (servers, approval, guardrails, memory, identity, its own cross-agents), in the running event loop. `shutdown()` on the result shuts down the whole team; if one agent fails to build, the ones already built are shut down and the error names the file. `SuperAgentLoader.cross_loaders` holds the resolved references at every depth, and `SuperAgentConfig.to_build_kwargs(cross_agents=...)` takes built peers.
- **Cross-agent: delegation limits and timeouts set in Python** -- `build_agent(max_delegation_depth=3, delegation_timeout=None, include_broadcast=False)`, `CrossAgent(timeout=...)`, and the same in `.superagent` files (`max_delegation_depth:`, `delegation_timeout:`, `include_broadcast:`, and `timeout:` on each `cross_agents` entry). A call deeper than `max_delegation_depth`, or back into a peer that is already working on the request, raises `DelegationError` (exported from `promptise`), which the model sees as the tool's error. `promptise.cross_agent.get_delegation_chain()` returns the peers working on the current request. `CrossAgent` is exported from `promptise`.
- **`.superagent` files: `bearer_token`, `api_key` and `audience` on HTTP servers, `webhook_secret` on approval** -- the credentials are sent as `Authorization: Bearer <token>` and `x-api-key: <key>` (both support `${ENV_VAR}`); `webhook_secret` is the HMAC secret the webhook handler signs `X-Promptise-Signature` with.
- **CLI: `promptise validate --allow-missing-env`** -- report missing environment variables as a warning instead of failing.
- **Runtime triggers: retries and a dead-letter list** -- new `ProcessConfig.trigger_delivery` (`TriggerDeliveryConfig`, exported from `promptise.runtime`): `max_retries` (default 0, since a retry re-runs tools with side effects), exponential `retry_backoff` / `retry_backoff_max`, and `dead_letter_size`. Undeliverable events are listed in `AgentProcess.dead_letters` with reason, error and attempt count; `redeliver_dead_letters()` and `clear_dead_letters()` manage them, and `status()` reports `dead_letter_count`, `pending_retries` and `filtered_count`. An event that exhausts its retries counts as one consecutive failure.
- **Runtime triggers: cron time zones** -- `TriggerConfig.cron_timezone` / `CronTrigger(timezone=...)` takes an IANA name and follows daylight-saving time. Schedules without one run in UTC (now documented); the payload carries `timezone`.
- **Runtime: `ContextConfig.conversation_history=False`** -- turns short-term memory off, so every run sees only its trigger event.
- **Runtime triggers: `watch_debounce_seconds`** for file-watch triggers (default 0.5 s), and `filter_expression` accepts a callable that takes the `TriggerEvent`.

- **Runtime: `BudgetExceededError` and per-call budget checks** -- `promptise.runtime.BudgetExceededError` is the error an agent receives as the tool result when a call is refused by its budget (see Fixed). `BudgetState.record_tool_call(name, enforce=True)` checks a call before counting it and returns a violation with `blocked=True` instead of counting it; `record_tool_call_sync()` is the thread-safe form.
- **Runtime: `ProcessConfig.restart_backoff`** -- seconds before the first automatic restart (default `1.0`); doubles per consecutive attempt, capped at 60 s. `AgentProcess.status()` now reports `restart_count` and `journal_enabled`, and restarts emit a `process.restarted` event.
- **Runtime: `RuntimeCoordinator(auth_token=...)` and `register_node(..., auth_token=...)`** -- the bearer token sent to nodes, per cluster or per node. Never included in `NodeInfo.to_dict()` or `cluster_status()`.
- **Runtime: `RuntimeTransport(port=0)` reports its port** -- with `port=0` the OS picks a free port and `transport.port` returns it after `start()`.
- **Runtime: `ProcessLifecycle.add_listener()` and `HealthMonitor.begin_invocation()`** -- a callback (sync or async) after every state transition; a listener that raises is logged and never undoes the transition. `begin_invocation()` starts a fresh tool history for stuck/loop detection (the process calls it before each invocation).
- **MCP client: resources and prompts** -- `MCPClient` gains `list_resources()`, `list_resource_templates()`, `read_resource(uri)`, `list_prompts()` and `get_prompt(name, arguments)` (it had only `list_tools` and `call_tool`). They reconnect after a lost session like `call_tool`. The list methods fetch every page; `get_prompt` sends non-string arguments as JSON. `MCPMultiClient` gains the same, routing `read_resource` by URI (static resources, then the servers' URI templates) and `get_prompt` by name, discovering on first use, with `server=` to choose a server, `bearer_token=` to read as a caller over that caller's session (like `call_tool`), and `resource_to_server`, `template_to_server` and `prompt_to_server` views.
- **`build_agent(expose_resources=True, expose_prompts=True)`** -- gives the agent `list_resources` / `read_resource` and `get_prompt` tools whose descriptions list what the connected MCP servers publish. They fire the agent's tool callbacks and, like MCP tool calls, send the invoking caller's bearer token to servers with `forward_caller_token` (the default), so a role-guarded resource or prompt is checked against the user, not the agent.
- **MCP server SDK: catch-all template placeholders** -- `{path*}` (or `{+path}`) matches the rest of the URI, slashes included (`docs://pages/{path*}` serves `docs://pages/guides/setup`); `{name}` still matches one path segment. Templates without a catch-all are tried first, and parameters are percent-decoded.
- **MCP server SDK: `list_changed` notifications** -- the server advertises `listChanged` for tools, resources and prompts, and a registration made while serving sends `notifications/{tools,resources,prompts}/list_changed` to connected clients. `server.notify_tools_changed()`, `notify_resources_changed()` and `notify_prompts_changed()` send them on demand.
- **`@prompt(description=...)`** and `Prompt.description` -- the description `MCPServer.include_prompts()` shows to MCP clients (a YAML prompt's `description` is used too).
- **`TestClient.read_resource_contents(uri)`** returns the MCP contents with their `mimeType`; `read_resource`, `read_resource_contents` and `get_prompt` take `headers=`.
- **`AutoApprovalClassifier(ask_rules=[...])`: "always ask a human"** -- a rule type that sends a matching call straight to the fallback handler, even when an allow rule, the read-only check or the LLM classifier would have approved it. Ask rules run after deny rules and before allow rules. Counted in `stats.ask_rule_hits`; the decision's `trace.layer` is `"ask_rule"`.
- **`ApprovalPolicy(on_decision=...)`: one audit hook for every decision** -- called with `(request, decision)` before the tool runs, for the handler's decisions, the classifier's, and the ones the gate makes without asking anyone (`max_pending`, the repeated-denial limit, a timeout, a handler error), and for server-side gates answered through MCP elicitation (`metadata["source"] == "mcp_elicitation"`). Sync or async; errors are logged and ignored. `ApprovalDecision` gained `decided_by` (`"reviewer"`, `"classifier"` or `"gate"`) and `trace` (the `ClassifierDecisionTrace`), and the `approval.granted` / `approval.denied` events carry `decided_by` and `classifier_layer`.
- **MCP tool annotations reach the approval gate** -- Promptise's MCP client now keeps each tool's annotations (`readOnlyHint`, `destructiveHint`, `idempotentHint`, `openWorldHint`, `title`) on the LangChain tool's `metadata`, as `langchain-mcp-adapters` does, and `ApprovalRequest.tool_annotations` carries them to the handler (and `to_dict()`, so webhook payloads). The server-side `ApprovalGateMiddleware` fills it from the tool's `@server.tool(read_only_hint=..., destructive_hint=...)`. The classifier's read-only layer uses them (see Changed).
- **MCP server SDK: `@server.tool(cache=False)`** -- keeps a server-wide `CacheMiddleware` from caching a tool (writes, things that must always be fresh). Tools annotated `destructive_hint=True` are never cached. Also on `MCPRouter.tool()`.
- **MCP server SDK: request coalescing in `@cached`** -- identical calls that arrive while the first is still computing share its result (or its error) instead of each calling upstream, so a burst against a cold or expired entry costs one upstream call (5 concurrent cold calls: 5 upstream calls before, 1 now). Keyed on the full, caller-scoped cache key, so callers the cache keeps apart are never coalesced. Per process. `@cached(coalesce=False)` turns it off.
- **MCP server SDK: `ConcurrencyLimitError` (`CONCURRENCY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`)** -- `max_concurrent` and `ConcurrencyLimiter` refusals had the same code as rate limits (`RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`), so an agent could not tell "busy, retry in a moment" from "over quota". **Behaviour change:** they now use their own code. `ConcurrencyLimitError` subclasses `RateLimitError`, so `except RateLimitError` still catches it.
- **`build_agent(context_compaction=...)`** -- turn compaction off (`False`: the model always sees the full transcript), change the threshold (an `int`: tool results before compacting), or pass a `ContextCompaction` (`promptise.engine`) with `after_tool_results`, `keep_result_chars`, `max_tokens` (also compact past this many tokens), `history_messages` and `history_chars`. `PromptNode(compaction=...)` sets it for one node and wins over the agent's setting.
- **`ConversationFlow(token_budget=...)`** -- flows ignored token budgets. A flow now takes `token_budget` (constructor argument or class attribute) and drops blocks lowest-priority first when over budget, like `PromptAssembler`. Also new: the `ConversationFlow.current_phase` property and `promptise.prompts.flows.FlowSessions`, the per-conversation registry the agent uses.
- **MCP server SDK: `scope` for `@cached` and `CacheMiddleware`** -- `"client"` (default: same issuer, tenant and client id), `"tenant"` (every client of a tenant) or `"shared"` (everyone; an explicit opt-out for caller-independent data). Exported as `CacheScope`.
- **MCP server SDK: `JWTAuth(audience=..., issuer=...)`** -- the multi-tenancy docs configured `JWTAuth(secret=..., audience=...)`, which raised `TypeError`: `JWTAuth` checked neither `aud` nor `iss`, so a token minted for any service sharing the secret was accepted. When set, `audience` (a string or a list) must appear in the token's `aud` (a string or a list) and `issuer` must equal `iss`; tokens without the claim are rejected. `create_token()` stamps the configured audience and issuer unless the payload sets them. `AsymmetricJWTAuth` takes the same two arguments (without `audience` it keeps PyJWT's behaviour of rejecting tokens that carry `aud`).
- **MCP server SDK: `MCPServer(hide_unauthorized_tools=True)`** -- `tools/list` returned every tool to every client, so a tool guarded with `HasTenant("acme")` advertised its name, description and schema to every tenant. With the flag, `tools/list` and the `docs://manifest` resource are filtered per request: the request is authenticated with the server's (or the tool's router's) `AuthMiddleware` and each tool's guards are evaluated against that identity. Fails closed: unverifiable credentials hide every tool that needs authentication, and a guard that raises hides its tool. Calls are still guarded as before. Off by default. `AuthMiddleware.authenticate(ctx)` is now public, resource handlers receive the request's headers in `ctx.meta` (per message, as tool calls do), and `TestClient.list_tools()` applies the same filter and takes `headers=`. It filters `resources/list`, `resources/templates/list` and `prompts/list` (and their manifest sections) the same way.
- **Example: `examples/mcp/multi_tenant_agent.py`** -- one agent serving two tenants concurrently over a real HTTP server, audience-checked tokens, caller-scoped caching, and per-tenant tool lists; runs offline.
- **Guardrails: tool-result scanning (indirect prompt injection)** -- `PromptiseSecurityScanner(scan_tool_results=True)` makes `build_agent` wrap every tool so its result passes `check_tool_result()` before the model reads it: all heads run (the injection model included), secrets and PII are redacted, and a blocked result is replaced with a `[Tool result withheld by guardrails: ...]` notice. Off by default. Also available as `promptise.guardrails.wrap_tools_with_guardrails()`.
- **`Action` and `Severity` are exported from `promptise`**, as the guardrails docs already imported them.
- **`build_agent(code_action=...)`** -- `exec_timeout`, `max_repairs` and `max_tool_calls` of the code-action pattern are now settable from `build_agent` as a dict or a `promptise.engine.CodeActionConfig` (unknown keys raise; using it with another pattern raises).
- **`trace_tools` covers every tool** -- cross-agent tools, sandbox tools and `extra_tools` now report to `trace_tools` and the observability collector like MCP tools do.
- **Progress notifications reach Promptise clients and agents** -- a server's `ProgressReporter.report()` calls reached the official MCP client but never a Promptise one: `MCPClient.call_tool()` passed no progress callback, so it requested no progress. `MCPClient.call_tool()` and `MCPMultiClient.call_tool()` take `progress_callback=` (`async (progress, total, message)`), `MCPToolAdapter` takes `on_progress=` (`(tool_name, progress, total, message)`, sync or async; a failing callback is logged and never fails the call), and `build_agent()` takes `on_tool_progress=`. With `build_agent(events=...)` each notification is also emitted as a `tool.progress` event, and `trace_tools=True` prints it. A progress token is sent only when one of these is set. Documented under [Receiving progress](../mcp/server/resilience-patterns.md#receiving-progress-in-a-promptise-client-or-agent).
- **MCPQueue: `queue_submit` lists the job types and their arguments** -- its description now names every registered job type with its docstring summary and the JSON Schema of its arguments (injected `ProgressReporter` / `CancellationToken` parameters left out), and `job_type` is an enum of the registered names, so an agent no longer has to guess. `priority` (and `queue_list`'s `status`) are enums too; an invalid value is a `VALIDATION_ERROR` instead of an `INTERNAL_ERROR`.
- **`MCPServer(cancel_grace_period=5.0)` and `MCPQueue(cancel_grace_period=0.0)`** -- how long a cancelled handler that takes a `CancellationToken` gets to stop on its own before its task is cancelled (see Fixed).
- **`examples/mcp/queue_server.py`** -- a runnable queue example: advertised job types, argument validation, progress polling, ownership and cancellation. The queue docs pointed to this file, which did not exist.
- **Adaptive strategy: lesson management** -- `agent.adaptive_strategy` exposes the manager, with `list_lessons()` (returning `AdaptiveLesson`), `pending_lessons()`, `approve_lesson()`, `forget_lesson()`, `reset()` and `synthesize()`, each scoped to the current or a given caller.
- **MCP server SDK: HTTP health routes** -- the HTTP and SSE transports answer `GET /health` (liveness, `200`) and `GET /health/ready` (readiness from the `HealthCheck` registered with `register_resources()`: `200` when every required check passes, `503` otherwise; `200` with no checks when none is registered). A plain `GET /mcp` is answered `406`, so the documented Docker `curl -f http://localhost:8080/mcp` healthcheck and the Kubernetes `httpGet: /mcp` probe always failed. Both routes skip the transport auth gate and `Host` validation (probes carry no credentials and send the pod IP as `Host`), are `no-store`, and never include a failing check's exception text — the `health://readiness` MCP resource keeps it. New: `HealthCheck.liveness_report()` and `readiness_report(include_errors=)`. Example: `examples/mcp/http_server_restart.py`.
- **MCP server SDK: `MCPServer.asgi_app()`** -- the server as an ASGI application for uvicorn, gunicorn (`-k uvicorn.workers.UvicornWorker`) or hypercorn, with the same routes, auth gate, token endpoint, CORS and health probes as `run()`. `Host` is validated against the loopback names plus `allowed_hosts`. `stateless=True` (Streamable HTTP) serves every request without a session so any worker or replica can answer it, at the cost of elicitation, sampling and per-session state.
- **MCP client: `MCPClient(auto_reconnect=True)` and `session_generation`** -- see the session-loss fix below; `auto_reconnect=False` keeps the old behaviour, and `session_generation` counts the sessions a client has opened.
- **Observability: control over what text is recorded** -- `ObservabilityConfig(record_tool_io=False)` (and `PromptiseCallbackHandler(record_tool_io=...)`) keeps tool arguments, result previews and tool error messages out of events, leaving the tool name, latency, error type and lengths; on by default. See [What text is recorded](https://docs.promptise.com/core/observability/#what-text-is-recorded).
- **Observability: `AgentRun` and `get_current_run()`** (`promptise.observability`) expose the invocation being observed — its `agent.input` entry id and running token and call counts.
- **`SemanticCache(cache_tool_turns=..., write_tools=[...], read_only_tools=[...])`** -- control which tool turns may be cached and which tools count as writes (`*` wildcards; `write_tools` wins). `cache.is_write_tool(name, annotations)` shows the verdict. The cache reads `readOnlyHint` from the annotations `MCPToolAdapter` puts on `tool.metadata`.
- **`FallbackChain.bind_tools()` and streaming** -- see Fixed. `promptise.fallback.is_request_error(exc)` tells a rejected request from a provider failure.
- **MCP server SDK: `verify_audit_log()` and `promptise audit verify`** -- check an `AuditMiddleware` log file. `verify_audit_log(path, key)` returns an `AuditVerification`: `ok`, the entry count, and each problem as an `AuditIssue` with its line and kind (`modified`, `inserted`, `duplicate`, `reordered`, `deleted` with the number of missing entries, `missing_start`, `malformed`, `unsigned`, `wrong_key`). A last line cut off by a crash is the warning `truncated`, not tampering, and `continuous` and `restarts` say whether the chain continued across restarts. Several files verify as one chain (a log rotated while the server ran), several keys verify a file that was kept through a key rotation, and `anchor=` (a `last_hash` saved earlier) catches entries cut from the end, which a hash chain can't show on its own. `promptise audit verify FILE... [--key-env VAR] [--anchor HASH] [--strict] [--json]` reads the key from an environment variable (default `PROMPTISE_AUDIT_SECRET`), never prints it, and exits `1` on tampering, `2` when the file or key can't be read. `AuditMiddleware(max_memory_entries=10_000)` bounds the in-memory buffer. Documented under [Verifying a log file](../mcp/server/observability.md#verifying-a-log-file).

### Changed
- **Events: webhook signatures cover the raw body and a timestamp (breaking for receivers)** -- `WebhookSink` signed `json.dumps(payload, sort_keys=True)` while sending a differently serialised body, so verifying the raw request body (what most web frameworks hand you, and what signature guides tell you to do) failed, and with no timestamp a captured request could be replayed forever. Every request now carries `X-Promptise-Signature: t=<unix seconds>,v1=<hex>`, the HMAC-SHA256 of `<t>.` followed by the exact body bytes (Stripe-style), plus `X-Promptise-Timestamp` and an `X-Promptise-Delivery` id that stays the same across retries. Retries are re-signed, so a late retry is not rejected as stale. The new `verify_event_signature(body, signature, secret, tolerance=300)` checks it in constant time, rejects signatures older than five minutes and accepts a list of secrets while rotating. A sink's generated secret is readable as `sink.secret`. **Receivers that compare the header to a bare hex HMAC of the sorted-key JSON must switch to `verify_event_signature()`.** `AgentEvent.compute_hmac()` is unchanged; it does not produce the webhook signature.
- **Cross-agent: `broadcast_to_agents` is opt-in in `build_agent()`** -- an agent with `cross_agents` now gets only its `ask_agent_<name>` tools; pass `include_broadcast=True` (or `include_broadcast: true` in a `.superagent` file) for the fan-out tool. `make_cross_agent_tools()` still includes it by default. The broadcast tool now takes `context` like the ask tools.
- **Cross-agent: the model can no longer choose a timeout** -- `timeout_s` is gone from the `ask_agent_<name>` and `broadcast_to_agents` arguments; timeouts come from `CrossAgent.timeout` or `delegation_timeout`.
- **`.superagent` files: `auth:` on an HTTP server is a validation error** -- it was accepted but never sent, so the server answered `401`; the error says to use `bearer_token:` or `api_key:`. `HTTPServerSpec(auth=...)` emits a `FutureWarning` that it is ignored, and `promptise run/list-tools --http "... auth=..."` is rejected (use `bearer_token=` or `api_key=`).
- **`.superagent` files: approval settings that cannot be built are rejected at load** -- `handler: callback` (it needs a Python function), `handler: webhook` without `webhook_url`, and `webhook_secret` with another handler are validation errors, also in `promptise validate`.
- **`.superagent` files: stdio servers start in the file's folder** -- a relative `cwd:` and a relative `command:` with a path separator (`./bin/server`) are resolved against the file's folder, so `promptise agent team/agent.superagent` works from any directory. Bare commands (`python`, `npx`) still come from `PATH`.
- **`.superagent` files: the loader reads `.env`** -- like the CLI, `load_superagent_file()`, `resolve_env_vars()` and `validate_env_vars()` load the nearest `.env` first (never overriding a set variable; `PROMPTISE_NO_DOTENV=1` turns it off).
- **CLI: `promptise validate` fails on missing environment variables** -- it exits 1 (pass `--allow-missing-env` to only warn, or `--no-check-env`), and it checks the references and environment variables of every cross-agent file at every depth, not only the first level.
- **`SuperAgentConfig.to_build_kwargs()` warns when it drops `cross_agents`** -- it cannot build them (each is an agent of its own); the `UserWarning` points to `build_superagent()`.
- **Runtime: `OrchestrationAPI` rejects an empty `auth_token`** and treats every loopback address (`127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `localhost`) as loopback; with a token set, requests to unknown paths get `401` instead of `404`.
- **Runtime: `ProcessConfig`, `TriggerConfig` and `TriggerDeliveryConfig` reject unknown keys** -- a misspelt option (`hmac_secrets=...`, `guardrail=...`) raised nothing and was silently ignored; it is now a `ValidationError`.
- **Runtime triggers: the webhook answers `503` with `Retry-After` while the process can't take events** -- see Fixed. `GET /health` returns `503` in that case too, and a `500` no longer echoes the exception text.
- **Runtime: `conversation_max_messages=0` is documented as unlimited** -- the field description said "0 = disabled", but the buffer always kept everything (consistent with `build_agent(conversation_max_messages=0)`). Use `conversation_history=False` to turn it off.

- **Runtime: `ProcessConfig.journal` is off by default** -- the default is now `JournalConfig(level="none")`. Journaling used to do nothing at all (see Fixed); now that it writes, a default of `"checkpoint"` would make every upgraded process start writing `.promptise/journal`. Pass `journal=JournalConfig(...)` (or a `journal:` section in a manifest) to turn it on; `JournalConfig()` itself still defaults to `level="checkpoint"`.
- **Runtime: mission evidence lists the current invocation's tool calls** -- `MissionEvidence.tool_calls` comes from the health monitor's tool history, which now starts fresh at each invocation.
- **AutoApprovalClassifier: deny rules are checked before allow rules** -- allow rules came first, so a broad allow rule won: `allow_rules=[ApprovalRule(user="admin@shop.example")]` auto-approved `delete_customer` even with `deny_rules=[ApprovalRule(tool="delete_*")]`. The order is now deny rules → ask rules → allow rules → read-only → LLM classifier → fallback, so a deny rule always wins. If an allow rule carved an exception out of a broader deny rule, narrow the deny rule instead.
- **AutoApprovalClassifier: `argument_contains` matches the JSON of the arguments** -- the docstring said "JSON-serialized arguments" but the code searched `str(arguments)`, so `"'force': True"` matched and `'"force": true'` didn't. It now searches `json.dumps(arguments, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False)`, as documented. Rules written against the Python repr (single quotes, `True`/`False`/`None`) need updating. Matching stays case-sensitive.
- **AutoApprovalClassifier: the read-only layer no longer goes by name prefix alone** -- `fetch_and_purge_cache` and `show_and_delete` were auto-approved because they start with `fetch_` and `show_`. A tool is now read-only only when no word of its name is a destructive verb (`promptise.DEFAULT_DESTRUCTIVE_VERBS`: `delete`, `purge`, `drop`, `reset`, `update`, `send`, ...; words split on `_`, `-` and camelCase), its annotations don't say `readOnlyHint=False` or `destructiveHint=True`, and it is either annotated `readOnlyHint=True` or (without one) named with a read-only prefix. New options: `destructive_verbs=` and `use_tool_annotations=`; `classifier.is_read_only(request)` shows the verdict.
- **AutoApprovalClassifier: `last_trace` is the most recently *finished* decision** -- read `decision.trace` instead (see Fixed). `stats` updates no longer go through an `asyncio.Lock`.
- **MCP server SDK: exception handlers for `MCPError` subclasses now apply** -- a handler registered for a specific `MCPError` subclass (`CircuitOpenError`, `RateLimitError`, `ToolError`, ...) is now consulted before the error is serialised; previously handlers only ran for non-`MCPError` exceptions. A catch-all `Exception` (or `MCPError`) handler still does not swallow structured errors.
- **Docs: Caching & Performance and Resilience Patterns** -- request coalescing, the `cache=False` opt-out, LRU eviction, sub-second Redis TTLs, `CONCURRENCY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and limiter placement, per-tool timeouts without middleware, what the circuit breaker counts as a failure, the single half-open probe, and the default `CIRCUIT_OPEN` response.
- **Docs: multi-tenant guidance corrected** -- the Multi-Tenancy, Multi-User Identity, Secure Multi-Tenant Platform, Multi-User Systems, Server Specs, Building Agents, Caching & Performance, Auth & Security and MCP Client pages (and the multi-user-identity and caching blog posts) describe per-caller token forwarding, the `forward_caller_token` opt-out, cache scopes, `JWTAuth` audience/issuer and `hide_unauthorized_tools`. The Secure Multi-Tenant Platform guide no longer builds a shared agent with one tenant's token; the Multi-User Systems guide's `AsymmetricJWTAuth` example uses the real parameter names.
- **Guardrails fail closed by default** -- a scanner whose ML or service-backed head cannot run now blocks every scan instead of passing it unchecked (see Security). `PromptiseSecurityScanner.default()` and `guardrails=True` need `transformers` and `torch` (`pip install "promptise[all]"`); pass `fail_open=True` to keep the old behaviour. GLiNER (`NERDetector`) stays a separate `pip install gliner`, now stated in the docs.
- **Guardrails docs corrected** -- model sizes (injection model ~750 MB, GLiNER ~500 MB), the `ScanReport` example (PII on input is a passing warning, not a block), and references to `scan_input` / `scan_output`, which do not exist (`scan_text`, `check_input`, `check_output`, `check_tool_result`).
- **Sandbox: fails closed instead of degrading** -- `build_agent(sandbox=...)` without code-action used to print a warning and build the agent *without* sandbox tools when the sandbox could not start (no `docker` package, Docker not running, gVisor missing); it now raises `RuntimeError` naming the cause, like code-action already did. Leave `sandbox` unset (or `None`/`False`) to run without one. Unknown sandbox keys raise a `ValidationError` with a hint instead of being ignored (`network_mode` → use `network`, `tools` → pick an `image`, typos get a "did you mean"), in `build_agent`, `SandboxManager`, `SandboxConfig` and `.superagent` files. `backend="gvisor"` without `runsc` now reports that the runtime is not registered (listing the available ones) instead of "ensure Docker is installed and running".
- **Sandbox: the configuration matches what is enforced** -- new `pids_limit` (default 256) is applied to the container. `disk_limit` is now applied as the size of the writable workspace tmpfs (default `"1G"`, was an unused `"10G"`), and `/tmp` and `/var/tmp` never exceed it. The `tools` option, and the internal `security_opt` and `cap_drop` fields, were never applied and are removed (passing them now raises). The unused `DEFAULT_APPARMOR_PROFILE` and the check for it are gone, and the docs no longer describe a seccomp whitelist or a custom AppArmor profile: the sandbox uses Docker's default seccomp profile and, on AppArmor hosts, Docker's default AppArmor profile, plus dropped capabilities, `no-new-privileges` and a read-only root filesystem. `HOME` defaults to the workspace for the container and every exec.
- **Packaging: `promptise[sandbox]` extra** -- the Docker client (`docker>=7.0.0`) is now installable on its own with `pip install "promptise[sandbox]"` (still included in `promptise[all]`); the error for a missing client says so.
- **Docs: `observe=True` writes an HTML report** -- the `build_agent` docstring and [Adaptive Strategy](../core/adaptive-strategy.md#observability-is-separate) now say that `observe=True` alone writes a report file to `./reports` at shutdown, and that adaptive strategy no longer needs observability.
- **Reasoning Graph: structured outputs are dicts** -- with a Pydantic `output_schema`, `result.output` and `state.context[output_key]` now hold `model.model_dump()` rather than the model instance. Code that read attributes (`state.context["verdict"].passes`) reads keys (`["passes"]`), or re-validates with `Verdict.model_validate(...)`.
- **Reasoning Graph: `PromptNode` default `max_iterations` (10) now applies** -- a custom `PromptNode` in a long tool loop stops after 10 model calls instead of the engine's 25. Raise it per node (`max_iterations=25`) where a deep tool loop is expected; the prebuilt patterns set their own budgets.
- **`ObservabilityConfig.record_prompts` defaults to `None`** -- "follow the level": prompt and response text is recorded at `FULL` and not below. `True` still records it at any level; `False` now also keeps it out at `FULL`. The `.superagent` `observability.record_prompts` field follows the same rule, and an `observability:` section in a `.superagent` file is now applied (`build_agent()` used to replace a dict `observe=` with the defaults).
- **`Mem0Provider.search()` raises on errors** instead of logging and returning `[]`, so an incompatible release or an unreachable store is visible; the agent still catches it and answers without memory context.
- **`PromptiseAgent.shutdown()` closes transporters after flushing them** (closes the NDJSON stream and log file handles, ends and exports open spans).
- **Top-level chain errors are recorded as `agent.error`** (not `tool.error`), and a sub-chain error is no longer counted twice.
- **Semantic cache: fewer requests are cached by default** -- follow-ups (requests with earlier turns) and turns that called tools are no longer cached unless you pass `cache_multi_turn=True` / `cache_tool_turns=True`; `scope="per_session"` needs a `user_id`; `build_agent(cache=...)` fails when the cache's packages are missing. See Security and Fixed for why.

### Fixed
- **Events: an agent's events disagreed on `agent_id`** -- `invocation.*` events carried the model name even when `observer_agent_id` was set, and every other agent event (`tool.*`, `guardrail.*`, `approval.*`, `cache.*`) carried none. All events an agent emits now share one `agent_id`: `observer_agent_id`, else the identity's `agent_id` (or IdP subject); agents run by an `AgentProcess` use the process name; otherwise the model name, as before. The model is also in `metadata["model"]`.
- **Events: `tool.error` never fired for failing MCP tools** -- an MCP tool that fails returns a normal result with an error in it (a Promptise server's `ToolError` arrives as `{"error": {"code", "message", "retryable"}}`), so no exception was seen and no event was emitted; `tool.error` only fired for raising `extra_tools`, only with `observe=True`, and with `tool_name: "unknown"`. Error results (`isError`, the `ToolError` envelope, a `ToolMessage` with `status="error"`) and raised exceptions now emit `tool.error` with the real tool name, `error`, `error_type`, `duration_ms`, and `code`/`retryable` when the tool reports them. A domain answer such as `{"error": "No invoice X"}` (no `code`) is not treated as a failure.
- **Events: `tool.error` and `tool.slow` required `observe=True`** -- both were emitted by the observability callback handler, which only exists with observability on (undocumented). They now come from a handler attached to every invocation of an agent built with `events=`, for `ainvoke()`, `chat()` and the streaming methods, without duplicates when observability is on. The `tool.slow` threshold is configurable: `EventNotifier(slow_tool_threshold=5.0)` (seconds; `None` turns `tool.slow` off); the event now includes `threshold_ms`.
- **Events: a process's last events could still be lost** -- a startup failure queued `process.failed` before anything had started the notifier, and stopping a process that had failed never drained it, so that `process.failed` was lost when the program exited. The process now starts the notifier before startup can fail, and `stop()` drains a notifier the process owns whether or not it failed; a notifier shared through an `AgentRuntime` is still drained by `stop_all()`.
- **Events: one slow sink delayed every other sink, and shutdown dropped events silently** -- the notifier delivered each event to its sinks one after another, so a webhook retrying against a receiver that was down (about 7 s of backoff with the defaults) held up every later sink and event, and `stop()` cancelled whatever was left after 5 s without a word (with the receiver down, the fourth attempt and the `CallbackSink` never ran). Each sink now has its own queue and delivery task, so other sinks get every event at once and order is kept per sink. `stop()` waits up to `EventNotifier(shutdown_timeout=10.0)` (or `stop(timeout=...)`), then logs every dropped event with its sink and type and counts it in `notifier.dropped_count`; a full queue now drops for that sink only. New: `notifier.flush(timeout=)`, `notifier.is_running`, and sinks with a `close()` are closed on stop. `emit_sync()` is safe from other threads and starts the notifier inside a running loop; a notifier left running by an earlier `asyncio.run()` recovers in the next.
- **Events: `WebhookSink` refused every local or private URL with advice that did not exist** -- the error suggested a "base_url override" that `WebhookSink` does not have. Pass `allow_private_networks=True` to deliver to `localhost` or a private network (also `allow_private_networks: true` per sink in `.superagent` files); the error now says so. URLs that are not `http(s)` with a host are rejected either way.
- **Events: thin payloads and a redaction gap** -- `session_id` and `metadata` were always empty, `guardrail.blocked` did not say why, and `approval.requested` did not include the arguments under review. `session_id` is now the `chat()` session (else `caller.metadata["session_id"]`); `metadata` carries `model` and an `invocation_id` shared by every event of one run, plus `process_name`, `process_id` and `trigger_type` in the runtime; `guardrail.blocked` has `reason` and `findings` (detector, category, severity, description — never the matched text); `approval.requested` has `arguments` as the policy redacts them. `WebhookSink` redaction covered `data` only, so a `user_id` that was an email address was sent in clear; it now covers the whole payload (`redact_sensitive=False` turns it off). `emit_event()` fills `agent_id`, `session_id` and `metadata` from the running invocation and takes a `user_id`.
- **Agents with an identity stopped working once the credential expired** -- `build_agent(identity=...)` fetched the identity's credential once and sent it on every request for the agent's lifetime. After it expired the server answered `401`, the MCP SDK tore the transport down, and the tool call waiting for its response never returned. The credential is now requested for each server's `audience` on every request, renewed before it expires (the session is reopened with it), refreshed once on `401`, and a call the server still rejects fails at once with `MCPConnectionRejectedError` (`mid_session=True`) instead of hanging.
- **MCP client: a call hung when the server rejected an expired static `bearer_token`** -- the call now fails at once with `MCPConnectionRejectedError(mid_session=True)`, whose message suggests `bearer_token_provider`; a session that ends while a call is in flight fails that call instead of leaving it waiting. `MCPMultiClient` names the server in the error and keeps routing the server's tools.
- **Identity: a credential living less than twice the refresh buffer (two minutes) was re-acquired on every request** -- the buffer is now capped at half the credential's lifetime. Projected-token files and OIDC tokens, whose audience is fixed, are cached once instead of once per requested audience.
- **Identity: `AgentIdentity.auto()` ignored `$PROMPTISE_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE` on AWS** -- the file that selects the projected-token mode of `from_aws` was not an AWS marker, so a pod with only that variable failed detection. It is now detected (projected mode); IRSA's `$AWS_WEB_IDENTITY_TOKEN_FILE` still selects STS mode, and its `sts.amazonaws.com` token is never presented.
- **`JwksAuth`: an IdP outage failed every request once the cached keys aged out** -- the cached keys now keep verifying while the JWKS endpoint is unreachable (retried every 30 seconds), and a token signed with a newly rotated key is accepted on first use.

- **Cross-agent: runaway delegation** -- nothing bounded delegation at run time, so an agent that kept delegating (to itself through a peer, or around a cycle) made well over a thousand model calls before stopping. Delegation is now limited by `max_delegation_depth` and loop detection (see Added).
- **CLI: `promptise agent` teams with stdio servers failed with "Not connected"** -- cross-agents were built in one `asyncio.run` and used in another, so their stdio MCP sessions were dead; cross-agents of cross-agents were ignored, and a cross-agent got only its file's servers, model, instructions and trace setting. The command now builds the whole team with `build_superagent()` in the chat's event loop and shuts it down there.
- **CLI: `promptise agent` with `approval.handler: queue` could not be answered** -- nobody read the queue, so every gated call waited for the timeout. At a terminal each request is now asked as a y/N question; with piped input the CLI says that requests will time out. A stdio server that fails to start is reported in one line instead of a traceback.
- **Cross-agent: `trace_tools=True` and observability did not show delegation** -- `ask_agent_<name>` and `broadcast_to_agents` calls now print and record like any other tool call.
- **Runtime triggers: `filter_expression` was never evaluated** -- events that should have been filtered out still ran the agent (and cost an LLM call). It is now applied to every trigger type. The string form is a small expression language that is parsed with `ast` and run by a whitelist interpreter, never `eval`: lookups (`payload['issue']['number']`, `payload.issue.number`, bare payload keys), comparisons, `and`/`or`/`not`, literals, and a few functions and string methods. Anything else is rejected when the `TriggerConfig` is created. The webhook answers `200 {"status": "ignored"}` for events that don't match.
- **Runtime triggers: file watch ignored `watch_events`, and one write ran the agent twice** -- the factory never passed `watch_events` (or a debounce) to `FileWatchTrigger`, so a trigger for `["deleted"]` fired on new and changed files. Debouncing was keyed by path *and* event type, so the `created` + `modified` pair that one write produces ran the agent twice. Events for the same path are now merged within the debounce window into one event, whose `event_type` is the net change (`created`, `modified`, `deleted` or `moved`) and whose `event_types` lists the raw events. `watch_events` is checked against that merged type, and unknown event names are rejected.
- **Runtime triggers: a failed process kept answering `202` while events piled up** -- after `max_consecutive_failures` the process went to `FAILED` but its webhook kept accepting events into a queue that nothing consumed. The webhook now answers `503` with `Retry-After` while the process is failed, stopping or its queue is full, so senders retry later. Events still queued at the failure are dead-lettered. Listener tasks wait for queue space instead of dropping cron and file events, and events that arrive while the process is still starting are no longer dropped.
- **Runtime triggers: a bad cron expression only failed at run time** -- `"every day at 9"` was accepted, the process started, and its listener logged a traceback every second forever. Cron expressions are now validated when the `TriggerConfig` or `CronTrigger` is created (5 fields, or 6 with croniter's trailing seconds field). A listener whose trigger keeps failing now backs off exponentially up to 60 s, logging the traceback only once.
- **Runtime: `ProcessConfig` silently dropped `guardrails`, `observe` and `cache`** -- `_build_agent()` looked for them, but the model didn't declare them, so they were discarded (as were `optimize_tools`, `adaptive` and `max_invocation_time`). They are now real fields and reach `build_agent()`.
- **Runtime triggers: a non-ASCII signature header crashed the webhook handler** -- `hmac.compare_digest` raised `TypeError` on it, so the sender got a `500` instead of a `401`.
- **Runtime trigger docs corrected** -- the webhook binds to `127.0.0.1` by default (not `0.0.0.0`) and has an HMAC check; `max_consecutive_failures` defaults to 3 (not 5); sub-minute cron works through the seconds field; `croniter` and `watchdog` ship with promptise; schedules are UTC unless a time zone is set.

- **Runtime: budget limits were only checked after the run** -- tool calls were counted as they happened, but `on_exceeded` ran only after `ainvoke()` returned, so an agent with `max_irreversible_per_run=1` could send ten emails in one run, and `max_tool_calls_per_run` / `max_cost_per_run` capped nothing inside a run. A budget guard now runs before every tool call (sync and async tools, parallel calls one by one): a call that would exceed a tool-call, cost or irreversible limit, or any call after `max_llm_turns_per_run` is exceeded, is refused before the tool runs and not counted, and the agent gets `BudgetExceededError` as the tool result telling it to stop and answer. `on_exceeded` still applies after the run.
- **Runtime: `restart_policy` and `max_restarts` did nothing** -- a process that reached `max_consecutive_failures` stayed `FAILED` whatever the policy. `"on_failure"` and `"always"` now tear the process down and start it again with backoff, up to `max_restarts` consecutive attempts (the count resets after a successful invocation, and a restarted process starts a fresh failure streak). `"always"` also recycles a process at `max_lifetime` instead of leaving it stopped. An explicit `stop()` cancels a pending restart; a failure of your own `start()` is raised, not retried. `process.failed` is now emitted for any `FAILED` transition, not only a failed start.
- **Runtime: `ProcessConfig.journal` was never written** -- the docs said every transition, trigger, invocation result and checkpoint is journaled, but `AgentProcess` never created a journal, so `promptise runtime logs` and `ReplayEngine` had nothing to read. A process with a journal level now records state transitions, invocation results and errors, restarts and a checkpoint (context state, lifecycle state, conversation, budget and mission state) after every invocation; `level="full"` adds trigger events, invocation starts and every tool call and result. Entries are filed under the process name, secret-access and mission entries land in the same journal, and journal failures are logged without stopping the process.
- **Runtime: replay applied stale entries over the newest checkpoint** -- `ReplayEngine.recover()` replayed everything after the *first* checkpoint entry, so with several checkpoints old `context_update` values overwrote the latest snapshot. It now replays only the entries after the last checkpoint, and works on a copy, so recovering no longer modifies the checkpoint stored in an `InMemoryJournal`.
- **Runtime: tool results counted as empty agent replies** -- the health monitor recorded every tool output as an agent response, so a tool returning `[]` three times raised an `empty_response` anomaly (and could pause or stop the process). Empty-response detection now looks at the agent's final reply only.
- **Runtime: scheduled agents were reported as stuck** -- tool history carried over between invocations, so an agent that checks the same queue once per cron tick raised a `stuck` anomaly on its third run. Stuck and loop detection now look within one invocation.
- **Runtime: `health.recovered` fired after every success** -- once any anomaly had occurred, every later successful invocation emitted `health.recovered`. It is now emitted once, on the first success after an anomaly.
- **Runtime: the conversation buffer stored the wrong reply** -- the process kept the *first* assistant message of the result, which is an earlier reply echoed from the conversation history or a tool-call request, not this run's answer. It now stores the agent's final reply.
- **Runtime: `process.stopped` events were lost** -- the agent's shutdown stopped the event notifier, which the process does not own (an `AgentRuntime` shares one with every process), before `process.stopped` was emitted, and stopped delivery for the other processes too. Stopping a process leaves the notifier running; a standalone process drains it after `process.stopped`, and `AgentRuntime.stop_all()` drains it once every process has stopped.
- **Runtime: `RuntimeCoordinator` reported failed operations as successes** -- `start_process_on_node()`, `stop_process_on_node()` and `inject_event_on_node()` returned the node's error body (`{"error": "Unauthorized"}`, `404`, `500`) as if the call had worked. They now raise `RuntimeError` naming the node and status.
- **Runtime: `RuntimeCoordinator` ignored `node_timeout`** -- one failed health check marked a node unhealthy. A healthy node now stays healthy until it has failed checks for `node_timeout` seconds (the result marks the miss with `missed_check`). A failing health round no longer ends the background monitor, and a cancelled node check marks the node unhealthy instead of crashing the round.
- **MCP server SDK: resources lost their MIME type and data** -- `read_resource` returned `str(result)`: a dict arrived as a Python repr, bytes as `"b'...'"`, and the declared `mime_type` was dropped. Dicts, lists and Pydantic models are now JSON, `bytes` are sent as a base64 blob (`BlobResourceContents`), and each content carries the declared MIME type. Without `mime_type=`, it is inferred from the return annotation (`application/json` for `dict` / `list` / a model, `application/octet-stream` for `bytes`, else `text/plain`). A handler can also return `ReadResourceContents` items for several contents. An unknown URI is the MCP "resource not found" error (`-32002`).
- **MCP server SDK: prompts returning a list failed** -- a handler returning a list of `PromptMessage` failed Pydantic validation. A prompt may now return a `str`, a `PromptMessage`, a `{"role", "content"}` dict, an MCP content block, a list of any of those, or a `GetPromptResult`.
- **MCP server SDK: prompt arguments and template parameters were always strings** -- they are now converted to the handler's type hints (`"3"` → `3`, `"true"` → `True`, JSON text → a `list`), with a validation error for a value that does not fit. A template whose `{placeholder}` names no handler parameter is refused at registration.
